Concept of Development: A Non-Trivial Outlook on Evolution

Author: Vladlen V.K.

Full English translation of «Концепция развития. Нетривиальный взгляд на эволюцию»

Prometej Publishers, Moscow, 2021 · ISBN 978-5-00172-246-5 · License CC BY 4.0

# Concept of Development: The Foundational Principle

Concept of Development: The Foundational Principle Abstract This article is devoted to the exposition of a worldview concept based on the awareness of the significance and role of the relations of the widely known foundational aspects of reality: content and form. Content and form are two inalienable constituent sides of any element of reality, be it an object, a process, or a phenomenon. By virtue of the omnipresence of content and form, the relations of these components — these two principles of reality — prove to be the most important and essential. In the course of analyzing the aforementioned relations, the internal source of evolutionary movement and the "mechanism" of its functioning are revealed, on the basis of which the directionality of evolutionary processes is defined and formulated for the first time (i.e., an idea of the vector of change, movement, and development of the surrounding world and its elements is given).

The content-form transformations of unitary directionality described in the article, which underlie all real processes, not only explain the spiral-cyclical nature of the world but also make it possible to forecast the prospect of its development. It is precisely this mode of interaction of the fundamental aspects (principles) of reality that ensures the irreversibility of the evolution of forms, as well as the novelty, uniqueness, and inexhaustible diversity of the latter. The approach under consideration fundamentally levels any "centrism" ("absolutism") of whatever form, pointing both to the limited applicability of the currently dominant anthropocentric (as well as bio-, theo-, and sociocentric) model of the world, and to the necessity of transition to another, more integral and adequate model of it. This concept may be recommended to the attention of researchers primarily in view of its integrity (in contrast to the fragmentary pictures of the world and theories of the past and present), as well as by virtue of the following considerations: whoever possesses the most adequate worldview — the future belongs to them. Introduction A reader inclined toward the philosophical comprehension of natural-scientific and humanitarian knowledge has surely had occasion to reflect on the fact that at the basis of real processes lie, and govern the surrounding world, deep-lying regularities different from those that are discovered on the surface and perceived as self-evident. We are speaking of objective fundamental relations that condition, among other things, the functioning and structure of the economy, politics, science, culture, and other aspects of the life of human communities. On the basis of many years of tracking discoveries and achievements in various fields of the natural and humanitarian sciences, I will venture to state that to this day what takes place everywhere is chiefly the mere accumulation and mechanical "warehousing" of factual material, and there is as yet not a single contentful unifying idea that would allow us finally to realize what the observed and "warehoused" phenomena actually represent. In other words, the need for an integral and adequate model of everything that exists, develops, and evolves is felt ever more acutely. I believe hardly anyone would object to the proposition that knowledge and understanding of the aforementioned regularities would contribute to raising the accuracy of analysis and forecasting (based on this analysis) of the course of real events. The article presents a non-standard view of the principles of organization of living and non-living nature, of the origins and directionality of evolution; after acquainting himself with it, a reader interested in such questions may

develop a motivation for a critical rethinking of established worldview attitudes and a revision of the methods of analyzing the factual material and information obtained, which will ultimately be expressed in an increase in the effectiveness of the cognitive process. This is, perhaps, the most integral and adequate concept available today, encompassing the spheres of interest of numerous scientific collectives and making it possible to comprehend the principles of correct forecasting of the development of reality. Below it will be shown WHAT in fact lies at the basis of the development of reality, which is accomplished in many respects through inversion (i.e., the "turning over" of roles, methods, and notions, in Marx's figurative expressions, or "return into the foundation," in Hegel's terminology [12, p. 102]). Thus, the reader's attention is offered a "thesis" version of the concept [1; 20], which examines the system of natural-scientific views and representations concerning the evolutionary movement and development of reality from the standpoint of implicit but objective deep-lying regularities that underlie these views and representations. The essence of the proposed concept of development and its foundational principle Traditionally, one speaks of the movement and development of anything, first of all, as of change: nothing remains for any length of time equal and identical to itself. At the same time, it is impossible to speak of changes apart from connection with what already exists. Stars, planets, oceans, and mountains change; plants, animals, people, and the products of human activity change even faster and more strikingly. In other words, changes are never isolated and abstract, but are always attached to what exists — to objects, processes, phenomena, i.e., to some already existing basis. At that, it is not possible to separate the changing from the existing, i.e., change from basis, which compels us to speak of the movement and development of any object, process, or phenomenon as of a derivative of a certain symbiosis — of basis and change. In general form, it is convenient to speak of such a symbiosis in terms of the bilateral, two-component relation {content/form}, i.e., in the notions of the categorical apparatus developed sufficiently deeply before us by our outstanding predecessors-dialecticians. Of the movement and development of any object, process, or phenomenon one can speak as of a contentful change of its form. The contentful changes that take place in reality manifest themselves through changing forms. Indeed, if one scrupulously analyzes the changes and transformations occurring in our surroundings (equally as in ourselves), one can see that behind all changes and transformations there is implicitly discovered a single-in-essence process of the movement of a

certain contentful activity through a series, a chain, a sequence of changing forms. In order to avoid misunderstanding and divergent readings, let us cite the textbook interpretation of the notions "content" and "form" [13, pp. 621, 622]. Content is the determining side of the whole, the aggregate and unity of all its constituent parts, properties, internal processes, contradictions, and tendencies; form is the mode of existence and expression of content. In the interrelation of content and form, content represents the dynamic side of the whole, while form encompasses the system of its stable connections. The relation of content and form is characterized by a unity reaching the point of their transition into each other; however, this unity is relative. Content is not the substrate by itself, but its internal state, the aggregate of processes, and in this sense content itself acts as a process. The understanding of form, in turn, presupposes the consideration of it as a developing and becoming structure. Content, being the active side, represents the mobile (as a rule, internal) constituent of the relation {content/form} and symbolizes change (at times quite hidden, veiled). Form, at that, as the passive external constituent-side of the same relation, follows the active internal content. As the active constituent side of the relation, content is internally inherent in a certain impulse, stimulus, urge, motivation toward change, movement, development. This is, first of all, what distinguishes the active contentful side from the formal passive one. Then change, movement, and development themselves do not arise suddenly and do not appear as some separate, isolated, and independent element, but prove to be a derivative or consequence of the interaction of unequal, dissimilar constituent sides in their relation and striving to equalize, to identify with, and to become each other. Meanwhile, at every attempt to single out either of these two constituent sides in "pure" form, without the "admixture" of its other, we get into a fix — both sides, upon closer examination, sink their roots one into the other, bear simultaneously the features of their other, which testifies, apparently, to their deep syncretism. Extremely important in this connection is Hegel's point of view on the interrelation of content and form, who believed that "content is not formless, while form is at one and the same time both contained in content itself and represents something external to it," which reduces to the absolute relation "between form and content, namely — their transition into each other, so that content is nothing other than the transition of form into content, and form — the transition of content into form" [2, p. 298]. In this way the constituents in the relation {content/form} interact with each other, or more precisely, as it were, "live" by the striving to become each

other and to absorb each other completely and without residue, by the striving to turn into identity with each other, by the striving toward mutual transition, mutual interpenetration, mutual transformation, mutual expression... Since it is simply impossible either to single out or to separate content from form (with respect to concrete objects, processes, phenomena), the most productive, rational, and correct way is to speak only of the relation of these two mutually interpenetrating constituents-components-sides of concrete objects, processes, and phenomena. In this light, every concrete object, process, or phenomenon is the relation of its sides or constituents {content/form}, and, in the final analysis, what matters here is not each constituent side by itself, but precisely their relation; the development of any object, process, or phenomenon, in turn, is the expression or representation of changing content by means of changing forms and, ultimately, it is the process of becoming and manifestation of each such concrete form in the form of a definite correlation (relative balance) of the aforementioned constituent sides (with the possibility of expressing and representing the said correlation, including in mathematical or digital form). Against the background and in the terms of these representations, the following simple idea acquires a more or less coherent shape: everything that we see, hear, and feel, everything with which and about which we think, i.e., everything that surrounds us, including ourselves, — these are various formations or forms of different levels (different degrees) of contentfulness, into which a certain contentful activity is clothed in its movement from level to level. Such formations-forms represent dipoles (spirals) of two poles (two branches), one of which is the contentful active constituent side of the dipole (spiral), the second — its (its) passive formal constituent side; in the philosophical aspect, the aforementioned dipole (spiral) is similar to the relation of two abstract notions {content/form}. An approximate visual model of such a dipole-spiral is given in Fig. 1.

[Fig. 1 — schematic representation of a dipole-spiral: the contentful side (movement of content) and the formal side (movement of form) as two branches of one spiral] Continuing to proceed from real relations and basing ourselves on them, we follow further in our reasoning. Interacting with each other, dipole-forms of different levels generate dipole-forms of new levels. The contentful constituent, being the active side, strives to escape the control of any and every form that encompasses and contains it; however, not having the possibility to realize itself otherwise than again through some form, it is forced each time to clothe itself in the next, unrelentingly following changing form. In prospect and in the ultimate expression, this process takes the form of an endless "chase" of the contentful constituent (content) after the very same contentful constituent moving and developing in such an uncomplicated manner. [One can arrive at such a conclusion by using, for example, the device of the limiting transition, which mathematicians apply when finding the limits of functions.] Modifying and adapting the initial model (Fig. 1) in line with what has just been said, we arrive at the following conceptual form of it, which brings us close to understanding the directionality of the course and development of all real processes, i.e., the vector of development of anything whatsoever. In a more or less laconic form, the formulation of the directionality of the development of reality looks as follows: the development of content is accomplished in the direction of the exhaustive expression (encompassment, absorption, assimilation, appropriation, comprehension...) by the developing content of the very content developing in such a way. Let us denote the formulation by the symbol @ for the convenience of further handling of it. With respect to the surrounding world, this formulation means approximately the following: everything that exists and develops has as the direction of its development and existence the expression (encompassment, absorption, assimilation, appropriation, comprehension...) by itself, the existing and developing, of itself, the existing and developing. Taking into account the previously noted inseparability of the components of content and form in their actual relation {content/form}, it becomes obvious that the external manifestation of the above-described process of contentful accumulation @ proves to be the renewal of the form of expression of the accumulating content by raising the "storey-ness" or level of this very form of its (of the accumulated content) representation. As indeed happens in reality, the movement of content determined by formulation @ cannot be realized otherwise than through forms of different levels of representation of this content that do not repeat in details.

Thus, we are speaking of the description of an infinite, unceasing process of contentful movement, realized by means of successive approximations to the exhaustive expression of the very contentful movement; in this way, in conceptual-abstract language, a multilevel formation (form) is described, whose constituent parts (branches), like the formation as a whole, are similar to "self-winding" spirals. Within the conceptual (notional) formulation @, which models the development of multilevel reality, in a "sublated" (as philosophers say) form are "packed" both the activity (movement-development), and the directionality of this activity (movement-development), and the contradiction (as the source of all movement-development), and the laws of dialectics, etc., etc. In essence, a general, single, universal internal principle of selfdevelopment (self-organization) of reality has been formulated, an implicit or explicit hint (and in other cases — a firm conviction) of the existence of which is present in the representations and statements of a huge number of scientists, researchers, and philosophers of the past and present who have devoted themselves to the study and interpretation of the surrounding world and its evolutionary movement [3-7; 9-11; 14; 16; 17]. That is why all real processes have a spiral-cyclical character !!! In other words, by virtue of relations of this kind, our world and all its elements are spiral-shaped: man is a spiral, a dog is a spiral, an ant is a spiral, a living cell is a spiral, a molecule is a spiral, an atom is a spiral, an electron is a spiral, a photon is a spiral, a galaxy is a spiral, the Universe is a spiral; anyone who wishes may exercise in continuing this list as far as they like. The brain of two hemispheres, the heart, the lungs, and other internal organs and systems of man (as, incidentally, of other individuals of the animal world) likewise represent bipolar spiral structures (see Fig. 1). On the other hand, it is precisely this mode of interaction of the foundational components of the said relation {content/form} that ensures the novelty, uniqueness, and irreversibility of the development and evolution of forms, as well as the inexhaustible diversity of the latter. As already noted above, in this way the components-constituents in the relation {content/form} interact with each other, submitting to the "dictate" of the objective tendency with directionality toward becoming each other and absorbing each other completely and without residue. However, if one gives preference only to this point of view on development as the movement of the foundational principles-components of reality only in the direction of convergence and reunification (i.e., thereby dooming everything visibly existing to a supposedly obvious end and degeneration into identity), — then how can one explain the presence and the tireless, unceasing replenishment and renewal of opposites ("dissimilarities") and

their inexhaustible interaction and opposition in the surrounding world? In what way does the inexhaustible difference then arise and manifest itself under the conditions of the total striving of everything toward unification and coincidence proclaimed here? However paradoxical this may seem, it is precisely by virtue of such a naturally conditioned character of movement (striving) that renewed extremes, opposites, and their opposition can only arise and manifest themselves. The interaction of two initial extremes, of two non-coinciding principles, of two "sworn" competitors, of two unequal and non-equivalent forms generates something third, a certain new form, which, along with the content and attributes "absorbed" into itself of the interacting initial forms, simultaneously bears in itself also the difference from each of them. [For this reason, in particular, the "holistic" assertion that the whole is always greater than the sum of its parts is not without foundation.] It is precisely the striving of two unequal, non-identical competing constituents of one relation to become each other in the course of their interaction (see Fig. 1) that leads to the emergence of something third, coinciding neither with the first nor with the second. In other words, the movement of content through the encompassment and absorption into itself of the content of previous forms constantly generates dissimilarity to everything preceding, i.e., novelty and uniqueness. But this very difference arising "in continuation," simultaneously or "in parallel," acquires, in essence, also the form of a new, next opposite, which immediately begins to oppose everything existing (and preceding) and, again, to enter into interaction with it. Thus, under the declared striving toward unity and coincidence, difference and dissimilarity (novelty) arise each time as if automatically and derivatively with respect to the more primordial, more initial, more fundamental and priority movement toward identity. On the other hand, this derivatively obtained non-coincidence, in turn, proves to be the cause and ground for the excitation of the next "act" of movement (striving) in the same direction toward unification, which again, in its turn, generates a new portion of difference and novelty, serving as the condition for the continuation of the "conflict," competition, interaction, etc., etc. — to infinity in its process of quantitative and qualitative renewal. The very divergence into extremes and opposites is a consequence of the preceding and simultaneously a cause and condition for initiating the subsequent movement in the same initially determined direction — toward the mutual transition and coincidence of extremes, toward their "drawing together into a point." [Let us note that the latter is not asserted (and not imposed) as a final result and achieved goal, but is considered only as an understood and comprehended objective tendency that subordinates to itself the course of all processes without exception — as an asymptotic relative movement toward a certain absolute, but never attained limit.]

It is superfluous to mention that representations of spiral development are as old as the world and there is no novelty in these representations. What is new and curious — is in the description of the "mechanism" that produces spiral-cyclical movement, i.e., in the description of the functioning of the internal source of such movement. Heraclitus's saying is widely known that everything flows, everything changes. But WHERE all this flows and changes — until now there have been no clear representations. Only today does the possibility open up to answer this question more or less intelligibly. Today we can meaningfully determine ourselves with respect to the vector of this universal movement: everywhere and always there takes place a total multilevel movement in the direction of achieving the highest level of contentfulness (see formulation @). Everything without exception is permeated by the striving and embraced by the movement in the direction of possessing the highest contentfulness. By this vector of universal movement are determined the causality, meanings, and goals: of life, development, evolution, history, etc. — i.e., of the being and existence of anything whatsoever, in whatever forms. The formulation of the principle of self-development @ gives an idea of the "unidirectionality" and "multilevelness" of development, i.e., firstly, of the uniqueness of each of the arising and developing forms-levels (which fundamentally levels the centrism of any form whatsoever, including biocentrism, anthropocentrism, theocentrism, sociocentrism, etc.), and, secondly, of the universality of the mechanism of movement-development of all forms at their levels (which, at last, throws the long-awaited bridge between the levels of the non-living, the living, and thinking, extremely in demand and necessary for the formation of integral and adequate representations of the world). By virtue of the inexhaustibility of the interconnection of the contentful and formal constituent sides in the relation {content/form} noted above, whatever relative independence the contentful constituent may possess, nevertheless it too cannot manifest (and keep manifesting) itself otherwise than through form. Therefore, despite the leadership and seeming autonomy of the active contentful side at all levels of development, as the contentful constituent at any of the levels can act, again, only forms (such is the essence of the "eternal curse" imposed on content — to be burdened with form, if one paraphrases Marx's well-known expression about the relation of spirit and matter [8, p. 29]). Thus, in both cases (both in the case of the active "contentful" constituent and in the case of the passive "formal" side) in reality one has to deal with changing and developing forms, by which one should understand all sorts of structures and formations of different (objective and subjective) levels of reality of varying degrees of contentfulness (both in macro- and microscopic executions). Realizing the total unitary directionality of all real processes, exhibited by formulation @, it is nevertheless necessary to take into account that at each

of its levels of movement the dominant active contentful constituent (contentfulness) has its own special (specific) forms of representation. A). At the level of non-living nature — this is, as a rule, the energy of the Sun and of space (light, heat, radiation, gravitation, high-energy particles), electrical energy, the chemical energy of oxidation-reduction reactions, etc. B). At the level of living inanimate nature, in addition to what is named above in point A — these are nutrients (from the composition of soil, water, air, combustion products), food (plant and animal), the flow of blood (in living organisms), the genetic program, reflex-psychic activity, instincts, etc. C). At the level of human and social nature, in addition to what is named above at the two previous levels A and B — these are mineral resources, fuel (coal, oil, gas and their industrial derivatives), the human resource (muscular force, spiritual and moral forces, creative and intellectual potential), products of human activity and thinking (image, idea, notions, tool, money, capital, financial and information flows), etc. In other words, at the level of man and society the same active contentful constituent circulates in the form of the forms enumerated in the two previous points A and B, with the addition of human-mediated forms (including the forms of all sorts of products of human activity and thinking — technologies and ideologies). [Additional commentary to the three last paragraphs above. From the standpoint of this concept we proceed from the fact that various kinds of energy, food (nutrients), money (financial flows), information (the information field), etc. — these are merely various forms of movement of the active contentful constituent of the relation {content/form} at different levels of reality. Thus, at the level of non-living nature the active contentful constituent circulates in the form (in the forms) of, chiefly, solar and cosmic energy. A derivative and variety of such circulation flows is electrical and chemical (of oxidation-reduction reactions, etc.) energy. At the level of living inanimate nature, to the above-enumerated circulation flows (continuing their natural transformations also at this level) is added the circulation of the following basic contentful forms: nutrients from the composition of soil, water, air, and combustion products; nutrients from the composition of the soil, plant and animal food; the flow of blood of animals; the genetic matrix, instinctive and reflex activity of the living, etc. And, finally, at the level of man, human thinking, and society — this is, in addition to the above-enumerated flows at the two preceding levels, the circulation of the contentful component in such forms as human-mediated energy of mineral resources and their industrial derivatives (metals, minerals, fuel, etc.), the human resource (in the form of muscular energy, spiritual and moral forces, intellectual potential), products of human activity and thinking (tool, money, capital, financial and information flows, ideas, notions, theories, teachings, i.e., in the most general form — technologies and ideologies), etc. Here it is necessary to note that everything enumerated above from the most widespread forms of movement and circulation of the

active contentful constituent at the aforementioned levels — these are only "reference points" in the general aggregate of such forms; guided by this approach, every inquisitive mind will be able to discover also many other active forms of different levels of contentfulness, also submitting in their movement to the single principle @.] As can be seen on the basis of the analysis of the formulation of the principle of self-development @, the question of the essence of development is not exhausted by the understanding of development as the complication of forms, or as the action of the great biological law of complication, or as the causal continuous complication of matter, etc. [4; 9; 11] (these explanations and definitions of the essence of development dominating in the scholarly milieu are capable today of satisfying at most a housewife); the essence of any development consists in the achievement by forms of different levels of development of the highest contentfulness for each level, which is expressed (and ensured) by the movement of forms in the direction of possessing the maximally possible contentfulness at their levels. The formulated principle of self-development @ is universal; all processes without exception at all levels of reality known to us are subordinated to it and governed by it: at the level of non-living nature, at the level of living inanimate nature, and, finally, at the level of man, human thinking, and society, which can be confirmed and interpreted by diverse factual material, including simple examples from everyday life [1; 20]. Below, on numerous examples, we will show — how this internal principle of self-development "works" in different areas and at different levels of the acquisition of changing reality. Why, then, are the concepts of such an obvious spiral movementdevelopment-evolution of forms considered obsolete today, are examined and discussed reluctantly, and are sometimes subjected to harsh criticism on the part of the official scholarly community? Specialists on questions of evolution substantiate their disagreement with concepts of this kind by the fact that in none of the developing forms (objects, processes, phenomena) does the course of their development resemble (and reduce to) in pure form an ascending classical spiral, and in the majority of analyzed cases this spiral is distorted and interrupted by segments of some straight lines unlike spiral ones (indicating the movement-development of forms by a certain "inertia"), horizontal plateaus (symbolizing the stagnation of forms) and, finally, descending segments (meaning regression, depression, decline, etc.). Indeed, in the study of the evolution of real forms, obvious deviations from the spiral character of their development are observed and recorded at every turn, and therefore, probably, the collective spiral as an indicative external form of expression of any development recedes into the background.

At that, however, the scholarly community loses sight of the following circumstance. As already noted above, the spiral contentful movement has as its reverse visible side the formation of new and the filling with content of renewing forms. In other words, the reverse side of the implicit, hidden movement of content in the direction @ proves to be the inevitable emergence and manifestation of modified and new forms of different levels filled with content, visible and registered by human consciousness, which, as they arise and are modified, enter into interaction with each other, continuing their movement-development in accordance with formulation @. And this objectively conditioned movement toward the increment of contentfulness of any and every interacting form, which, as already noted above, is characteristic of all levels-forms without exception, is accompanied by the total competition of forms (as if we were speaking of participants in a race to a single finish). Not having other sources for replenishing and building up their content except other similar forms, all forms, in the name of ensuring their own movement and development under the "dictate" of principle @, are forced to enter into a harsh competitive struggle "for survival" with the forms surrounding them, the outcome of which proves to be either continuing development, or the perishing and "tearing apart" into parts (absorption and assimilation by competitors) of this or that form. Regression, by and large and on a universal scale, does not happen! By virtue of the mutual total, universal, and wholesale competition of forms striving toward one "goal," which is determined again by the principle of selfdevelopment @, forms "withdraw" from each other and "appropriate" for themselves everything that lends itself to withdrawal and appropriation. The "uncontrolled" taking away and annexation of portions of content takes place at different levels and in different chains of development. At that, the objects of multilevel taking-away-appropriation prove to be quanta of energy, electrons, ions, genes, chromosomes, cells, portions of substance, objects, ideas, bits of information, name, glory, reputation, ideologies, technologies, and entire fortunes (everything, as in real life!). As the experience of numerous and many-year observations shows, when one form "weakens" and "dies" — somewhere nearby (inside or outside) another begins to "assert itself" and "flourish." Stagnation or regression of any form means an increment of content in some of its neighbors (and perhaps not exactly neighbors). As soon as at any form at any of its sublevels the initiative is substantially "intercepted," the "tearing apart" of the content of this form at all its other sublevels and sub-sublevels immediately begins — up to the complete liquidation of this form and the assimilation of its content by other forms. That is why one level-form can quite unexpectedly fade or disappear, and another begin to develop and modify spontaneously. Thus, universal and wholesale stagnation or regression does not happen, but always and everywhere there take place only content-form transformations (or, speaking in the habitual "school" language, — the transition of the contentful constituent from one form into another, i.e., from level to level),

during which the decline or loss of content by one form are signs of the interception of this content and its annexation to itself by another form. Which of the forms, of what level, at what segment or stage of its development will hold out in the unceasing competitive struggle and continue (possibly even more intensively, with a "leap") its further development, and which will disappear forever — it is extremely difficult to predict; for, by virtue of universal competition and total rivalry, the process of the evolution of forms does not have the appearance of a straight high road and does not fall under the definition of a strictly "tree-like" process (as is noted by modern leading evolutionists [18]), but is realized by the method of "punctuated gradualism" with the recreation and reproduction of a "mosaic" picture of development. That is why (namely — owing to such "pressure across the whole field") any and every of the existing and developing forms (including humanity when colliding with the so-called challenges and threats from the side of both other forms of the surrounding world and man himself) has to prove every moment its competitiveness, viability, and right to further existence and development. In the end, against the background of the general single regularity that lies at the basis of the change-movement-development-evolution of all forms without exception and is called the principle of self-development @, the interference and mutual intervention of forms (in line with the same regular striving of theirs toward the appropriation and annexation to themselves of portions of content by taking these portions away from other forms) lead to the chaoticness and spontaneity of the movement of individual forms, which leads to the visible distortion of the general picture of development by inclusions of deviations from the general regularities. The implicitly expressed cause of the departure from the regularities of development (determined by principle @), which (cause) is the interference-intervention of forms in each other's spiral development, has received (both in science and in everyday usage) the name of the all-reconciling, all-explaining, and allforgiving chance. May the reader forgive some figurative expressions and free analogies used in the article to simplify the real picture with the aim of facilitating the examination of the essence of the relations under consideration, — the French, for example, when encountering deviations of this kind in the behavior of men, habitually say: "Cherchez la femme" ("Look for the woman"). With respect to this context, i.e., in a broader sense, this means: look for the form (or forms) from the near and far surroundings that draws onto itself, "snatches away," and annexes to itself portions of "alien" content, thereby distorting the universally spiral configuration (with respect to individual fragments of the general picture) of regular development. It is precisely the total competition of forms unitarily oriented in their movement that violates the decorous regularity and predictability of the trajectory of their development. Not seeing all this background, human consciousness in the person of the scholarly community for that very reason does not wish to

recognize the spiral character of development as the only essential and really realized mode of evolutionary movement. We will not fail to add that the above-stated fully applies also to man as a multilevel natural formation, as an aggregate of a huge number of concrete relations (of different levels and different contentful filling) of the same omnipresent abstract components {content/form}. The failures, illnesses, miscalculations, and errors accompanying man throughout life are the result of the taking-away-appropriation of portions of his own multilevel content (from the category of energy resources of flesh and spirit, food, blood, money, capitals, ideas, knowledge, technologies, information, etc.), carried out (we are speaking of the taking-away-appropriation) by multilevel forms (both "internal" and "external") from among the forms of his near and far surroundings. The complexity of the analysis of the development of such systems is conditioned by the unimaginable interweaving of the simultaneously interacting aforementioned levels-relations, as well as by the inseparability of the components of these relations; the said elements are so interwoven with each other and coiled into such an "ugly" tangle that for the correct untangling of the tangle universal patience and exemplary accuracy are required. [The mentioned complexity is a consequence, firstly, of the natural, objective blurriness and fuzziness of the boundary between the constituentscomponents examined by us in their concrete relations and the most general relation {content/form} and, secondly, a consequence of the infinity of the representation of any and every form, including the "numerators" and "denominators" of the relations under consideration, the same thing in the form of similar relations, and, correspondingly, of the unthinkable interweaving of all these relations and their components with each other. The levels of development are often inseparable, not detachable from each other because the contentful constituent, say, of one level can at one and the same time (in interconnection) prove to be the formal constituent for another level, and vice versa. And also because the contentful constituent of one level necessarily represents a new relation {content/form} for another level. The same should be said about any formal constituent. But the main obstacle on the path of clear understanding of the course of the process of development and of its precise formulations consists in the fact that the natural boundary between the components-constituents "content" and "form" in their relation is difficult to distinguish, and sometimes simply not perceivable (for the time being). Thus, the ingrowing of levels-forms into each other and their growing out of one another is precisely the cause of the impossibility of singling out any of them in "pure" form]. Analogous reasoning, but with the opposite sign, can be adduced with respect to the ascending segments of intensive development of any form (as of any organism, including man) — at such stages of its life path any form, being an active contentful link and on the rise, itself successfully performs

the taking-away and appropriation of contentful portions composition of the forms of its near and far surroundings.
