the

It should be especially noted that the principle of self-development is unconditional and non-relative, and this circumstance is emphasized by the content of its formulation @. The "activity" described by the presented formulation is inherent in all levels of reality without exception and can be characterized as "blind," since in its initial foundational form it contains neither explicit features nor explicit shades of goal-setting. Shades of goalsetting and expediency of attitudes clearly manifest themselves only at quite definite levels of quite concrete forms of realization of this principle, beginning with the forms of higher animals, where the intensity of content-form transformations is substantially higher than the intensity of similar transformations at the preceding levels. In other words, by virtue of the noted heightened intensity of content-form transformations at the levels of animal forms and man in comparison with the speed of similar transformations at other levels-forms, the omnipresence of the said principle is guessed and fixed by human consciousness from a certain time ever more and more definitely, which testifies to its (of the principle) unconditionality and non-relativity contrary to the erroneous opinion about the endowment with the capacity for goal-setting of only some "higher" levels. This, so-called, goal-setting (return to oneself at a new level, feedback, reflection) is simply still very weakly manifested at the "lower" levels of the development of reality owing to the small intensity of contentform transformations at these levels (i.e., by virtue of the small curvature of the spiral coils of development of these levels). In actual fact, this internal principle is immanent to all levels of the development of reality without exception. Independently of us and our preferences, the source of the development of concrete forms-relations at all levels of the organization of reality known to us (quantum [15], atomic, molecular, cellular, tissue, organ, organismic, social) is served by the same principle @, which remains "through-going" for all levels of contentful movement; only the forms of realization of this principle at different levels change, owing to the growth of the intensity of content-form transitions from level to level. Thus, the Universe (as an aggregate of non-living and living formsformations, including thinking) from the standpoint and in the light of this concept — is a gigantic, majestic, multilevel content-form transition stretched over billions of years (more precisely, a chain or sequence of transitional forms of different levels) in the already known direction @ (i.e., in the ultimate expression the world, the Universe — this is precisely the "self-winding" content of "all-embracing" scale, see the simplified visual three-dimensional model of the unfolding process in Fig. 1, approximately reproducing the picture of real "universal" relations). The starry sky — this is an instantaneous "slice" of the forms-traces of the parallel-sequential

movement of "content," grasped and fixed by human consciousness at one moment. The host of worlds hanging above us — these are intermediate layers-stages-levels-forms of the passage of contentful activity, but not in the form corresponding to the initial moment of its sequential movementpassage through the mentioned stages-forms, but in the form of something already "become" and developed by virtue of the unceasing parallelcontinued and continuing since that moment contentful "winding" of these forms. At that, the conditionally-final link of the development of contentful activity in the person of us (humanity) — is also merely an intermediate form of expression or representation of this activity, which, after the passed stages-levels of "non-living" forms, passed also through the stages of more mobile forms — "living-biological," and further — "living-thinking-animated." Today's we (humanity) have reached in our mental development such a level when we can quite well imagine ourselves in the role of a sort of "contentful mollusk" located "in the shell" of an all-round "expanding form" (the Universe) and attempting, as the most contentful link (of those reliably known to us at the current moment), to comprehend, to cognize (i.e., to fill with content and true meaning) that chain of forms which we have "passed" before this since the time of the Big Bang. In turn, that "expanding form" once passed by us strives after us (and, in essence, toward us) for reunification with us and the possession of our contentful principle. In actual fact, we, as the next form of the contentful principle, attempting to close this coil, unwind it again, but already at a qualitatively different (new) level. At that, we equally have no grounds to assert both that the sequential contentful movement ends with us (humanity) and that we (humanity) are the crown of all development, and that an analogous kind of movement of contentful activity did not take place before the moment of the so-called Big Bang and does not take place after the appearance of us (humanity). The awareness of this circumstance, as it were, formally closes the next "circle" of cognition and opens a new stage-coil of qualitatively different investigations of the Universe and the evolutionary movement. Contentful stratifications contribute to the change of the intensity of the striving of content "to itself at a new level" or, in other words, to the change of the curvature of the coil of the spiral of development, — and it is chiefly by the values of these characteristics that one level of development differs from another. Whether we understand this or not as people, whether the dog and the ant "want" this or not as an animal and an insect, whether the river, the ocean, the mountain, the planet Earth, the Milky Way, or the Universe visible and thinkable by us "know" about this or not — independently of this, everything enumerated is in the movement described by formulation @. By the way, when attempting to depict or reproduce in the minutest details the most intimate moment of the mutual transition of content into form and vice versa with the help of, for example, pencil and paper, — your hand will unfailingly draw something resembling an apple, a pear, a leaf of a plant, a

heart, or anything else in a similar vein (for schematic clarity one can recommend for viewing the video clip [19]). This circumstance allows one to discern in the actions of nature a process analogous in directionality, namely — with stubborn periodic constancy, mother living nature herself, year after year over hundreds of millions of years, solves the same task that you have intended to solve as well. Apples, pears, acorns, grains, seeds, leaves, grass, flowers, bushes, trees, animals with their mirror symmetry-asymmetry of forms-bodies (see Fig. 1), as well as the naturally conditioned relation {man/woman} continuing this same list, etc., etc. — all these "living" attributes by their obvious external appearance and internal structure point to the uniform unitary directionality of natural processes, which have as their background the interaction of two constituent sides-components of a single whole — the relation {content/form}. And just as you, she (nature) in no way succeeds in mastering the moment of the content-form mutual transition finally and irrevocably, since the very unattainability and unrealizability of the moment of the exhaustive expression of content by form, the very indeterminacy of this moment (in view of the presence of the so-called step or gap in the place of the supposed closure of the "circle," but of the actual transformation of the "circle into a spiral," see Fig. 1) is precisely the main condition, the moving force, the motive, the stimulus, the cause of the non-stopness, inexhaustibility, and unfathomability of the movement and development of everything existing. The moment of indeterminacy is also an objective and inalienable element of any development! In an analogous manner proceed the processes (movement, flowing-over, and mutual transition of notions and images) also at the level of thinking. At the seemingly attained moment of "victory" it is suddenly discovered that you have expressed and comprehended by your thinking not quite what you were exerting yourself to do a moment earlier, but stand at the source of something else, which is no longer the crown of the preceding, but is the beginning of a new something; i.e., you and your thinking are simply thrown out onto a new coil, onto a new level, toward a new understanding, toward refined-renewed notions and representations, and you are forced to continue the begun movement in the direction of comprehending and shaping the continually slipping-away content (in the guise of a promising thought or a coveted-sought meaning), but already on a new coil and at a new level... And there is no "return" road, since along the road "there" in the direct direction you and your thinking have managed to change, to grow over, to accrete, to enrich yourselves with new contentful details, to "shake off" which and return to the former state there is no possibility whatsoever. Processes similar in character proceed also in the milieu at the level of society; moreover, on the example of the development of social relations this shows through especially prominently. The confrontation (competition, rivalry) of, say, ideological schemes (ideologies) or the geopolitical opposition of states and peoples, the movement from a unipolar to a multipolar world and vice versa, the emergence and resolution of revolutionary situations —

all this also belongs to the external manifestations of the development of the relations of the aforementioned contentful and formal constituent sides (see Fig. 1), but already at other levels and, correspondingly, in other specific forms. The confrontation of ideas and ideologies — this is the highest (from the point of view of human consciousness) level-form of contentful movement of the levels-forms known to us today. The difference in ideologies, i.e., the visible distinction of competing and opposing forms in their relations at this or that level, is conditioned by the internal functional distinctions of the active (often aggressive) and passive (conservative) components of these relations. And for a whole series of individuals (and their communities), participation in confrontation at such a "high" level will be more attractive than interest in the results and consequences of movement and transformations at other, more "primitive" (say, physiological, including the striving toward satiety, physiological pleasures, bodily comfort) levels. And therefore the human striving toward ideological victory, intellectual or moral superiority, spiritual greatness in many cases takes the upper hand and proves to be more priority than the striving toward physical well-being. Everywhere and always, at all levels without exception, there takes place the wholesale, total competition (from soft "sporting" contest and rivalry to merciless struggle) of forms for the possession of the highest level of contentfulness; from the standpoint of human thinking, the level of the highest rank and fullness of embodiment of accumulated contentfulness today is precisely the ideological level. Proceeding from these representations and the understanding of the essence of the problem, it proves simpler to build an adequate model of the opposition and interaction of geopolitical antipodes (two branches of the coil of the spiral of development, see Fig. 1) in their relation of confrontation (conflicts, wars) and simultaneous closest interconnection with each other; on this same basis it is easier to forecast and direct the efforts of competing human communities toward the achievement of a balance of interests, social justice, and harmony. All nature, the whole world throughout its existence solves in fact all the same, one and the same task — of the exhaustive expression of developing content by a moving form, spirally ascending by means of some of its elements (shoots, offshoots, curls), and the final solution of this task has not existed, nor does it exist. To see this all-absorbing movement, one needs only to look more intently at the world surrounding us, equally as at ourselves. All the more so since thinking is already "pregnant" with this concept; sooner or later this approach will be laid at the basis of a new world-understanding. Humanity will be forced to pass over to the practical application of the new model in the name of ensuring its own survival (as a separate definite level-form) in the competitive struggle and interaction with all the other forms-levels surrounding it. Year after year this is demonstrated ever more prominently by the rapidly growing ecological dependence and

vulnerability of man; for the following of the imperative of maintaining ecological equilibrium in the system {man/nature} is nothing other than the painful search by human thinking of the optimal (i.e., the most contentful for every concrete level-stage of development) dialectically verified correlation (relative balance) between the contentful and formal components in this system. The new approach simultaneously testifies both to the limited applicability of the currently dominant anthropocentric (including sociocentric, and theocentric, if you will) model of the world, and to the necessity of transition to another, more integral and adequate model of it. Moreover, outside this approach humanity will not be able even correctly to untie the knot of the aggravating internal contradictions between the components in the relation {West/East}, let alone effectively compete with the forms-levels of its external surroundings (from the composition of the planetary natural environment, and then of near and far space). And, finally, this concept knocks the ground out from under the feet of any and every fanaticism and terrorism: there are no objective grounds for any exclusivity and "Godchosenness." Nazism, religious fundamentalism, "American exceptionalism," or "Banderism" (implying the illusory racial, religious, intellectual, or any other superiority of one nation or confession over another) are based on the excessive absolutization and unjustified centrism of their forms. No exclusivity whatsoever is realizable in principle by reason of the limitedness and transient character of any and every form; this conclusion must be brought demonstratively to the consciousness of people in order thereby to liquidate worldview illiteracy. The algorithm of development is common and universal for all levels-forms, from the photon to the most intricate notional schemes and constructions in the heads of individual individuals: new content will always strive to acquire a new form, and forms — to be filled, to be enriched with new content. Hence — the vortex movement of everything and all. The concept merely notes the typical, standard character of the course of things at all levels: the universal, generalized relation {content/form} is clothed at each concrete level in its own concrete form and "marches" in a through-going manner across the levels in the direction of the replacement of forms by ever more and more contentful ones. Behind each concrete relation-form from the series of such, for example, concrete relations as: {movement/rest}, {time/space}, {acceleration/gravitation}, {energy/mass}, {field/substance}, {continuity/discreteness}, {wave/corpuscle}, {negative charge/positive charge}, {seed/fruit}, {male/female}, {masculine principle/feminine principle}, {spirit/flesh}, {personality/society}, {politics/economics}, {liberalism/conservatism}, {enterprise/inertness}, {intuition/logic}, {subjective/objective}, {abstract/concrete}, {function/structure}, {process/result}, {quality/quantity}, {change/basis}, {active principle/passive principle}, etc., etc., — hides the most abstract relation of

the components {content/form}. And now the investigation of the prospects of change, movement, development of the endless number of diverse concrete relations conditioning the existence of various areas of multilevel reality can be conducted already with the understanding of the essence of this movement-development, i.e., more effectively. With respect to all concrete forms-relations (including the above-enumerated set), the noted movement @ in the direction of achieving the highest contentfulness means that in the course of the development and evolution of concrete forms-relations there is observed a shift toward the increase of the "specific weight," role, and significance of the contentful component (i.e., the "numerator" of the relation) in the composition of concrete forms-relations. At that, the previously formed correlation or established balance of the constituents (components) in their concrete relations is little by little violated, which periodically requires the search and establishment of a new correlation, a new position of the boundary of division between the components in the concrete relations-forms changing in this way. In the course of studying the regularities of the development of relations of this kind, the role and purpose of dialectics itself becomes clearer — as the art of seeking out and establishing the correct correlation (or balanced position of the boundary of division) between the constituent sides in their concrete relations, conditioned (we are speaking of the correct correlation) each time by a definite stage of the development of each concrete relation (i.e., by its definite location in the chain of development). Until now and to this day the said task (of determining the correct correlation between the components in concrete relations) has been solved and is being solved exclusively intuitively, by touch, empirically... The concept, however, allows and proposes to do this more consciously, with the attraction of the enormous potential of human thinking, thereby ensuring the competitiveness of humanity in the confrontation with other forms-levels rivaling it. Thus, everything particular and concrete — these are separate forms into which the generalized abstract form of the relation {content/form} examined by us "dresses itself" in the process of its evolutionary movement @ through the levels. Acquaintance with the concept, which discovers the omnipresent presence of content-form transformations of known directionality, allows one to look with different eyes both at the Chizhevsky cycles, and at the Kondratiev cycles, and at Mendeleev's periodic law, and at the true roots of holism and Bogdanov's tectology, and at the phenomena of similarity, fractality, the anthropic principle, and at the meaning of the golden section, and at the essence of world constants, and at the numerous physical laws of conservation (of substance, energy, momentum, charge, parity, strangeness), and at the gender relations {men/women} [1, p. 55], and at the symmetry-asymmetry of the world (including the mirror and "chiral" symmetry of the forms of the animal world, of man, of his brain, internal organs and systems [1, pp. 13, 14, 25]), etc., etc.

Let us show in the most general terms (without detailing) the "work" of the model {content/form} and of the principle of self-development on the example of tracking the modifications of the forms of the animal world (including man). Mollusks with their spiral-shaped shells — perhaps the most visual ancient specimens of content-form transformations in the course of the movement and development of the contentful constituent of reality at the level of the "living." The mirror symmetry of living beings (the non-identity of the left and right halves of the body) likewise personifies nothing other than the content-form interaction of two sides, of two opposing components of one whole (organism) in the course of the entire life cycle of the individual (we are speaking of insects, reptiles, fish, birds, mammals, etc.). The mirror-reflected parts (branches of the coil of the spiral, see Fig. 1) of living organisms alternately, in the process of the life of the individual, perform functions of either a "purely formal" or a "more contentful" character. The interaction of the mirror halves of a living individual accompanies the growth (accumulation) of the volume and the maturation of the deep content up to the moment of the transfer of this accumulated contentful volume in a "coiled," compressed, concentrated form to its offspring. Further, contentform transformations accompany the process of the mating of individuals, the fertilization of the egg (ovum), the origination of a new phase of the contentful principle and the continuation of its further movement in the same direction, but at a new stage (coil) of its development. Most prominently this process is represented by the widely known examples of the metamorphosis of insects (butterfly — egg — caterpillar — chrysalis — new butterfly). An ant, a dog, a man — these are "shortened" spiral coils of the forms of reality, manifested correspondingly to different stages-levels of its development. If for the non-living (and even for some early, simplest forms of the living) one still has to contrive to discern this spiral coil (by virtue of its small curvature-intensity of development), then for developed living forms it is manifested in the most visual way and is too obvious. The opposition of the left and right halves of living beings (which is most expressed in animals) unmasks the interaction of the contentful and formal halves of the coil in the process of the existence and development of the organism (see Fig. 1) and makes this interaction, like the coil itself, maximally visual and obvious. At that, the symmetry and equilibrium of the left and right halves of the organism — are only seeming; in actual fact this is the asymmetry of two competing, non-identical, non-equivalent constituents-sides of one whole (Fig. 1), ensuring the movementdevelopment of the organism as a whole.

For the entire animal world (including man) one can reveal, trace, and describe the content-form transformations in the most generalized form: the movement of content against the background of the content-form interaction of visually symmetric parts of the organism; further, the interaction of the components of "content" and "form" at the level of the relation {male/female}: mating, fertilization of the ovum, and the birth of a new contentful link in the form of offspring; and as a continuation — the reproduction of the next similar coil of content-form relations at a new level. Moreover, the absorption of one form by another form (the eating of living forms) in the process of the life cycle of the individual and the enrichment (feeding, nourishment) thereby of the developing content — also constitute the essence of content-form transformations at the level of the living. The veil of mystery over the relations between man and woman (as two "halves" of the single whole of the relation {man/woman} — see Fig. 1), the sacraments of love and the continuation of the human race (including the acts of conception, the development of the embryo in the maternal womb, the birth of a child — see Fig. 1), as, incidentally, the riddles of gender relations as a whole, — becomes more transparent in the light of the representations of the content-form movement determined by model @. At that, the development of each smaller fragment (constituent part) of this process, in turn, can be examined (and requires examination) from the standpoints of relations of similar sides (an example of this is, say, the content-form interaction of the branches of the spiral of the DNA molecule throughout its life cycle, the replication of molecules, the division of cells, etc.); processes of the same directionality proceed at the cellular, tissue, organ levels of living organisms, as well as at the atomic and molecular levels of organic and inorganic nature. The incomprehensible until now natural symmetry of the "left" and "right" (characterized by the non-coincidence, opposition, and mutual complementation of the symmetric parts of the whole, and not only in the case of mirror, "chiral," or circular symmetry, but also in the case of other types of symmetry, including the specific symmetries of the physics of elementary particles and quantum mechanics), the interaction of electric charges and the phenomena of electromagnetic nature, the formulations of various laws of conservation and the conditions of the achievement of equilibrium or balance of the quantities (components-constituents-sides) of the relations under consideration in different areas of reality, the phenomena of all sorts of "periodic laws" and "similarity" (including the so-called "fractality" and "anthropic principle") also conceal in their depth the presence and development of spiral-cyclical content-form transformations of known directionality. [At that, owing to the "liveliness" and inexhaustibility of the mentioned relations-transitions, symmetry inevitably transforms into asymmetry, and the laws of conservation are violated at every turn, i.e., are fulfilled only in some approximation with the indispensable reservationsassumptions-limitations].

Within the limits of the plant world, one can judge the analogous character of transformations by the spiral-cyclical movement in the chain "plant — fruit — seed — new plant," by the external appearance and internal structure of trees, bushes, leaves, grass, flowers, fruits... This same principle of self-development allows one to grasp the essence of the directionality of the modifications of forms also in non-living nature, to which, say, for over a century and a half the Periodic system of elements of D.I. Mendeleev (the periodic law of the variability of the chemical and physical properties of elements depending on their atomic mass) points us in its main features. To see such development and manifestation of the essence of content-form relations in the non-living objective world is much more difficult than in the living, since the intensity of the development or transformations of the components-constituents of the relation {content/form} here is extremely low, and therefore the periods of such transformations (coils) stretch out sometimes not simply for centuries, but for millions and billions of years. Let us examine, for example, how and what content-form relations determine the variability of natural landscapes. It is known that the non-uniformity of the heating of various segments of the earth's surface by the sun causes the movement of air masses (winds, hurricanes, tornadoes, etc.). Even if one does not take into account other sources of changes (precipitation, earthquakes, thunderstorm phenomena, cosmic influences, etc.), the movement of air masses one way or another still changes the relief of the locality (the form of the surface of segments of the Earth). This tells on the change of the conditions of the heating of the surface, which, in turn, influence the intensity of the heating of the air and introduce their changes into the character of the movement of the masses of this air. The latter again is reflected in the relief of the locality and leads to the change of the picture of the heating of the surface. In this way, by means of the accumulation of definite local changes (even excluding the actions of tectonic and volcanic nature), different platforms with different climatic zones can be created on the earth's surface. The changed "form" of the surface (the relief of the locality) immediately begins to absorb the sun's rays in a new way and to assimilate their energy-"content" in a new way. This is expressed in the change of heat emission, and, consequently, in the change of the degree and intensity of the heating of the air above the surface. The latter circumstance, in turn, leads to the change (with respect to the preceding) of the character of the movement of the masses of air above the same surface and, correspondingly, to new changes of the relief (form) of the locality. Thus, the new "form" of the surface, as it were, follows the changing heatenergy-"content" of the surrounding air medium. Repeatedly recurring changes of this kind over a long time lead, in the final analysis, to radical qualitative transformations — to the avalanche-like

following of the form after the contentful (in everyday usage — thermal, energy) changes, which means, in the end, a qualitative leap and the transition to a new cardinally changed form with a new volume of accumulated content. The result can be the complete destruction of the former form (landscape) and its replacement by a new one, or such its modification which promotes the even more intensive further following of the "form" after the heat-energy-"contentful" changes (in everyday usage — the establishment of an ever more flexible and close feedback). Let us complicate the task. The interaction with heated air and solar radiation of the surfaces of different water bodies (rivers, lakes, seas, oceans, etc.) leads to the evaporation of the surface layers of the water mirror, which, rising above the earth, are cooled by the atmosphere and form clouds of water vapor. The formation of cloudiness in itself is a factor of substantial influence on the intensity of the heating of the locality by the sun's rays, which, in turn, tells on the character of the movement of air masses above the earth's surface. But this is not all. In the course of rising and cooling, the water vapors condense and the formed moisture, in the end, returns to the earth in the form of precipitation (rains, downpours, hail, snow). This circumstance causes the change of the outlines of shores, of the areas of water surfaces, of the depth of water bodies, of the speed and volumes of the transfer of soil by water, the appearance of new water bodies, and, consequently, the change of the conditions, character, intensity of the subsequent stage of the evaporation of water. The alternate alternation (or, more precisely, the "parallel-sequential" action) of the regimes of the evaporation of water and the falling of precipitation leads at times to substantial changes of the relief (form) of segments of the earth's surface and to the change of climatic zones. Thus, the "form" constantly, as it were, pulls itself up to the "content" of the changes taking place. Even without taking into account the influence of tectonic and volcanic activity, as well as of other cosmic influences, except the action of the sun's rays, one can see how the content-form interaction described above is expressed in substantial modifications of the "forms" (the change of segments of the locality, the displacement of climatic zones), following the heat-energy-"contentful" stratifications. From the standpoint of the described approach, by the way, the essence of such a well-known phenomenon as the water cycle in nature, studied in the junior grades of elementary school, is also understood more easily. Now let us add one more factor accompanying the process under consideration. It is known that the formation of clouds is sometimes accompanied by their polarization (i.e., the accumulation in the cloud of electric charges of one sign). Upon the approach and collision (contact) of polar-charged clouds with each other or with the earth, powerful electric discharges (lightning) arise, which cause at times colossal changes in the surrounding environment and lead to the appearance of new forms. Such can happen again by reason of the change of the relief of localities because of fires, or owing to the formation of qualitatively new chemical compounds of

elements (from the composition of the earth's crust, water, air, etc.) under the influence of the high temperature at the place of the electric discharge. The arisen compounds of elements, having absorbed new contentful changes, themselves contribute to the accumulations of further content in qualitatively new forms. It is quite possible that it is precisely such electric discharges that led, in the opinion of scientists, to the creation of the conditions for the emergence of the prototypes of more mobile, more flexible forms adapted to the absorption and accumulation of subsequent changes, i.e., of "protoliving" forms and subsequently — of protoplasm. The form here unswervingly follows the contentful changes of the surrounding space, contributing, in turn, its own share to the volume of the contentful accumulations caused by this movement. In the final analysis, this can lead to radical changes of a contentful character in the form of cataclysms and the leap-like emergence of cardinally new forms. At that, not only the outlines (relief) of the former forms of the locality and the climatic conditions can change, but also compounds of elements with changed and previously unseen chemical compositions can appear, which, in turn, can become the cause of the emergence of new more flexible and mobile contentful structures and forms. [Let us note that the examples adduced here are examined by us under the assumption of the absence of any elements whatsoever of living nature. The presence of living forms would undoubtedly accelerate the content-form transitions examined, which is what takes place in reality at the present stage of development. Besides, the analysis adduced here is based on the examination of only factors belonging to the category of atmospheric phenomena. An analogous analysis can be conducted also with the examination of the consequences of the influence of other cyclical factors (such as, say, earthquakes with volcanic eruptions and tsunami waves, as the rotation of the Earth around its axis and its movement along the solar orbit, as the leaps of solar activity, as the collisions of the Earth with celestial bodies, etc.)]. All natural cyclical phenomena (for example, the change of seasons: spring — summer — autumn — winter) — this is nothing other than the spiral-like ascent of the content of reality by its renewed forms, i.e., the manifestation of the same veiled content-form movement in the direction of the absorption of the "developing content" by the "developing content" itself. If one speaks of the regularities, say, in the sphere of economics and social development, then Marx's teaching on the change of socio-economic formations owing to the development of the productive forces and the inevitable entry of these forces into confrontation (contradiction) with the production relations (i.e., owing to the contentful outgrowing by the productive forces of the forms or frames of the production relations) — is nothing other than the description of the manifestation of the action in a concrete kind (form) and in a concrete area of reality of the same general tendency or generalized abstract model @.

Again, the productive and any other activity of man is directed at the creation of new forms of different levels in the expectation of the all-round and exhaustive coverage by them and the realization (in accordance with formulation @) of the "carrying-away" content in the image of the needs, desires, intentions, interests, will, aspirations, goals, schemes, ideas, theories arising in the consciousness (and subconsciousness) of man... Billions of people daily, "every moment" consciously and unconsciously are involved in the same single universal process of content-form transformations and participate in the chase by their "forms" after their own "content" fleeing from them, in the hope and striving to clothe this "content" in the proper and befitting the current (or prospective) moment new or renewed (perfect) "form." And let these people move (both mechanically, and physically, and "psychophysiologically") in different directions, commit different deeds, perform various actions (lying, sitting, standing, on the run, moving in an automobile or airplane, etc.), the essence of their striving, movement, existence is one and the same — the taming of their restless, fidgety contentful "I" by clothing it in a correspondingly new and more suitable for this new content form (both in the form of speculative schemes and plans, and in the form of productive activity and its products, as well as in the form of all sorts of external mechanical movements, including the purposeful movements of various parts and organs of the human body). Thus, the productive (practical-theoretical) activity of modern man in aggregate with the products of this activity (in the form of all sorts of technologies and ideologies) represents nothing other than the perfected, developed, complex forms of manifestation of the same single changing reality, abiding at the levels of heightened contentfulness and having come to replace and as a continuation of the forms of the original (simple) labor and cognitive activity of early man in combination with its primitive primary results. The formulated principle of self-development is internally naturally and organically inherent both in us (man) and in all our surroundings. The content-form transformations of unitary directionality lying at the basis of all real processes, described here, not only explain the spiral-cyclical character of these processes, but also allow one to forecast their development. One can additionally adduce a great multitude of examples interpreting the action of the universal principle of self-development @ at different levels of the content-form movement of reality: this internal principle ignites and extinguishes stars, rotates the Earth, moves across the surface of the planet the masses of soil, water, and air; it also "hides" behind the external appearance of vortex atmospheric and oceanic currents and formations (well distinguishable on weather images from near space), of processes of volcanic nature, of the processes of the formation and shaping of planets, stars, galaxies, nebulae (on photographs of far space in the overwhelming majority

of cases the patterns of spiral-like branches of the so-called interstellar and intergalactic substance catch the eye); this same principle stands behind the transformations of chemical elements and compounds upon the change or loss of orbits by valence electrons at the atomic and molecular levels; it also drives "in a circle" the herds of wild animals and flocks of migratory birds; thanks to the realization of this principle people are born, live, communicate with each other, embody in life their intentions and plans. Everywhere visually and speculatively the spiral-cyclical character of really proceeding processes is noted, behind the facade of which are hidden the content-form transitions of known directionality. We (humanity), equally as all our surroundings, are embraced and simultaneously permeated by this principle, constitute its essence and represent it in phenomena, are in its power, are subordinated to it, and it is we who permanently renew and reproduce it. And no external act of creation and control on the part of some "creator" or "maker" is required for the realization of this internal principle. The world is relatively simple and uniform in essence (by the principle of organization, the mechanism or algorithm of the movement and development of content), but infinitely complex and diverse in its manifestations by form — owing to the natural, unimaginable in details mutual interlocking, interweaving, and ingrowing of forms-levels into each other in their interaction. Moreover, in the light of the concept expounded here, the universal and allembracing Absolute in the form of an eternal, unchanging, finished, and perfect abstraction is unrealizable in principle, since everything existing, in the final analysis, is a process to a much greater degree than some result fixed once and for all, than some finished formation, than some perfect "construction," than some "boundless-limitless essence," etc., etc. The Universe, equally as all its separate elements, is made, recreated, reproduced, and renewed every moment, in real-time mode, being modified, existing, and coexisting in the form of changing forms. With an eye to what has been said, it becomes obvious in what manner one should relate also to the question of the cognizability of the world. Since the world, the Universe is incessantly and non-stop "made," "recreated by winding," and does not represent a sort of suitcase with books, the cognition of the making, every-moment-reproducing world — this is all the more an infinite and infinitely fascinating contentful process. How can one speak of some all-round, full, and final cognition of the world, if the world incessantly "unfolds" in ever new and new contentful forms? In such a cross-section it is more reasonable to speak of the cognizability and cognition of the world as of the perfection of the possibilities for the anticipation of such an "unfolding" of forms, of the possibilities for the foretelling of the emergence of new and the tendency of the change of existing forms, i.e., of the forecasting of coming events and their consequences, speaking in the habitual everyday language. The effective forecasting of the development of the forms of reality — this is the prospect of the contentful development of humanity.

The representations of development expounded above are directly connected also with the question of the elaboration of the methodology of the investigation and cognition of the surrounding world. We are speaking of the creation of a more or less universal form-algorithm of the investigation of the development of any objects, processes, and phenomena in different areas of reality [1, p. 66; 20, p. 36]. The revealed general objective regularities of development serve simultaneously as the single unifying foundation for all the sciences and areas of knowledge existing at the present time. The principle of self-development (self-organization), which is the central core of the proposed concept of development, easily lends itself to mathematical formalization (by way of reduction to recurrent dependences and the organization of iterative-cyclical computations by the method of successive approximations), which allows one to hope for the construction of more perfect and effective (than those existing at present) prognostic models of the development of reality (or, in the habitual terminology, models of the surrounding world). One can suppose that the most complex and interesting moment in the course of the creation of the corresponding mathematical models of the investigation of the spiral-cyclical development of this or that form of reality will prove, in all probability, to be the solution of the question of the all-round account of the consequences of the interference (superposition, intervention, and influence) of the multitude of similar spiral processes of the development of all sorts of neighboring and accompanying competing forms. The identification of the process of cognition with the process of the contentful multilevel movement-development of reality by way of the change of its forms-levels in the direction @ allows one to speak confidently of the achievement of a certain meaningfulness in the realization of the process of the cognition of reality itself. As one can notice, there are described here mainly the general principles of the organization and development of reality (including the process of its cognition). It goes without saying that it is impossible without the participation of professionals to correctly adapt the general propositions to the numerous specifically-oriented areas of knowledge. This part of the work is within the power only of "narrow" specialists. At the same time, acquaintance with the general principles will considerably facilitate for any specialist the sorting-out in the existing interweaving of specific formsrelations of different levels forming the sphere of his professional interests. Afterword In our days, in scientific circles there is widespread a rather biased and arrogant attitude toward the subjective and the ideal. And meanwhile, by what other path, if not as a result of the "construction" by individual individuals of subjectively-abstract ideal forms, is the universally significant

and universally accepted built up in the course of successive approximations to the reproduction of the most objective of all (i.e., the objective content of reality)? One should not think that everything expounded above belongs only to the category of exercises in the construction of empty ideal abstract constructions. Most concentratedly today the essence of even the most pragmatic concept (theory, teaching, etc.) can be expressed only by means of conceptual (notional) formulations, i.e., by means of abstract subjectively-ideal forms. The region, the medium, and, finally, the level of the ideal — this is that "space" into which modern man lays out everything most significant, most contentful, that he alone can dispose of today. And goals, and methods, and schemes, and decisions, and conclusions, and models, and images, and pictures, and plans, and programs, and ideas, and theories, and teachings, etc., etc. — all this is from the region of the ideal (which is likewise merely a form of the next approximation to the most complete expression of the objective content of developing reality). One more argument in favor of the significance of the ideal for man consists in the fact that without an idea and, in a broader sense, outside ideology, man simply "becomes brutish," reducing his life to the stuffing of the belly and the performance of natural needs, i.e., to a "looped" existence at "earthbound" levels. It is precisely with reliance on the contentful ideal constructions being built that the speech here is about nothing other than the prospect of the development of humanity. In times when, behind the infinitely-routine everyday cyclicality, man sees no meaning in life and in his existence, there opens up the possibility of looking at all this with completely different eyes. Every man is not simply capable, but obliged to become a researcher. In each of us slumbers the subconscious striving to "contrive to peek around the corner" (satisfying his innate curiosity), to foretell the onset of events, to forecast and anticipate the appearance and development of new forms. One needs only to activate this slumbering natural striving and impart to it a new impulse, i.e., consciously bring it out to a new level. The approach described here is called upon to aim man not simply at the cognition of a certain world existing from time immemorial, but predisposes to the meaningful investigation of everything that "is being made," that "is being reproduced" and "recreated" directly here and now — in real-time mode both in our near and far surroundings. To date there exist a million concepts of the world-order. Everyone builds his own model (picture) of the world in his own way, proceeding from the knowledge and experience both of his own and of preceding generations. Hence grow the most diverse variants of world-understanding, a series of

which proves to be among the dominant models of the world-order accepted by social consciousness. Being in actual fact different approximations to one and the same, many of them at first, and sometimes for quite a long time, seem equivalent — go figure which variant correlates more precisely with developing reality... But only until the time when obvious failures and irreparable losses begin in the making of decisions and the realization of the corresponding actions in the channel of following this or that universally significant model. And only then, when the problem of the survival of humanity (or at least of its part) is pushed to the foreground, does the question of the assessment of the adequacy of the existing worldunderstanding rise to its full height, i.e., the assessment of the sufficiency of the level (fullness) of the objective contentfulness (objective content) of the ideal model "chosen" previously as the guiding star. Inadequate (low-contentful) thinking (with all its low-contentful non-true abstract subjectively-ideal forms-models of reality), having as its foundation including false premises and sincere delusions, sooner or later risks being punished by the premature termination of existence and the disappearance of the very form of humanity owing to the "dragging-apart" into parts, the absorption and assimilation of this form by more contentful competitors. Taking into account the circumstance that there are no two or more equally contentful and equivalent "truths" or points of view regarding one and the same subject of judgment or investigation (i.e., there do not exist in principle identical and equal-sized approximations to the absolute truth), one must clearly realize that the acuteness of the question of the assessment and choice by man of the most contentful (true) and most adequate variant of decision from among two or more such variants will never be blunted or lost. Meanwhile, the inadequacy of the perception of the world leads man to catastrophe — one wants to think that someday this thought will take possession of the developing human consciousness and become its topical and habitual content. In such a connection it is difficult to overestimate the significance of the decisions made by man (as well as the measure of his responsibility) in the choice of this or that ideal model of the development of reality. Such is the far from harmless role of every, it would seem, insignificant subjective ideal. Much of what has been expounded above, as has already been noted, is presented in various sources of scientific and philosophical literature in parts. However, such a concentrated exposition of separate views and representations in the form of an integral single concept is reproduced for the first time — everyone who is accustomed to systematically tracking scientific and philosophical publications of the indicated directionality can verify this. The ever-growing number of examples of the interpretation and confirmation by factual material of the conceptual propositions formulated above gives grounds to consider that the worldview concept presented here proves to be today the most contentful and adequate. The Nature and Meaning of Fundamental Constants

The Nature and Meaning of Fundamental Constants Abstract The description of the most general and many specific regularities of the development of the surrounding world is seldom managed without the presence of the so-called world constants — universal values entering into the composition of the form-building means of such a description (mathematical formulas, mathematical dependences, mathematical expressions). A great multitude of printed works is devoted to these values, and, despite the abundance of investigations of their remarkable properties and characteristics, the circumstance is universally recognized that until now the true nature and the actual meaning of the fundamental world constants are unknown to anyone. The present work removes the veil of mystery from the very popular and extraordinary numbers π, e, and φ, to the solving of the secret of which numerous publications in various information sources are devoted. On the example of these three most ancient and omnipresent numbers, the article shows in what manner one should approach the establishment of the nature and the revelation of the meaning of constants of this kind. The work can be recommended to the attention of researchers as initial material for the study of the background and the elucidation of the meaning of the entire spectrum of existing fundamental constants, as well as as a source of alternative views on the nature and essence of the surrounding world. Introduction Many versions are known regarding the origin, purpose, and role of the fundamental world constants. We are speaking, first of all, of the most ancient constant "π" [2, 10, 11, 13, 17, 20, 22, 28, 34, 51, 52] — the designation and numerical value of the ratio of the length of the circumference to its diameter, as well as of the no less fundamental and popular (although not so ancient as the first) constant "e" [2, 8, 10, 11, 17, 20, 26, 29, 31, 43, 51, 53] — the designation and numerical value of the base of natural logarithms. In this same connection we shall not fail to recall yet one more outstanding constant "φ" [1, 2, 3, 7, 14, 19, 23, 25, 35, 39, 40, 47, 50, 51] — the designation and numerical value of the so-called golden ratio, or golden section, or golden proportion.

To the extraordinary and mysterious (not to say — mystical) numbers π (pi), e, and φ (phi) a great multitude of printed works is devoted. Special attention is traditionally paid to the enumeration of the astonishing properties of these dimensionless constants. And at the same time, despite the abundance of investigations and descriptions of the remarkable characteristics of the indicated numbers, the circumstance is universally recognized that until now the nature and the actual meaning of these values are unknown to anyone. In contrast to the mission of replenishing the list of enthusiastic descriptions of the wonderful properties of the above-enumerated world constants, the modest task of the present article consists in showing the source of their extraordinary properties, in uncovering and laying bare the true nature and essence of these values. We shall solve the set task, relying on the concept of development described in the previous article of this same collection, as well as in the works [5, 21, 54]. In investigating the essence of the mentioned constants, we proceed from the fact that reality (the world surrounding us, nature) is always at least twosided (dual). Any object, process, phenomenon in the most general abstract form represents a relation of two sides-constituents-components: {content/form}. Then any concrete form (implying under itself the same object, process, phenomenon) is determined by the correlation of the indicated sides-constituents in each of their concrete relations. The development of the forms themselves represents the change of the correlations of the noted constituents within the framework of their relations. In such a connection it becomes obvious that the forms determined by the above-mentioned correlations can be both more and less contentful (by virtue of the presence of different shares of the contentful and formal components in their relations). The general tendency of the development and change of forms (as the experience of human observations throughout human history shows) is such that in the course of their (the forms') development there takes place a shift of the correlation of the components (in their relations) toward the "numerator," i.e., toward the contentful component (what is meant is the principled orientation toward the general movement-development of forms in the direction of the acquisition of the highest contentfulness) [5, 21]. Such an installation-orientation is called the principle of self-development, with the formulation of which the reader can acquaint himself there — in the previous article of the present collection and in the works [5, 21, 54]. This formulation means approximately the following: everything that exists and develops has as the direction of its development and existence the expression (coverage, absorption, assimilation, comprehension...) by itself, existing and developing, of itself,

existing and developing. And such directionality or orientation of movementdevelopment is inherent in all developing forms without exception. In the text of the article the reader will not encounter a single formulaic expression for the determination (calculation) of the values investigated by us, just as he will not see "juggling" with their numerical values, which at present have the possibility of being reproduced with any preassigned accuracy. The demonstration of such effects does not add, unfortunately, clarity in the intention to find out the true nature and meaning of the constants under consideration: for no matter how many times one says the word "halva," it will not become sweeter in the mouth. For us in this case the most important thing is to uncover and manifest the deep philosophy of these phenomena, i.e., to reveal the essence, meaning, background, nature, and the urgent necessity of the use by man of these parameters (values) in his activity. The possibility of the solution of this task is determined by no means by the skills of mastery of the craft, but by the level of mastery of the art of "aerobatics," i.e., dialectics. Without being distracted by the description of the astonishing properties and characteristics of the dimensionless constants under consideration (these properties are enumerated and described in abundance in the literary sources by the references mentioned at the very beginning and in many, many others), let us stop on the main, in our opinion, question: in what, strictly speaking, consisted and consists for man the urgent need or necessity of the introduction of these numbers into everyday use, into practice, into circulation? In other words, we shall answer predominantly such a question: what main problem for himself did man attempt to resolve (let it be unconsciously, unthinkingly, intuitively) by way of the introduction of these numbers into practical circulation? At that, let us note that, answering these simple questions, we shall inevitably have to answer also the more general question: what fundamental regularity of development leads to the necessity of the seeking-out and use of such constants? In two words and still very simplistically, the answer to these questions will look approximately as follows. When man does not know something, does not understand or underunderstands it (i.e., encounters something unexplored, both in the small and in the large), he strives with all his might to express this unknown through the known and cognized, — let it be not so smoothly and thoroughly, not completely, but with some "appendage" of the not quite ordinary (say, through some atypical or unknown — until the time — combinations of known forms), but still mainly through the habitual and already familiar. And this means that in such cases man is moved by the striving — to clothe the new developing content (new knowledge) in a more or less known form, to impart to this new content (new knowledge) a more or less already meaningful and habitual appearance. Therefore, however strange this may look, not only at the first steps, but always in such cases human thinking

solves the main philosophical problem for itself of the seeking-out and clothing of the renewing and accumulating content of developing reality in a form more or less (and in the limiting expression — in the most and maximally) answering to this content and simultaneously "digestible" for comprehension. In the same cross-section, the initial internal contentful human activity or aspiration seeks embodiment or expression through the external formal pragmatic execution; at that, the contentful side in this tandem (relation of the contentful and formal components) always proves to be the leading and moving side. Probably, for the majority of us this assertion abides in a still insufficiently manifested and not fully realized form, but the matter stands precisely so! Now let us pass over to the detailed examination of what has just been said with respect to the numerical constants of interest to us. § 1. Let us begin with the number "π" (pi) As is known still from the school bench, the number "π" represents the ratio of the length of the circumference to its diameter. This is the most distinctly manifested relation of two sides-constituents of reality, for which are characteristic in this case such basic relations of its properties as {curvature/rectilinearity}, {continuity/discreteness}, {boundlessness/limitedness}, etc. In each of the noted relations there are present and mutually linked two sides-constituents of reality. However, notwithstanding the closest interconnection, neither the one nor the other side is identical to the other and represents components not replaceable by each other; at that, to such a degree not replaceable that between these sides-components there always lies a naturally conditioned boundary of division. Human thinking comparatively easily copes with the realization of the formal side of such relations, i.e., of the "denominator," and matters stand much worse with the perception of the contentful side — the "numerator." Proceeding from this, from the most ancient times everything still not understood and incomprehensible man trivially strives to express through the already understood and comprehensible. The relations adduced above in this sense are no exception. In his attempts to express, to comprehend the incomprehensible continuity and boundlessness of the curvature of the circumference through the comprehensible limitedness and discreteness of its rectilinear diameter, man inevitably comes to the sought relation, expressed and designated by a certain from time to time approximately repeating numerical value (subsequently named the constant "π"): in a rather obvious and unpretentious way he manages to express the incomprehensible irrational through the well-known and sufficiently studied rational. Thereby, in the image of the number "π" we find the first productive attempt of man to cope with quite serious difficulties, which are conditioned by the objective presence of two objectively opposing and non-interchangeable

sides of reality [5, p. 55]. This is an undoubted achievement and victory of man, but not without subsequent disappointments. Perhaps the simplest algorithm (way) of finding (computing) the constant "π" reduces to the seeking of the limit of the ratio of the perimeters of the regular polygons inscribed in the circumference and circumscribed (around it) (with the unlimited increase of the number of sides of these polygons) to the diameter of this circumference. In this way is realized the striving toward the expression of the most contentful correlation between the contentful and formal components in their relation {length of circumference/diameter of circumference}, indirectly determining, in essence, also the correlation between such fundamental sides-components of reality as {curvature/rectilinearity}, {continuity/discreteness}, {boundlessness/limitedness}, etc. The straight line and the circumference — these are the two most popular lines on the plane of the times of the absolute leadership of geometry among all the sciences then being born. Between the two remarkable lines — the circumference and its diameter — is enclosed a whole family of broken lines (polygons) inscribed in the circumference (and circumscribed around it) with perimeters easily computable and arbitrarily closely approaching the length of the circumference. An excursion into history shows in what unpretentious, but quite laborious way rational man approached the comprehension of the irrational: Archimedes, as is known, in his computations went as far as the calculation of the perimeters of the 96-gons inscribed in the circumference and circumscribed around it [42, p. 43; 52]. And, despite such laborious and painstaking work, to express the length of the circumference wholly and without remainder through its diameter still did not succeed [18, p. 291; 24, p. 131; 51, pp. 33–34]. All subsequent attempts of new times only confirmed the unsolvability of this task in an exhaustively exact expression. Today the constant of interest to us has already been computed with an accuracy of up to billions of significant digits after the decimal point [20], but in the final form the task has still not been solved. What does this speak of? Only of the fact that in the course of investigations of this kind man has come up closely against the inexpressibility of the real (really existing) irrationality by a simple rational way, even in the case of a deliberately limited, so-called flat geometric space. Alongside the testimony of the achieved victory, corresponding to some smallish possibility of representing one through the other, the number "π" — this is at the same time also an indicator of the futility of the attempts to achieve the final and complete expressedness of the objective boundlessness, continuity, curvature through the no less objective limitedness, discreteness, rectilinearity. The inexhaustibility and progressive productiveness of such attempts, but at the same time also the principled unattainability of the expression of the entire fullness of content in a final kind and form are interpreted and substantiated by the above-mentioned formulation of the principle of self-

development, which proves to be the background of that very (sought) regularity of development leading to the necessity of the seeking-out and use of this constant. In the indicated connection, the meaning of the constant "π" is seen in the fact that it fixes a certain transitional ("borderline") numerical correlation between the contentful and formal constituents in the relation {"curvilinear" circumference/"rectilinear" diameter}. In this case human thinking contrived to express in a quite definite manner — by a more or less concrete number — the correlation between curvature, boundlessness, and continuity (the personification of which is the curved line of the circumference), on the one hand, and rectilinearity, limitedness, and discreteness (personified by the rectilinear diameter of the circumference), on the other hand. And in this same case we state by no means a final and exhaustive, but still a productive attempt of thinking to throw a bridge from the rational reality (from the standpoint of traditional human thinking) to a reality to some extent irrational (the term "irrational" is used here in the meaning of something not fully cognized, insufficiently investigated, and comparatively more contentful, not yielding to realization in full measure, and not in the sense of something mystical and otherworldly). In the striving to express the irrational through the rational (i.e., to throw a "little bridge" from one to the other) and appear the remarkable relations, similar to the number "π". By reason of the objective unattainability of the full and exhaustive expression of the irrational through the rational (the infinite through the finite, the continuous through the discrete, the indefinite through the definite, the abstract through the concrete, etc.), such relations are expressed and displayed by irrational numbers (i.e., infinite non-periodic fractions). The very irrationality of such numbers-relations (inherent in the dimensionless world constants under consideration) always presupposes, as it were, a long (infinite) "run-up" before the "jump" from the formal side-constituent of the relation to its more contentful side-constituent (for example, from the straight line or plane to the circumference or sphere in the case of the determination of the number "π"). Just as any natural formation, man and his thinking are at least two-sided (dual), and therefore contradictory. By virtue of the indicated peculiarities, even such a powerful instrument as abstract human thinking with its characteristic enormous intensity of the realization of content-form transformations proves unable to solve such tasks, moving exclusively in measured-gradual steps; only by a leap in thinking (i.e., at the level of the forms-concepts and representations mediated by human thinking about the contradictory components of reality in their actual relations of interconnection and mutual transitions) is the barrier or boundary of division between the opposing sides-components of reality overcome. Whether we want it or not, dialectics is present here and further, as they say, at full height.

From the set of information about all sorts of investigations of the number "π" it is known that there exists a multitude of curves of different orders, somehow correlating with the segments of straight lines connecting the ends of these curves on the plane, but only the ratio of the length of the circumference to its diameter is the most large-scale and "full-blooded" in magnitude (i.e., the most contentful) of all such relations. The highest contentfulness of the relation-form expressed and designated by the number "π" (as the ratio of the length of the circumference to the length of its diameter) is conditioned, first of all, by the remarkable property of the "numerator" of the relation, i.e., of the circumference; as mathematics asserts, of all closed curves of given length on the plane the circumference bounds the region of maximal area [2]. From the outstanding contentful fullness of the circumference and proceeds the intuitively-practical interest of man in the calculation of the length of the circumference by means of its diameter — in the striving and hope of the finding of a simple device for the exhaustive expression of the irrational curvature through the rational rectilinearity. Let us keep in memory this remark about the highest contentfulness of this wonderful correlation of the two components under consideration in their relation. Thus, of all the correlations between the components of reality in the general relation-form {curvature/rectilinearity}, the number "π" represents such a special correlation of the components in their particular (and most kindred to the general) relation-form {length of circumference/diameter of circumference} which answers to the maximally possible contentful filling (or the highest contentfulness) of this form-relation. At that, the irrationality of the number "π" testifies to the principled impossibility of the establishment in exhaustive form of the final numerical correlation between the components of the relation-form {length of circumference/diameter of circumference}, corresponding to its maximally contentful filling, and thereby to the impossibility of the acquisition of the highest contentfulness by this form-relation. The impossibility of the exhaustive expression of the highest contentfulness by the indicated form-relation is ascertained in the course of the modeling of the process of the acquisition (achievement) of this highest contentfulness by the procedure of the computation of the correlations between the perimeters of the polygons inscribed in the circumference (or circumscribed around it) and the diameter of this circumference with the unlimited growth of the quantity of the sides of the polygons. The productively realized attempt to link two independent and noninterchangeable sides of reality proves subsequently to be in demand in the plan of the use of the found compromise number "π" in all other cases which one way or another require the replication of similar "borderline" values for the adequate description of more complex real situations. Thereby the number "π" belongs to the series of remarkable and extraordinary numerical

correlations thanks also to the need for it for the subsequent application in the composition of formal (formulaic) expressions and dependences describing the development of diverse (including compromise) real complex forms and processes. The presence of the constant "π" in such descriptions indicates the belonging of the forms and processes described with its help to the number of optimal (i.e., endowed with heightened contentfulness). Examples of diverse tasks in the solution of which the number "π" participates as a constant for the description of various kinds of optimal forms and processes can be found, say, in such printed works [6, 10, 11, 16, 24, 27, 30, 32, 33, 38, 46, 48, 49]. § 2. Let us pass over now to the investigation of the constant "φ" (phi), responsible for the so-called golden ratio, or golden section, or golden proportion The main peculiarity and rule of the construction of the golden proportion are more or less precisely, in our opinion, conveyed by the words of I. Kepler, who called this proportion itself continuing itself: "It is arranged so that the two junior members of this endless proportion in sum give the third member, and any two last members, if one adds them, give the following member, with the same proportion being preserved to infinity" [15]. This quotation describes a special algorithm of the composition of the whole from the aggregate of parts. Continuing to answer the question posed at the beginning of the article — what fundamental regularity of development leads to the necessity of the seeking-out and use of such a constant and of the golden ratio displayed by it, — we state that this regularity too is conditioned by the above-mentioned principle of self-development. In the course of the realization of this principle, the universal internal mechanism (algorithm) creates ever more "living" (i.e., ever more contentful) unique natural forms-formations. Submitting to the single principle of the exhaustive expression of the whole integrity of the developing content, nature by successive approximations solves the task of the coverage, representation, and comprehension of this contentful integrity by a totality of ever newer and newer forms. In this concrete case investigated by us, the role of the contentful and formal constituent sides in the generalized abstract relation {content/form} is played by the components {whole/part}. Nature, as a definite-level external formalization of the developing reality, constantly gropes for the most optimal way of solving the indicated task. By the empirical path (by way of the realization of numerous approximations) there are produced forms which most correspond to the requirements of the exhaustive in the final analysis solution of the mentioned task, i.e., the most contentful forms. Forms-relations reproduced on the basis of the golden correlation between the components of these relations stand in the row of

such most contentful forms for their levels. The developing reality is capable, through human thinking as yet another link-form in the chain of forms of its contentful development, of fixing and evaluating the degree of contentfulness of its own (both already existing and newly arising) linksforms in the striving of the latter toward the possession of the highest contentfulness on their levels. What is characteristic of the heightened (and in the limiting case — the highest) contentfulness, and in what does it express itself with respect to the forms-relations endowed with it? At the level of human consciousness the mentioned contentfulness of the correlation displayed by the number "φ" and the effectiveness of the chosen path are evaluated and confirmed by the maximal density of the "mastering" (i.e., the "best mastering") of free space by separate forms [2, 15], as well as, by virtue of this circumstance, by the establishment of the presence of the indicated correlation in a whole series of natural formations-forms-relations [1, 3, 14, 19, 23, 25, 40, 50, 51]. As in the case with the remark (see earlier) kept in memory about the highest contentfulness of the correlation designated by the number "π", with respect to the number "φ" the speech likewise goes about forms with correlations (between the components {whole/part} in their relation) answering to the expression of heightened contentfulness. The golden relation-form differs from other relations-forms by such a correlation of the contentful and formal components which ensures the maximal filling with content of this form-relation at the given (this or that) stage-level of its development. A similar remarkable correlation is fixed by a certain number "φ" in the row of all other numerical values corresponding to all other correlations within the framework of the relation under consideration. And the matter, perhaps, is not even in some concrete numerical value of this correlation, but in the fact that such a remarkable correlation exists in principle among the multitude of correlations and of the numerical values corresponding to them. The special correlation of the contentful and formal components, ensuring the maximal contentfulness of the golden forms-relations, endows these forms with heightened stability and viability. For the expression of the remaining advantages of such outstanding forms-relations, and in fact for the evaluation of the degree or measure of their internal contentfulness, man has invented and uses in everyday life a great multitude of all sorts of epithets and definitions (directly linked, among other things, with the aesthetic principle manifesting itself at the level of external human emotions), such as, for example: divine beauty, perfection of form, ideality, exceptionality, uniqueness, harmony, optimality, special property, high quality, "pleases the eye," and many, many others. The heightened (maximally attainable or highest for its level and stage of development) internal contentfulness of such forms-relations, expressing itself in a set of their remarkable external characteristics and properties, is connected with the presence, as already noted, of the above-mentioned concrete numerical correlation in a whole series of nonliving and living forms known to man. Confirmation of this can

be found in numerous literary sources (see the references above and below) devoting excessive attention to the description of the wonderful properties of the golden section. Coming to such a kind of optimal correlations in a series of certain forms, nature from year to year continues by spiral-cyclical movements to "polish" its creations, achieving results still more astonishing and still more approximated to the expression of the highest contentfulness. In such a way the developing reality itself, through the host of its changing and newly arising forms in the channel of the algorithm (formulation) of the principle of self-development, by periodic reciprocating movements "refines" the value of this correlation in the relation {whole/part}. Meanwhile, human admiration, deification, and exaltation of the known properties represent a reaction of man only to the external signs of the manifestation of the internal contentful fullness of such special forms and leave without due attention this very internal contentfulness. Not being sophisticated in such questions, man is inclined to absolutize and mystify everything that is above his understanding, taking the superficial phenomenon for the innermost essence. And the golden section as an external manifestation of the heightened internal contentfulness of definite forms is not an exception in this case. So, in what, in fact, does the divinity of the forms-relations reproduced by the algorithm of the golden proportion consist? Only in the fact that precisely such a correlation, such a proportion of the part and the whole ensures the maximally possible contentful filling of this or that form at a definite stage of its development, i.e., at this definite segment of its path the given form is most contentful (stands out from the other forms by its heightened contentfulness). The loss or forfeiture by a form of a part of its heightened internal contentfulness at another segment (stage) of its path inevitably leads also to the change (or disappearance) of the corresponding external signs of this form. As in the case with the number "π", the establishment of yet another remarkable correlation "φ" between the contentful and formal components in their relation is, undoubtedly, yet another victory of rational man. About the special relations of the whole and the part man has reflected from time immemorial.
