In this connection it becomes obvious that forms of reality defined by the aforementioned ratios can be either more or less Contentful (due to different shares of content and formal components in their relations). The overall trend of development and change of forms of reality — as human observations throughout human history show — is such that during their (forms') development there occurs a shift in the ratio of components (in their relations) towards the numerator of the relation (if we consider such a relation as a mathematical fraction), i.e., toward the Contentful component of the relation (meaning the fundamental orientation toward the general movementdevelopment of forms of reality in the direction of increasing their Contentfulness, and ultimately achieving ultimate highest Contentfulness) [5, 7]. This setting-orientation indicating the direction of variability of all forms is called the universal principle of self-development (self-organization) of reality, formulated as follows: the development of the content of forms of reality takes place in the direction of exhaustive expression of developing content by the very same developing content of these forms [3, 5, 7, 18]. Applied to the surrounding world, this formulation means roughly the following: everything that exists and develops has the direction of its development and existence expressed (i.e., encompassed, absorbed, assimilated, comprehended, etc.) by itself existing and developing. As a visual example in this case, the image of a "self-winding" three-dimensional spiral seems most appropriate [2, 7]. And such an orientation or tendency of change-movement-development belongs to all developing forms of reality without exception. For greater illustrativeness of what was just said, note that with regard to such a level (area) of developing reality as, say, man and society, this universal principle drives forward not only positive (from the standpoint of human consciousness) morally ethical and humanitarian aspirations of man but also negative ones (again, in terms of human consciousness) — his thoughts, motives, secret impulses and "sinful" inclinations — such as arrogance and condescension, feelings of exclusivity and superiority over one's neighbour, envy and vanity, etc., etc., Everyone intends and strains to present himself and appear as the most significant, competent, respectable, successful, etc., in short — the most contentful, which is also determined and conditioned by the same universal objective striving of any forms for possession of the highest contentfulness. After all, how sweet and tempting it is to participate in some kind of political hangout and be considered in this world an influential person — a disposer of human destinies!

Thus, in light of the dialectical concept of development presented here, objective reality represents motion and succession of its forms in the direction of possessing the highest contentfulness (i.e., maximally achievable content), and all processes of development and cognition of reality should be studied and viewed through the prism of content-form transitions, transformations, transmutations of unitary (i.e., strictly defined) directionality. A specifically directed variability of the abstract relation {content/form} is conditioned, on the one hand, by the unity and, on the other hand, non-identity (non-sameness) of its components, due to which both components are in a state of interaction and inexhaustible movement towards convergence with each other and acquisition of equilibrium (balance) or mutual expressibility by each other (which means: becoming maximally contentful for form and defining its perfect form for content). The presence in one integral relation of two components with different types of activity generates a whirlwind, vortex, circulation, "selfwinding" spiral, i.e., a specific type of change (movement, development) of both components of this relation and the relation as a whole. The famous saying of Heraclitus about everything flowing and changing still left open the question of where exactly everything flows and changes — clear ideas were lacking until now. Only today does the opportunity arise to answer this question more or less sensibly; today we can meaningfully define the vector of this universal movement: everywhere and always there is total multi-level movement towards reaching the highest level of contentfulness; everything is pervaded by the striving and covered by movement towards possession of the highest contentfulness. This universal movement vector determines causality, meanings and goals: life, development, evolution, history, etc., i.e., the existence of anything whatsoever, in whatever forms. The algorithm of development — common and universal for all levels-forms of reality, from photon to the most elaborate conceptual schemes and constructions in individual minds: new content will always strive to acquire a new form, while forms seek to be filled, enriched with new content. Hence — a vortex-like motion of everything. By this conception, only the typical, standard character of the course and transformation of things at all levels is pointed out: the universal generalized relation {content/form} is embodied on each specific level in its own concrete form and consistently "proceeds" through levels towards replacing relations with increasingly more contentful ones. The movement or striving to achieve the highest contentfulness (as a single goal for all changing forms of reality) conditions the fiercest rivalry and competition between such moving and developing forms of reality. The inevitable competition and rivalry arising from this movement (movement toward a common finish line) manifests itself as confrontation, opposition and interaction of real objects, processes and phenomena at different stages, in different areas and at different levels of their existence-movement-development.

In geopolitics, this can be expressed by the confrontation and opposition of components in such relations, say, as {West/East}, {metropolis/colony}, {global/national}; in politics and economics — in relations {internationalism/nationalism}, {proletariat/bourgeoisie}, {wage labor/capital}, {liberalism/conservatism}; at the biological object level — in relations {male/female}, {man/woman}, {masculine principle/feminine principle}, {right side of the body [of living organisms]/left side of the body [of living organisms]}; in physics — by relations {time/space}, {energy/mass}, {wave/corpora}, {negative charge/positive charge}, {motion/rest}, {acceleration/gravity}, etc., etc. Being an entirely abstract construct, the relation {content/form} is fundamentally not manifestable in its most general form or image, but it nevertheless constitutes the core or very essence of any and every real concrete form (object, process, phenomenon, etc.). The noted duality of constituents-components of real forms-relations with a very strict and definite directionality of content-form transformations during the interaction of these constituents-components explains the moment-to-moment uniqueness of the emerging world (Universe, nature, life, etc.) as a whole, as well as the unique and unprecedented diversity of existing and nascent forms as individual elements of this world. The abstract relation {content/form} with quite unequivocal directionality of change-movement-development of its components or constituents, just like the entire relation as a whole, is something that exists originally, everywhere, objectively and independently of any particular concreteness; it is what pervades all existing and emerging concrete forms-levels-relations of surrounding reality due to its omnipresence. It is precisely the content-form transformations described above (i.e., changes of components and their relations in a strictly defined direction — towards achieving the highest contentfulness) that underlie the development of forms of reality, which are themselves only a concrete embodiment and incarnation of the model presented above (i.e., the abstract relation {content/form}). And it is exactly this initial model, owing to its foundational and universality, that is most acceptable and effective for deep study, research and penetration into the essence of the surrounding world (developing reality) at the current stage of its development. This investigative approach demonstrates its effectiveness through examples of successful studies of issues that until recently were considered highly problematic and "tricky" [5, 6]. Why do content-form relations suddenly come to the fore, become the core and subject of examination and study of dialectics? Because the very fact and the corresponding irrefutable assertion that there does not exist a single real object, process or phenomenon devoid of content and/or formal sides-constituents inevitably leads to the conclusion that content and form are those two "principles" (two main key components, constituents, "opponents", etc.) which,

interacting (opposing and simultaneously complementing each other) within the unified integral relation {content/form}, create ("generate") and reproduce socalled objective reality, i.e., the entire surrounding world (including ourselves) with all the diversity and uniqueness of its forms. According to the textbook definition, dialectics is "the teaching about the most general lawful connections and becoming, development of being and cognition, and the method of creatively cognizing thinking based on this teaching... The fundamental principles of dialectics, constituting its backbone, are universal connection, becoming and development, which are conceptualized using the entire historically established system of categories and laws" [15, p. 154]. The priority of the categories {content/form} over all others is obvious and confirmed by the circumstance that in the foundation of all other categories and concepts (just as of the actual objects, processes, phenomena of the physicalobjective level of reality denoted by these categories and concepts), one can easily discover ubiquitous content-form beginnings-constituents-components. It goes without saying and is undeniable that no one will ever be able to encompass (i.e., comprehend) the entire volume of connections and the total sum of changes occurring in all elements of the surrounding world (starting from photons, including the abyss of atoms, molecules, living cells, etc., and ending again with bizarre ideas and fantasies in the heads of individual human individuals), but grasping and covering the trend or patterns of such changes with human thought (from the standpoint of human thought or consciousness itself) still turns out to be possible. Here is what Lenin writes on this matter in his book “Materialism and Empiriocriticism”, referring to just a relatively small segment of reality — the global economy: "Each separate producer in the world economy is aware that he introduces a certain change in production technique, each owner is aware that he exchanges some products for others, but these producers and owners do not realize that they thereby alter social being. No 70 Marxs could have covered the sum of all these changes in all their ramifications in capitalist world economy. At best, the laws of these changes have been discovered, the main and basic objective logic of these changes and their historical development has been shown... From the fact that you live and manage your affairs, give birth to children and produce goods, exchange them, an objectively necessary chain of events emerges, a chain of development independent of your public consciousness, never fully comprehended by it. The highest task of humanity is to grasp this objective logic of economic evolution (evolution of social being) in general and basic terms in order to adapt one's public consciousness to it as clearly, distinctly and critically as possible..." [11, pp. 317–318]. Thus, the "objectively necessary chain of events" (including an objectively necessary change in forms of economic management) determines or sets the direction of change-movement-development-improvement of forms of human

public consciousness, generating a so-called algorithm for changing its (human consciousness) forms. The abstract forms of human consciousness (thought) arising or newly appearing in this connection grow out of more concrete forms by transforming their content to a new level of reality while preserving continuity and compatibility of contents of both — i.e., abstract and concrete — forms, which ultimately testifies to the contentfulness (objective contentfulness or truthfulness) of the entire chain or set of interconnected forms (from concrete to abstract ones) [5]. From the "objective logic of evolution of social being", there follow: both "...the highest task of humanity — to grasp this objective logic of economic evolution (evolution of social being) in general and basic terms..." (according to Lenin, see above); and, in a broader sense, the task to define and cognize the direction of development of reality and, accordingly, the goals and meanings of life and development of mankind as a contentful movement (and succession) of cognitive forms of reality towards possession of the highest contentfulness. Solving this last problem implies providing human thought with increasingly exhaustive and all-embracing methods of prospective, predictive, prognostic, anticipatory cognition. Efforts should be concentrated precisely in this direction — on cognition of patterns of change and emergence of forms of reality, on cognition of patterns of its (i.e., reality's) content-form transformations. It is here that the ideological justification of the essence (and existence) of man lies; it is by this that the perspective of life and development of all mankind as a whole is determined. And who, if not us — direct heirs and successors of the legendary bearers of priority ideas of the Great October — should continue to embody this revolutionary ideology into practice, into life !!! With the destruction and loss of the USSR, Russia lost the prospects of its further development previously outlined. Lenin and his comrades, being deeply enlightened and educated people, clearly understood what the formulation of goals and perspectives of movement-development meant for an individual person and for a nation as a whole. Without formulating a goal and prospect of development perceived and approved by the people, no initiatives, initiative, enthusiasm can exist on a "mass scale". To unite and organize the masses, public goals, directions and prospects of country (state, society) development must be formulated according to which a strategy for achieving these goals and prospects is developed. Moreover, defining a perspective should not boil down to an immediate reaction to external and internal so-called challenges and irritants but must be drawn up taking into account long-term forecasts and strategic plans. Why did Lenin and his like-minded associates understand this? Because the quality of those country's leaders was immeasurable compared to today's; they were people of a high level of consciousness and, respectively, personalities of quite another scale. Under the rule of today's voracious and insatiable "locusts", such "trivialities" are not mentioned. Furthermore, current "rulers", waving off public demands for ideology, do not even understand what is expected from them. Thus,

patriotism has somehow migrated into the realm of ideology and is presented to citizens of the country as an ideological prospect, although any reasonably educated person should realize that patriotism is not an ideology but a feeling instilled (or not instilled) in a person with mother's milk (and possibly already present in embryo at the genetic level), but requiring, as a rule, further cultivation and education. An idea, however, is something different from a feeling; it is a product of a completely different level. An idea is, speaking figuratively, the light at the end of the tunnel for every enlightened mind. A developed person cannot imagine himself without an idea and ideology. Feelings, sensations, needs, interests, "values" (in the meaning of this word used by the "Western" world) — these are all products of more "down-to-earth", though also more fundamental levels-components of the human "I", whereas an idea is a product of the highest of reliably known today levels of the latter — the level of human consciousness (thinking). An idea is based on feelings, continues them, but by no means reduces to them or is exhausted by them. An idea ignites a person from within and guides him through life like a guiding star; but not everyone, only one prepared, ripened enough to perceive this idea. Hence: you may not be a bearer of an idea, but you must be a patriot! — without any ideology, simply by right and by the feeling of one's belongingness and relatedness to a great people. Undoubtedly, of course, once an idea arises, it begins to influence the feeling in turn, developing and enriching it, from which spirituality of the human personality emerges, having the most direct connection with patriotism. But primordially in this tandem is still the feeling. If patriotism is mainly cultivated, then ideology, being a category of worldview, is formed on the basis of knowledge, i.e., through educational means. For example, the socialist idea, based on developed feelings of justice and patriotism, is already the beginning of a renewed ideology. Yesyes, renewed (emphasized specially for the current "rulers" and their ilk), built with consideration of previous blunders and lack of information, natural for the previous epoch. And there is no need to frighten the people or hope that everything will end again in levelling and stagnation: a ship reaches its destination not only and not so much because it was correctly oriented at the start of the journey, but mainly because its course is periodically corrected, clarified, changed according to circumstances. Improvement of wage systems and procedures of accounting and control, as well as turnover and renewal of personnel at all managerial levels from top to bottom — just as with the classics, but clearer, meticulous, thorougher, more energetic than before — will eventually produce the expected positive effect. Back then, there was no time for details; all attention was focused on creating foundations. And now it's not easier, but with a solid foundation underfoot, it is still simpler to timely determine the directions of relevant and vitally necessary changes.

Besides, today's civil society is different — older, more responsible, demanding, exacting... The people are not against the socialist idea per se, but against its ossified forms and against party functionaries of the past and present (including those sitting in parliamentary factions and supposedly opposing power), who parasitize on this idea in its deadened, dogmatic form, who have not changed since the hour when they betrayed this idea, burying Soviet power with its institutions (although the organs and structures of Soviet power needed to be improved and modified, as Lenin himself always pointed out — in each of his articles one can see how flexible, optimistic and "creative" he was in his aspiration toward updating and perfecting the forms of the revolutionary movement). The question or idea of building a renewed socialism meets the demand of society for a new ideology. At the same time, the course toward building a socialist society and a socialist way of life is no longer an end in itself, but merely one of the main conditions and means of ensuring further contentful development of man and mankind as a whole. However, it would be more correct in this case to speak not about the enthronement of some kind of renewed socialism, but about the manifestation and formation of another stage of the socialist movement or about the embodiment of the developing socialist idea through yet another of its forms. The absence of an ideological perspective "vacuums" human consciousness. At the same time, reliance on the old tried-and-tested religious idea is fruitless, since the latter slowly but surely fades away. Having played, without any exaggeration or doubt, a progressive role in its time, but unable to satisfactorily answer the increasingly acute and problematic worldview questions of young inquisitive minds, archaic god-centered doctrine will lose its relevance and naturally, without any violence over it, recede into the background, giving way to new worldview concepts (similarly, say, as horse-drawn carriages are still encountered on our roads, but the bulk of transportation is carried out by modern modes of transport). This trend is visible to the naked eye. Therefore, today's "rulers" should not beat around the bush. It's a pity for the youth: at the state level, "popovshchina" is being pushed through instead of focusing on literacy and education. With a balanced and effectively functioning system of upbringing and education, there will be no loss of spirituality accompanying the alleged decline of religion. Where does this categorical nonsense come from — that morality, ethics, humanism are allegedly always conditioned by a religious idea? The aforementioned categories and notions (from which the spiritual and moral bonds and guidelines of many human communities are "woven") arose and took root in human life as the transformation and improvement of forms-levels of human consciousness proceeded during man's development; one might think that Prince Svyatoslav, being a pagan, followed the moral instructions of god Yahweh (Jehovah, YHWH, etc., depending on reading) when he uttered his immortal: "Idu na vy" ["I go upon you"] or "Mertvye sramu ne imut" ["The dead know no shame"], — all this came from the

deep nature of man, from his contentful constituent, acquired through historically changing forms of human consciousness in the multi-layered, multilevel structure of man: both in the form of "spirit", and in the form of "soul", and in its later complex form — the human personality. To obsequiously cross oneself, relying on the supreme deity, requires little intelligence. It is certainly easier than taking full responsibility for the destinies of the country and mankind solely on the strength of one's own abilities and possibilities. For an individual preferring to dwell on platforms of religious concepts, the issue of responsibility is predetermined: fate and prospect will ultimately be decided for him by god with his prophets. There seems to be no dilemma worth a cracked egg: choose whomever you like — believe and rely on the omnipresent "uncle" or don't believe and rely on yourself. Nevertheless, finding oneself suddenly, even in shallow water, none of the believers stops struggling and fighting the wave, although, following their own logic, they could fully depend on the almighty supreme saviour... Any "ruler" should first learn, besides parish school, proper teaching of natural science (natural-scientific disciplines) in a Soviet university and not skip classes. Such a proven profile of the educational process undoubtedly contributed to acquiring a correct worldview orientation adequate to the level of cognition of developing reality achieved by human civilization. It is sometimes awkward to observe and listen to grown-up "underdevelopeds" with several (by their own words) "completed prestigious higher educations" discussing with a thoughtful air on some political-economic show or forum about "good and all good" in the guise of Christ or universal "evil and all bad" in the image of the devil. The advice in this case can only be: study Lenin's lessons of dialectics, miserable C students! However, for a more or less adequate understanding of the substance of multilevel reality, initially one should firmly grasp only one thing: EVERYTHING THAT exerts any influence on our level of reality (i.e., the level of human consciousness) and tries to correct (interfere with) our lives, claiming the status of Absolute, ITSELF occupies a subordinate position and is represented by equally imperfect and non-absolute form, likewise dependent on influences (impact) of forms of all other levels of reality. Absolute is impossible in principle! — and development of reality, never ceasing for a moment, continues as the movement of the contentful component beyond the limits of its forms ceaselessly and infinitely. As Absolute, human consciousness takes simple aggregate of forms of various kinds (including those accessible only to subjective imagination) levels of reality. Orienting on a model of reality's development based on representations of content-form transformations proceeding in the known direction, one clearly and unequivocally understands that man with his consciousness is not exhausted by subject-sensory-thought formation (i.e., three-dimensional physical-material level-form of existence in time and space of his flesh), but "extends" beyond the obvious boundaries of his bodily confines. The very process of development, the very developing multi-level reality (as motion and succession of forms of this reality) is even more inexhaustible and unlimited by the reached level-form of

man and mankind. However, in what transformed form the subsequent contentful movement continues with transition to the next (next after man) level of reality remains to be researched and studied. Still, even today we can say quite definitely regarding this matter that the form of clothing of the contentful principle on that next level of reality's movement differs in increased informative or energy (i.e., contentful) "capacity" and "capability" compared with preceding forms-levels, and therefore it is even more abstract than the preceding forms-levels. [When moving to the next level of contentfulness of forms (to the next level of development of reality), importance for any individual, as a certain correlation of components in the relation {content/form}, will have only what — with what intellectual-spiritual (contentful) developments the transformational contentful constituent (principle) of this individual transforms onto that level. Since that next level is "abstracted" (abstract, abstracted) to a significantly greater degree than the preceding ones, it goes without saying that neither yachts nor mansions with Mercedes cars can be dragged along there. What matters will be the accumulated during the "earthly" cycle spiritualintellectual potential and experience of dealing with abstraction, whereas yachts and Mercedes cars — as well as all other material tools — are merely auxiliary auxiliary means expanding the framework of formal framing of the developing contentful constituent for further disclosure (development) and multiplication of this very potential. Hence, it is important to properly dispose of these means during the period of "earthly" life. This explanation is addressed primarily to those who want to see further than the tip of their nose. Ultimately, the ability to identify, generate and use ideally abstract forms of a new level, maximally filled with objective content, i.e., true forms (see article "Universal Criterion of Truth" in this collection)]. Ego-centric and poly-centric models (pictures) of the world (including: theo-, socio-, anthropo-centric models) are receding into the past. They are being replaced by a concept of a single universal internal principle of selfdevelopment: all forms-structures of reality change and develop under the management (i.e., under the auspices) of a universal "mechanism", algorithm, inner principle immanent to all form-structures of reality regardless of their level, stage (place and time) of development. This is a model (concept) of contentform transformations in the direction of achieving the highest contentfulness by any and every developing form of reality. To understand and anticipate the change and development of real forms, to look as far ahead as possible "around the corner", "beyond the horizon" — this is the main task that will stand before man and which man will be forced to solve quite consciously (thoughtfully) both in the near and distant future for the sake of preserving himself as a species and surviving in competitive struggle with other forms-levels of reality. Without awareness of this circumstance, man will not be able to fully confront so-called challenges from nature (the surrounding world). Humanity is painfully parting with the aforementioned models, theories and pictures of the past; hence there is a new energetic surge of religious, ethnic,

national, racial, civilizational and other similar conflicts and wars. A worldview crisis of faiths, ideologies, all kinds of "values", priorities and exclusivities (the notions of exclusivity and absolute form lose their former significance and informational value, receding into the background and making room for concepts of equal uniqueness and irreplaceability of forms) gives way to another worldview model [2, 3, 5, 7, 18]. Previous orthodox preferences require clarification, supplementation and replacement by new more contentful models and forms. However, the task of finding balance and correct correlation between interacting constituents in the relation {content/form} at present and identifying the trend of change of this balance — for the future — remains as relevant as ever. In accordance with the universal principle of self-development (see above), multilevel change, movement, development of forms takes place (in contrast to the principle and mechanism of "functioning" of various centeric models) by "piling up" (i.e., developing) their content, "making from oneself". With the emergence and establishment of a new concept of the development of the surrounding world (replacing losing meaning and strength God-centered theories), inevitable earlier wars and rivalry of religious ideologies will recede into the past. What was the contentfulness of that supposedly over-ideologized Soviet era? What inspired, motivated the Soviet person to move forward? The romance of everyday life was built on the dictate of the heart, energy of impulse, fascination with breaking through to the new and unknown; proceeding from the need of the soul, man was ready to discover what had not been discovered, comprehend what had not been comprehended, conquer nature, space, taiga, virgin lands, peaceful atom, etc., etc. It was about unleashing and liberating man's inner forces and creative energy in the process of constructive labor for the sake of achieving common goals and ideals (including well-being, but by no means in its hypertrophied phase clothed in the guise of the golden calf with prosaic accumulations). By refusing the elevated approach to setting, formulating and achieving socially demanded ambitious goals, we "throw out the baby with the bathwater". From the very first days of Bolshevik Soviet power, the issues of actualizing (awakening) the energy of impulse, enthusiasm and initiative of the masses arose in full force. The solution of both highly complex and simple everyday problems implied and required not only routine strain of physical effort but also activation of cognitive aspirations of man, inciting him to satisfy his innate human curiosity. One should primarily learn contentful thinking from Lenin. Lenin fully realized that human efforts ultimately aim to forecast, anticipate, predict with increasing accuracy the perspective of development of real events, build sound plans and correctly set priorities in their implementation, which meant — learning to look further and more successfully "beyond the corner", "beyond the horizon" (i.e., looking into the future).

Two paths are known and possible for increasing, accumulating and multiplying the own contentfulness (volume or fullness of content of one's own form): 1. Appropriation of someone else's content ("foreign" volumes, accumulations of content belonging to other forms); as a particular case of this variant — simple destruction (destruction, liquidation, neutralization) of an "adversarial" form that impedes or hinders the movement-development and enrichment of content of one's own form. 2. Cooperation (symbiosis) with other forms and joint accumulation, increase of content of each of the interacting and cooperating forms. Needless to say, combinations of these two variants are significant. Applied to the development of human communities, the essence of one and the other path boils down to the following. The first path is the path of war, violence, coercion, applied, for example (at the level of states and peoples), by metropolises toward their colonies (subjugation by metropolises of the will, appropriation of natural resources and fruits of labour of colonial peoples and nations). To date, humanity has mastered and refined precisely this path. The second path, the socialist path of development with indispensable attributes of cooperation among people and achievement of goals jointly, is still in embryonic state; it was exactly this method of socialist construction and creation of a new type of public order on a mass scale that V.I. Lenin advocated. It is well-known (and confirmed beyond doubt by processes taking place during the not-so-distant times of the breakup of the USSR) that initial capital accumulation proceeds, as a rule, by violent and fraudulent means — by appropriation and seizure. We have all witnessed how the redistribution of public and state property, capitals and assets took place in post-perestroika Russia and other republics of the USSR that suddenly became "independent". This was a path of deceit, swindling, gangster feuds, mockery of privatization coupons and pledge auctions. The methods of executing this grand-scale fraud were, without any exaggeration, predatory, and they relate entirely to the first path (variant) of increasing contentfulness (i.e., enrichment) of the most active individual reformers (young and not so young). Undoubtedly, reverse expropriation of property and assets acquired in such a manner also implies, to some extent, responsive violence at the initial stage of building socialism. The retaliatory move is inevitably connected with redistribution of once criminally appropriated or wrongly amassed "bourgeois" property and financial assets, but this procedure is carried out in the interests of the overwhelming majority of working population (citizens of the country) and for the sake of restoring historical justice.

As regards the legality of expropriation of property and capitals during times of socialist revolutions, the procedure is performed, as already mentioned, in the interests of the vast majority and is single-time (within a limited-short timeframe); and whoever thinks that such procedures can be dispensed with at periodic revolutionary iterations is deeply mistaken or simply ill-informed regarding the inevitability of substantial transformation of current foundations into the configuration of future public and governmental structure. This stage is necessary, in particular, for redistributing initial capital in the interests of the majority of the population in order to create normal starting conditions (positions) for ordinary workers and to restore a justified balance with the positions of aggressively pushy and shameless (for the most part) minority that once usurped assets not belonging to them. Yes, development proceeds, and history is written "by tacks", zigzags — every time returning to itself at a new level by segments of spiral turns that never fully line up and are never perfectly finished. Such is the external appearance and character of the dialectical mode of development of anything whatsoever. Somewhere again forced measures of expropriation will be needed, because otherwise it just won't work: nobody ever voluntarily surrenders what was gained in the sweat of others' brows and appropriated in the sweat of one's own brow. One must be quite naive and unschooled in practical life not to understand this. In contrast to the first path, implementing the second option for increasing the contentfulness of real forms of public order means immersion into socialism, toward which man is increasingly pushed by emerging circumstances regardless of his consciousness; and we will have to revive and continue this path whether we like it or not because further contentful development of mankind outside of this path is unprofitable, suboptimal, inefficient, "dead-end" and therefore unlikely. And deeper study of socialist theory will be required. And again, one will have to acknowledge the fairness and scientific nature of Marx's approach applied in working out social and economic theories (otherwise, gentlemen courtiersapologists of today's socio-economic foundations, you will simply lose your target "fan" audiences and, as a consequence, your piece of bread; then there will come time to laugh good-naturedly at those who arrogantly mocked the orthodoxy of Marxism and indulged in primitivism of scientific communism — well, sirs, he laughs best who laughs last). Following Lenin's methods and research tools, we can state today that thanks to applying the dialectical approach or method of cognition, much of what is called world order becomes clearer than before. At the same time, the stillrelevant task does not diminish but rather stands out even more clearly: to keep learning how to see the future picture more accurately (i.e., learn to look ahead "around the corner" with ever greater efficiency) so as to understand ourselves and surrounding reality better.

Solving this problem is identical to solving the eternal issue of finding meaning in life for every individual person, which (solution), in its first approximation, takes on the following form: everything existing moves in a single direction towards expressing objective truth in its most contentful, ultimate form — i.e., a form endowed with the highest (objective) contentfulness or the form of so-called "absolute" truth. This path undoubtedly belongs not only to any separate individual but also to whole peoples and states. Knowing this, we must and should lead this movement (process) since we are currently the best informed about it and understand it best. Following the course towards achieving the highest contentfulness by forms of developing reality (including humanity) is also a movement towards effective resolution of the perennially troubling human question regarding the possibility of cognizing and cognition of objective truth. What is life, motion, development, progress, etc., in general terms is already understood: it is a process of contentful filling of forms while striving for them (at all levels without exception) to possess the highest (objective) contentfulness. It is a process of "unleashing" the inner content of reality and presenting it outwardly in the most contentful form possible at any given moment, approaching each time the form of exhaustive expression of objective truth. With small acts of subjective human movements (whether achievements in painting, literature, music, sports, science, etc.) humankind approaches closer to the fullest expression of the objective content of developing reality (expression of objective truth) with each such elementary act. Every sound, every note produced by a skilled musician or composer seeks to bring to light and reveal to the world objective truth — i.e., the most contentful form of representation of reality at that particular moment (from the viewpoint of the maestro). The same applies to poetic word or brushstroke on an artist's canvas, let alone the contentful theories and concepts of a scientist. From the most contentful subjective opinions and views on the world, objective truth is formed [5]. But understanding and awareness of the general trend of real processes by no means exhausts the solution of all current issues: the crucial and essential question for man remains open forever — the question of anticipating, foreseeing, preempting, predicting with maximum accuracy and certainty changes (variability) of existing forms of reality and emergence (appearance) of new ones, and taking into account this circumstance — the question of planning subsequent human activity. Well, and since man by his very nature and place in the chain of development is a highly active linkcomponent (in other words — subject of activity), he inevitably interferes with the development of reality and embodies thereby his own vision, understanding and "plans", impacting the surrounding world and changing it in the direction required by him (strictly in accordance with the aforementioned universal principle of self-development). Despite our ability to define the meaning of life and existence both for man and his entire environment today (see above), the matter still lies in learning

to anticipate the change of existing forms of reality and the appearance of new ones, in order to predict events and developments as precisely as possible, to learn to peer deeper into the future — and not just to satisfy immediate curiosity, but mainly to survey and outline distant prospects. Development is the succession of forms of reality aimed at creating and establishing forms filled with higher objective content. All ongoing processes in the surrounding world are "targeted" at enhancing the contentfulness of existing forms representing changing reality. In this lies the essence of the orientation of transformation of nature in the universal cosmic dimension. The search for, acquisition, comprehension of truth, i.e., discovering the most contentful forms of representation or acquiring developing objective content, permeates all of nature — from living thinking beings (with their specific subforms in the guise of ideal abstract constructs of thought) to an inanimate stone-boulder, and any molecule, atom, electron, photon (quantum) within this stone or beyond it. And no empty speculations on this score, embodying former "highbrow" (sophistical, demagogic, speculative, scholastic, etc.) philosophy, are probably needed anymore, since they turn out to be low-contentful and therefore fruitless. Philosophy gradually changes its status and role, turning from a habitual tool of passively descriptive and explanatory type into an instrument of actively prognostic, predictive and transformative character. Its speculative nature, conditioned by centuries-old intrigues or games of idle human mind, finally turns itself into an uninteresting useless "thingie", a whimsically intricate relic of the past. Dialectics comes forward as the core and embodiment of a new, deeper philosophy, as the art of generating and bringing to life correct forecasts and planned transformations conceived by man. And here our updated philosophy meets its inevitable merger and fusion with science, as was excessively discussed throughout the 20th century. Summarizing the above in two words, we have every reason to draw the following conclusion: to learn to understand and know how to see the perspective of development of reality, i.e., the perspective (orientation) of change of its (reality's) existing forms and emergence (appearance) of new ones, and on this basis plan subsequent human activity — this is the main task facing the man of the future, worthy of investing any resources for its gradual step-by-step resolution. The socialist idea is closest, most responsive to the very spirit of the Russian person, whose essence since time immemorial has been considered an inexhaustible craving (aspiration) for justice. Truly popular can only be the power that professes exactly this idea; when a working person can feel with every cell of his that this is his native people's power — then he will defend it, and cherish it, and nurture it, and improve the institutions of power, and invest with enthusiasm his strength and energy into "arranging" life. But if we have given the land, its subsoil, national wealth and means of production into the

hands of "scoundrels", allowed them to fence themselves off from the people with high fences and armed security regarding all this primordially people's property — then where can unity for the sake of a common goal come from — no matter how you "blur" the eyes of your fellow citizens with pharisaic slogans about the supposedly "national property". The craving for justice inherent in every Russian person and, as a derivative of this property of the soul, the aspiration to save one's neighbor are conditioned by his natural inclination to search for and find balance (equilibrium) both inside and on the outer contour of society in opposition to encroachments (aggression) on freedom and dignity both at the level of an individual person and at the level of human communities. The social orientation of the state or public structure in this case should be determined by the officially proclaimed policy of the state, not by charity, assistance, petty handouts, voluntary donations or patronage timidly pushed through from time to time and on the sly. Of course, a state of this type must be built on principles other than the existing ones. It is necessary to return to the working person the feeling of involvement in a great cause, a great people, a great country with its innumerable natural resources and national wealth; return the feeling of involvement in a great common goal, most effectively approached by joint efforts using socialized means of production. When there is such a feeling, which in turn revives and nourishes feelings of human pride and dignity, — then a person is ready to give himself entirely to the common cause, which inevitably becomes the personal cause of everyone. We must return to our people the lost sense of selfworth inherent in the Soviet man. Does the first tentative approach to mastering the socialist idea mean the complete collapse of Marxism, Leninism, theories of socialism and scientific communism, etc., as primitive and short-sighted politicians, political scientists, philosophers, economists believe? Of course not! Humanity will return to such fundamental currents and theories more than once, approaching them in a spiral-cyclical way (or by successive approximations) — in full accordance with the nature and character of the content-form (dialectical) movementdevelopment of reality — to an increasingly exhaustive version of their implementation. And something here suggests that the point of view of the wise classics of Marxism is not devoid of grounds and will, in the end, prevail in the structure of searching for the optimal path of mankind's development, since it most fully corresponds to the inner content of man, his mood, oriented towards preserving human identity, culture, traditions, dignity (accordingly to genetic predisposition, genetic matrix, genetic code and the so-called call of ancestors and blood). According to the formulation of the principle of self-development, everything existing and developing periodically returns "to its own circle", but in other, more contentful and enriched forms.

Dialectics is the art of finding balance of interacting sides, which (art) Lenin possessed impeccably and perfectly. For Lenin — it is always a result-oriented search for the optimal correlation between the most essential sides-constituentscomponents of development (in their relation): in the range, for example, from de-kulakization and surplus-appropriation to the introduction of a food tax and NEP, or from asserting total collective ownership of the means of production to establishing and recognizing as relevant a mode of production with elements of private property, etc. In this regard, first of all, his ability to find and highlight the most essential sides-constituents of reality, being in direct interaction and relation to each other (i.e., in live confrontation and opposition) precisely at this particular segment of their development, and already on this basis — to demonstrate the ability to forecast the trend of change in the correlation of identified sides, and along with this — to anticipate the further course and perspective of development of the entire relation as a whole. And it looks absolutely fantastic to be able to identify sources of renewal (inflow, increase, receipt) of Contentful energy in pairs of interacting components and further — to anticipate and track the bias of content-form transformations (transmutations) of these components and their relations as a whole. It is on considering issues similar to those touched upon above that we will focus, studying and analyzing the works of V.I. Lenin from the standpoint of their being imbued with the spirit of dialectics. Any article or work of Lenin is a dynamic creation, structured according to a universal scheme corresponding to the dialectical nature of the development of reality itself (i.e., according to the scheme of tracking the change-movement of interacting forms towards achieving their highest contentfulness): the variability of the correlation of two interacting sides-components in their relation is investigated with subsequent forecasting of the development of the result of this interaction towards (into the area) of predominant increase in the contentfulness of the active component and the relation as a whole. Compositionally and structurally it looks something like this. o

o

o o

Description and analysis of the current situation, i.e., description of the set and alignment of interacting forces-factors-constituents-components (regardless of whether it concerns the reorganization of the Rabkrin, the situation at the front, the organization of socialist competition, etc., etc.). Identification of directly interacting forces-factors-constituentscomponents and their "combination" into the main relation (at this step, the separation of the essential from the non-essential is actually performed). Identification and highlighting of the active (leading) and passive (driven) components within the established relation. Assessment of the established correlation of the mentioned components in the relation composed of them and determination of the vector (orientation) of probable change-movement-development of both individual components and the integral relation.

o

Forecasting of event development: on the basis of the assessment made, the change of existing forms and the emergence (appearance) of new forms of reality is anticipated (predicted). Thus, every time after analyzing the established state of affairs and alignment of forces, Lenin determines, firstly, with specific further steps towards achieving immediate goals and, secondly, with an assessment of the strategic perspective of the development of the situation as a whole. That is why each such article turns out to be not just a beautiful dialectical twist (decorative icon), but a guide to action; each of his articles contains a description of a way out of the current situation and a transition (possibly with a leap) to a new turn-level of development of interacting elements, and in each article without exception the author offers his original and effective solution to the problem under consideration. All of Lenin's forecasts are made on the basis of correctly composed relations from interacting sides-components of reality and also based on anticipating the trend or orientation of subsequent contentful filling of these relations. At the same time, the more accurately the sides-components directly opposing, confronting and interacting with each other are identified, the more correct and reliable the predictions of further development of events and situations turn out to be. Of course, conditions, circumstances and forms interacting in the present differ from those of the past, but the principle (algorithm, "mechanism") determining the development of reality is invariant over time; that is why the proposed scheme or methodology of effective research into the surrounding world resembles the one presented above. Below we will analyze all this using examples of well-known theoretical works by V.I. Lenin relating to three different periods of his life: pre-revolutionary, immediate pre-revolutionary and post-revolutionary. [We should only additionally note that square brackets scattered throughout the text contain references to literary sources (as is usually done), as well as notes, explanations and comments (including comments on comments) by the author of this article]. § 1. Works of the pre-revolutionary period We will examine the dialectical background of Lenin's works from the prerevolutionary period (which began a dozen and a half years before the Great October Socialist Revolution) using such Leninist works as “What Is to Be Done?”, “One Step Forward, Two Steps Back”, “The Beginning of the Revolution in Russia”, “Marxism and Revisionism” and “On the Road”, united by a single internal logic and canvas of corresponding historical events. 1. In his book “What Is to Be Done? Burning Questions of Our Movement” [8, pp. 80–236], published in March 1902, Lenin presented an overview of the initial stage of the revolutionary movement in Russia. The book was written during the

acute struggle for the creation of a revolutionary Marxist party of the Russian working class and played an outstanding role in the formation of the party. In this work, Lenin identifies three stages and accordingly — three periods in the development of the revolutionary movement in Russia. He describes the first stage of 1884–1894 as the embryonic development of social democracy carried out separately from the labor movement. Regarding the second stage of 1894–1898: "social-democracy appears on earth as a public movement, as an upsurge of popular masses, as a political party" [8, p. 227]. Alongside and instead of "going into the people", there began a flow of intelligentsia toward workers (as the most active force-component of the future revolutionary movement) and widespread fascination among workers with strikes. However, this same strike struggle for the sake of increasing its own efficiency forced them to learn, read illegal literature, as well as diligently engage with issues of legal populism without forgetting at the same time about Marxist theory and the task of overthrowing autocracy. "The establishment of the party in the spring of 1898 was the most vivid and at the same time last act of the Social Democrats of this stripe" [8, p. 228]. The third stage finally replaces the second one starting in 1898. It is a period of confusion, vacillation, disintegration. The leaders of the movement were afraid to move forward (to political struggle), yielding to the strength of the spontaneous upsurge of the popular masses. The slogan "class struggle" did not push towards ever broader, more energetic activity but served as a means of appeasement: after all, everyone knows that "economic struggle is inextricably linked with political". The idea of the party did not serve as a call to create a combat organization of revolutionaries but justified some kind of "revolutionary bureaucracy", childish playing with "democratic" forms. And here, in this connection, the author, like a true scholar drawing conclusions only based on facts (without fabrications or fantasies), having difficulty saying when the third stage will end and the fourth begin, nevertheless expresses firm confidence that the fourth stage will lead to strengthening militant Marxism; that the rear-guard of conciliators and deserters will be replaced by the actual vanguard of the most revolutionary class (proletariat), which will manage to overcome uncertainty, hesitation and lack of confidence in their own forces. And then (in terms of calling for such a change and summarizing what has been said), it becomes possible to give a concise and exhaustive answer to the question "what is to be done?" — liquidate the third stage (period of confusion, vacillations, hesitations and lack of confidence in one's own forces) [8, p. 229]. [Thus, Lenin outlined here the objectively occurring successive replacement of stages (forms) of substantive revolutionary movement expressed qualitatively and quantitatively through changes in the correlation between the contentful and formal components in the known relation {proletariat/bourgeoisie}. The correlation of components (within the specified relation) changes with the growth in share and importance of the active revolutionary component (proletariat) due

to increased contentfulness conditioned by the influence of the following accompanying factors: emergence of social democracy => becoming established of social democracy => unification of social democratic formations on the basis of the idea of creating a Marxist proletarian party => establishment of the Bolshevik Party of the Russian working class as its most revolutionary vanguard. This entire chain of transforming factors directly affected the numerator of the aforementioned relation (i.e., the "proletariat" component), raising its contentfulness within the whole relation. In other words, Vladimir Ilyich, with impartiality of a scientist, stated that for the period from 1884 to 1898 and further (later) there took place and continues objective movement (transformation, transmutation) of the contentful component of the mentioned relation towards increasing its contentfulness while simultaneously upsetting equilibrium in the relation {proletariat/bourgeoisie} in favor of strengthening and predominance of influence of more contentful active proletarian forms of movement over more conservative bourgeois ones. An innate ability and predisposition for dialectical thinking allowed Vladimir Ilyich to break down step-by-step the phase-wise and periodic nature of the revolutionary struggle, identify the active and passive forces-components of the revolutionary movement in Russia of that time, trace their genesis and determine measures to activate and strengthen the contentful proletarian component (which meant overcoming uncertainty, hesitation and lack of confidence inherent in socialist-democratic circles and organizations "nourishing" the contentfulness of the "proletariat" component). There is also a source of replenishment of the contentfulness of the "proletariat" component, which at that time consisted of the educational standard of the bourgeois intelligentsia]. 2. The continuation of the struggle for cleansing the revolutionary movement and its vanguard (the Marxist party) from its wavering members (random elements) is described very vividly by Lenin in the book “One Step Forward, Two Steps Back (Crisis in Our Party)” [8, pp. 237–421]. The book resulted from Lenin's thorough study and analysis of numerous protocols of meetings and resolutions of the II Congress of the RSDLP and was released in May 1904. In this work, Vladimir Ilyich shows how something big, main and essential eventually emerges and takes shape from small things (seemingly insignificant matters, squabbles, disputes, discussions and quarrels serving as signs of fierce clash of opinions and different points of view). The art of a great dialectician manifested itself in this case in recognizing this essential thing — the role and significance of organizational issues in the process of forming the core of the revolutionary movement (i.e., the Bolshevik Party), as well as timely demarcation from conciliators and wavering elements regarding principal questions, attracting to one's side by polemic and persuasion the majority of potential sympathizers. As a result of this struggle, the idea emerged of establishing and organizing a monolithic nucleus of the labor movement — the Bolshevik Party — in opposition to amateurishness and circle-mindedness characteristic of the social-democratic movement. Without consolidation around a single center, without subordination to decisions of this center (that is, without

iron discipline), the future conquests of the Great October would have simply been unthinkable and impossible. Precisely from the standpoint of the dialectical approach (from beginning to end), Lenin conducts his painstaking analysis of the accompanying events of this struggle. For the proletariat (and any public association alike) there is no other weapon in the struggle for power except organization (organized effort) — writes Lenin. The proletariat can become an invincible force only because the ideological unity of its theoretical principles (first of all, Marxist theory) is secured by material unity of organization uniting millions of workers into a single army (of the working class). Before this army, the decrepit power of international capital will not stand. This army will draw closer and tighter ranks despite any zigzags and steps backward [8, p. 413]. In the course of detailed study of the background of this issue, Lenin draws attention to the fact that every development indeed proceeds along a dialectical path of resolving emerging contradictions: minority becomes majority, majority becomes minority, each side moves from defense to attack and from attack to defense; the original point of ideological struggle is "negated", but then begins "negation of negation" and the struggling sides return to the original point of purely ideological struggle, but now this "thesis" is enriched by all the results of the "antithesis" and turns into a higher "synthesis"; when an isolated random error has grown into a quasi-system of conciliatory views on the organizational issue, and the erroneousness of such an approach becomes increasingly apparent. "In short, not only oats grow according to Hegel, but Russian socialdemocrats fight among themselves too according to Hegel," concludes Vladimir Ilyich [8, p. 410]. [It is extremely important to note that the dialectical character of formulating various conclusions and findings through "piling up" and layering of minor questions and discussion results upon each other or through "returning to oneself at a new level" is recorded by the author as self-evident. Of course, for us living today, concepts and terms like "proletariat", "bourgeoisie", "classes", "class struggle", etc., seem antiquated and exotic. At the same time, forgetting the terminology of the past does not mean liberation from the elements of developing reality denoted by it. After all, the class structure of society still persists, whatever anyone says: on the one hand — hired labor, on the other — financial-industrial capital (generally speaking, "oligarchy"). And only the most shameless liar risks claiming that everything in such a public arrangement is "even". That is why the meaning of the phrase "class struggle" nowadays differs little from the content of this terminology in the examined pre-revolutionary times, and the concept of "class struggle" is unlikely to disappear until the notion of "classes" fades away. Therefore, even today it remains important (and perhaps even more so) to be able to find interacting components (competing, conflicting, confronting sides) and correctly compose relevant content-form relations (of the type {content/form}) in order to properly define directions of variability both of the components themselves and of their relations as a whole (i.e., of social systems and structures), distinguish and

separate the essential from the secondary (less significant) and thereby predict changes in existing forms and the appearance of new ones. All this, undoubtedly, may assist in devising the most effective forms of class struggle, etc. Applying to today's situation, the terms in the relation {proletariat/bourgeoisie} can be replaced by terms: {hired labor/capital}, {class of non-propertied/class of owners of means of production}, etc., without loss of meaning. Our current capitalists and bourgeois are "blown-up", random, staged characters whose state and public property on the means of production fell into their private hands as a result of unprecedented manipulations and machinations, fraud and raiding (people's memories are still fresh enough about how the initial capital was acquired and accumulated during the uncontrolled descent of the collapsing country into the quagmire of wild capitalism) — and therefore they receive no respect, reverence or trust from the people or representatives of hired labor. It is enough to look more closely to see the same standard scheme serving as the framework for the overwhelming majority of public (socio-economic) formations: hired labor on one side and capital on the other. The "court" philosophers, economists and political scientists (just like in Lenin's prerevolutionary times, servicing similar power structures and institutions, feeding from their hands, and imposing an opinion about the accomplished reconciliation of possessing and non-possessing counterparts) invented (no doubt, out of thin air) some kind of "middle class"; although any schoolchild knows that there existed before and still exist today two main classes differing by their relation to ownership of means of production, with a bourgeois stratum of small commodity producers cushioning this entire "construction" between them. The reader probably understands that at the current stage of social development, the arsenal of tools for production and reproduction has significantly expanded and, besides traditional ones, now includes land, dams, roads, overpasses, vehicles, communication facilities, computers, printing and information dissemination media, instruments for managing financial flows, various robotic complexes, systems with artificial intelligence, etc., etc. Apologists of homogeneity and uniformity of modern social arrangements could be advised to pay attention to how the current coronacrisis instantly stripped off the lacquered drapery and glittering tinsel from the appearance of the vast majority of representatives of the so-called "middle class" (for example, in the USA), leaving behind only debt credit obligations and nothing resembling substantial property (in the form of means of production, capitals, financial assets, fixed and working capital, etc.). In any conflict, war or internal catastrophe manifested externally, its inner core ultimately turns out to be a dispute and confrontation of Contentful and formal constituents. And what these constituents are clothed in (what forms they take) — this becomes the subject of skillful analysis and recognition; here one must be able to correctly formulate (compose) a relation from interacting components: whether it is {hired labor/capital}, or {class of dispossessed/class of owners of means of production}, or {liberals/conservatives}, or {productive forces/production relations}, or

{public character of production/private-capitalist mode of appropriation}, or {transnational (global)/national economy} and so on. Carrying out further analysis of variability, say, of the last relation listed above, one cannot fail to notice that at today's stage of development the component "national" represents a fairly stable side of the relation {transnational/global/national}; hence the noticeable and strong resistance (opposition) of this component relative to its opposite — the component "transnational". However, subsequently the correlation of components in this relation will inevitably change due to increased efficiency (i.e., contentfulness) of the numerator component, and trans-national will prevail over nationally-state just as the public (socialized) will find itself in a better position compared with private (privatized) in the relation {public/private}, which will correspond to the law-governed increase in contentfulness (efficiency) of these form-relations. Cost reduction, market expansion and other optimization characteristic of transnational (and in perspective — socialized) economy to a greater extent than national-state economy will lead to growth in contentfulness both of the numerator component of the considered relation and of the whole relation. In such connection, it is fascinatingly interesting to follow the intricate flow of Lenin's thought flowing from less contentful preceding conceptual construction into a more contentful and capacious conceptual formation, and to see that the spirit and manner of thinking of the great dialectician is akin to the process of contentful layering, similar to rolling up a "snowball", and remains essentially unchanged from article to article. Compositionally, this looks exactly like this: description of the initial situation and existing state of affairs; statement of alignment of forces; identification of objective prerequisites and trend towards changing the correlation of forces and the overall situation; prognostic description of prospective or succeeding new form-relation (or system of forms) and ways of its emergence]. 3. The article "The Beginning of the Revolution in Russia" [8, pp. 422– 424] was written by Lenin in January 1905 — almost immediately after the events of Bloody Sunday in St. Petersburg. Judging by the fact that the spring was slowly compressing ("the tsarist government deliberately gave relatively unhindered development to strike movements and the beginnings of broad demonstrations"), it was not difficult to assume that the government acted with the intention of driving things toward the use of military force [8, p. 422]. As a result: thousands killed and wounded. The Rubicon has been crossed: the compressed-to-the-limit spring had to release its accumulated energy — the proletariat rose up and began to arm itself. And immediately the entire palette of revolutionary appeals, slogans and actions changes. The rebellious Petrograd workers put forward demands for immediate convocation of a constituent assembly based on universal, direct, equal and secret suffrage, as well as for the immediate overthrow of the government; at the same time, "arming the people becomes one of the nearest tasks of the revolutionary moment." Events develop with astonishing speed: "the revolutionary education of the proletariat advanced in one day

more than it could have done in months and years of gray, humdrum, downtrodden life". Immediate arming of workers and all citizens generally (it was recognized and pointed out that revolutionaries of bourgeois democracy could be used for the overthrow of Tsarism), "preparation and organization of revolutionary forces for the destruction of governmental authorities and institutions — this is the practical basis upon which all and any revolutionaries can and should combine for a common blow" [8, p. 424]. [It should be noted here that there are no Councils of Workers', Soldiers' or Peasants' Deputies yet on the forefront of the growing struggle. The situation develops exclusively within the framework of the relation composed ("formed") of directly interacting components {proletariat/autocracy}: on the one side — the working class under the wing of the Social-Democratic Party; on the other side — autocracy with its coercive institutions]. And no matter how the present uprising ends in St. Petersburg itself, it will inevitably and necessarily become the first step toward a much broader, more conscious, better-prepared uprising. The government may manage to postpone the hour of reckoning, but postponement will only make the next step of the revolutionary onslaught even grander [8, p. 423]. [This example clearly demonstrates how V.I. Lenin masterfully formulated and "calibrated" the previously reproduced dialectical scheme: precise identification and definition of interacting constituents-components in their relation {organizing proletariat/institutions of autocratic power}; identifying the active component in this relation; determining the direction of variability (movement, development) of the active component and the whole relation; forecasting the emergence, accordingly, of new contentful forms as a result of this change-movement. Human thought always tends towards representing (expressing) its most perfect form of maximum content volume. Structurally-compositionally, the same scheme looks like this: description of the established situation in conditions of disequilibrium; identification of causes and backgrounds contributing to disruption of situational equilibrium; description of the process of situation development while moving it towards a more equilibrated, more balanced state; description of the new state of affairs and newly emerging situation replacing the existing one due to inevitable ongoing changes]. 4. The article "Marxism and Revisionism" [8, pp. 36–43] was written in March-April 1908. Describing the initial starting position, Lenin draws attention to the alignment of forces (i.e., lists interacting components) in the course of the struggle unfolding in the theoretical sphere. In this sphere, newly emerged natural-historical theories in the mid-19th century, touching old theological prejudices, caused and continue to cause the fiercest opposition and struggle [8, p. 36]. Bourgeois scientific thought and philosophy do not want to hear anything about Marxism, declaring it refuted and destroyed [how wonderfully "fresh" and

recognizable is such an attitude toward Marxism even today!]. The increasing role of Marxism and consolidation of its ideas among the working class inevitably provokes sharpening bourgeois attacks against Marxism, which after each "destruction" by official science becomes stronger, tougher and livelier. Having defeated and outplayed many hostile external currents over the past fifty years, today Marxism faces opposing currents requiring its correction within Marxism itself. Pre-Marxian socialism remains strong in Russia and sometimes revives. Generally speaking, pre-Marxian socialism has been broken, but it continues fighting not on its own independent ground but on common Marxist ground — as revisionism [trying to legalize and prove viability of a mixture of "a bulldog with a rhinoceros"]. Essentially, however, all this petty-bourgeois revision of Marxism (revision of small shopkeepers) always boils down to negation or diminution of the factor of class struggle and channeling class struggle into the stream of "class cooperation" [8, pp. 38–39]. What accounts for the inevitability of periodic revival of revisionism and its roots under capitalism? It lies in the fact that in any capitalist country there are always broad layers of petty bourgeoisie, small proprietors alongside the proletariat. Capitalism was born and constantly reborn (regenerated) from small production [8, p. 42]! A whole series of "middle strata" [by analogy with some "middle class", using modern language] is inevitably created again and again by capitalism due to its inner necessity (services of individual craftsmen, homebased work, small workshops serving large industry, etc.). When they go bankrupt, these small producers are inevitably thrown back into the ranks of the proletariat, "diluting" their views and beliefs with petty-bourgeois worldview, their ideology (which, apparently, requires constant work on retraining and reeducation). This is completely natural and will be so for a long time while antagonistic classes exist. And ideological struggle of revolutionary Marxism with revisionism is merely the prelude to great revolutionary battles of the proletariat until complete victory of its cause despite all vacillations and weaknesses of the petit bourgeoisie [8, p. 43]. Bypassing superficial phenomena (effects), Lenin constantly brings to light the most essential, profound sides-components consisting in actual relations of interaction and confrontation. Thus, contrary to the widespread opinion among revisionists, he states that growing parliamentarism and education of the proletariat do not weaken crises and eliminate revolutions but, on the contrary, contribute to intensifying civil war during these crises and revolutions [8, p. 41]. They (parliamentarism and education) help enlighten and organize ever larger masses of population correspondingly to the increase ("piling up") of substantive (informational) accumulations. This constitutes the internal dialectics of parliamentarism and bourgeois democratism, subordinate following of which expresses itself in even more acute than before resolution of disputes and contradictions through mass violence. [Lenin is a master at identifying dialectically confronting components (i.e., the most interconnected and substantially conflicting sides, speaking in everyday terms) in all episodes of development of reality that he analyzes. In this case,

according to the established circumstances, he identifies the main actively interacting components of development (of the revolutionary movement): Bolsheviks on one side, Mensheviks on the other. Ideological grounds or background guiding the interacting parties are: revolutionary Marxism on the one hand, and revisionism on the other. Material carriers of these sides turn out to be the revolutionary proletariat on one side and wavering petty-bourgeois middling elements on the other. At later times, the latter would give way to bourgeois intelligentsia (unsteady, eternally vacillating and conciliatory). Then Lenin identifies the active contentful component, anticipates the direction of its change-movement, and further forecasts the orientation and priority of subsequent movement-development of the entire system composed of the considered components together with other changing factors of political reality]. 5. The transient nature and finiteness of prosperity of any form (whether among forms of economic management or among forms of revolutionary movement) is evidenced by the periodic renewal of crises (industrial, economic, financial, political, social, etc.). The reason for writing the January 1909 article "On the Road" [8, pp. 537–544] was the organizational and ideo-political crisis in the Social-Democratic Party of Russia. The background of the crisis lay in the urgent need to cleanse the "workers' party of wavering, intellectual and petty-bourgeois elements who had joined the labor movement mainly hoping for the imminent triumph of the bourgeois-democratic revolution and who could not hold out during the reaction" [8, p. 538]. The instability of these "elements" affected both theory, tactics and organizational policy of the party. Conscious workers took charge of running party organizations and leading them. The situation required convening the AllRussian Conference of the RSDLP, where answers were actually formulated regarding what path the working-class party should choose in the difficult current period. The content of this answer consisted of the following decisions and developments of the conference: "Marxist analysis of the current relationship between classes and new policies of Tsarism"; indication of the immediate goal of struggle set by the Social-Democratic Party; "assessment of lessons learned from the revolution concerning the correctness of revolutionary-social democratic tactics; clarification of causes of the party's crisis and pointing to the role of the proletarian element of the party in combating it; resolving the issue of the ratio of illegal and legal organization"; recognition of the need to use the Duma tribune and development of precise guiding instructions for the social-democratic faction in connection with direct criticism of its errors [8, p. 538]. Autocracy [instead of the term "autocracy", one can use today's equivalents (terms closest and similar in meaning are): authoritarianism, autocracy, tyranny, despotism] still acts as the main enemy of the proletariat and all democracy, but it would be a mistake to think that it remains unchanged, does not change or mimic.
