Today "autocracy attempts to take upon itself the solution of objectively necessary tasks of the bourgeois revolution — creation of popular representation

truly governing affairs of bourgeois society, and cleaning up medieval, tangled and obsolete agrarian relations in the village; but precisely the practical result of these new steps of autocracy turns out so far equal to zero, and this only makes even clearer the necessity of other forces and means for solving the historical task" [8, pp. 539–540]. New conditions of the moment require new forms of struggle: using the Duma tribune appears an unconditional necessity; long-term work on education and organization of masses of the proletariat is moved to the forefront; combining illegal and legal organization presents special challenges to the party; popularization and explanation of the experience of the revolution discredited by liberals and liquidator intellectuals are needed both theoretically and practically. But the tactical line of the party, which must manage to account for new conditions in methods and tools of struggle, remains unchanged. The correctness of revolutionary-social democratic tactics has been confirmed by the experience of mass struggle of 1905–1907. The defeat of the revolution at the end of this first campaign revealed not incorrectness of goals, not utopian nature of objectives, not erroneousness of means and approaches, but insufficient preparedness of forces, insufficient depth and breadth of the revolutionary crisis. Let liberals and bewildered intelligentsia fall into despair after the first massive battle for freedom. Conscious proletariat will respond to them that great tasks of revolutions were solved only when advanced classes repeated their onslaught more than once and achieved victory, having been taught by the experience of defeats. Defeated armies learn well. Revolutionary classes of Russia have been defeated in the first campaign, but the revolutionary situation persists. [Lenin's inexhaustible optimism calling to learn also from defeats evokes nothing but admiration!!!]. In new forms and another way, the revolutionary crisis looms again, matures again [it is known that any crisis always precedes and ends with replacement of forms representing developing contents of reality]. Long-term preparation of broad masses — preparation taking into account higher and more concrete goals — should be carried out, and the more successfully it is done, the surer the victory in the new struggle will be. And the same proletariat will now be able to patiently and steadfastly carry out the work of educating and preparing new cadres of a mightier revolutionary force. [It concerns periodic evaluation of the correlation of interacting components in their relation and constant tracking of the trend of shifting this correlation towards increasing and predominance of the more contentful component, for which the contentfulness of the active component (i.e., the revolutionary spirit of the proletariat) must be "replenished" through popularization and elucidation of the experience of mass struggle and revolution]. Since the general link diluting and weakening Marxist ideology of the proletariat continues to be the small commodity producer (petty bourgeoisie), drifting between two clearly defined classes of capitalists and proletariat, then under such circumstances the task of the day is: "Strengthening of the illegal party organization, creating party cells in all areas of activity, creating above all purely party, albeit few-membered workers' committees in every industrial enterprise,

concentration of leading functions in the hands of leaders of the socialdemocratic movement from among the workers themselves" [8, p. 543]. Each cell and each party worker committee must become a support point for agitational, propaganda and practically organizational work among the masses, i.e., necessarily go where the mass goes and try at every step to push its consciousness toward socialism, winning for itself leadership in all proletarian legal organizations by virtue of its energy and ideological influence (rather than titles and ranks). [According to the testimony of N.V. Volsky (a contemporary of Vladimir Ilyich, with the real surname N.V. Valentinov), who never was, by his own admission, either a Bolshevik or a pronounced Menshevik [1], Lenin was a supporter of the agitational work with all strata of the population, but the emphasis proposed to be made nevertheless on the work with the proletariat of Russia.] In any case legally or semi-legally, openly or covertly, the working class will find points of consolidation everywhere — conscious party social democrats will always move ahead and consolidate among themselves to impact the masses in the party spirit. A party of the vanguard class (proletariat), which manages to organize its avantgarde and direct its forces so as to exert social-democratic influence on every manifestation of life of the proletariat — this party will win no matter what [8, p. 544]. [By analogy with the previous article, V.I. Lenin bases his analysis of current events again on the existence and interaction of two main classes: the ruling class of owners of means of production and the subordinate class of nonpropertied, dependent hired laborers free from ownership of means of production. Between these two classes drifts a layer of declassified pettybourgeois elements, periodically replenishing either class (much more often — the class of non-propertied hired laborers). The cause of the ideological-political crisis in the Russian Social-Democratic Party was exactly the uncertainty of position of representatives of this stratum within the social-democratic milieu. All other conclusions and fabrications stem from evil, belong to the realm of fictions generated by apologists serving the ruling class among university professors, professional philosophers, political scientists and propagandists of all stripes. Neither Marx nor Lenin were discoverers of classes or originators of ideas about class struggle as the driving force of social development — bourgeois historians and economists had "exploited" these discoveries long before Marxism emerged. But both used primarily these concepts and representations to analyze ongoing historical processes, their patterns and trends. Then came times when the aforementioned concepts (denoting the main subjects of social activity and the process of its implementation) were buried

under splinters and chips flying from the axe that carried out major public transformations. Thus, the most important components of development and their sacramental relations were put away in a drawer for a long time and thoroughly forgotten. Nowadays it is quite common to hold an opinion — usually expressed by the same "court" politicians, economists and philosophers close to power structures, as well as their media outlets justifying the fairness and eternity of existing foundations, i.e., apologists of current social arrangements — that class theory has hopelessly become obsolete and that now property ownership itself (especially on means of production) plays no role; supposedly, what matters today are opportunities to control financial flows. No, gentlemen, you are wrong. In order to manage financial flows, play multi-step financial combinations, rule over the whole world, etc., you still have to eat, drink, live somewhere, "manage households, give birth to children, produce products, exchange them..." (see the above-quoted Lenin passage from the book “Materialism and Empiriocriticism”), which does not allow ignoring property relations and everything connected with them. And if you want to rise higher, soar above earthly life with its material physical side — this will not happen either. To realize such a "wish", one would have to completely detach oneself from the objectively-material level of one's bodilyintellectual organization, from the basis of your precious "I", which is absolutely impossible without loss or deprivation of this unique "I". The level of ideal subjective forms enters only narrowly (although being the most contentful of reliably known ones today) into the overall set of levels constituting your "I", whereas the bulk of every human "I" consists of rather "down-to-earth" object-matter layers of reality. And you cannot get rid of the heavy shackles of these "coarse" layers while remaining among those living on earth. This is precisely what Marx called, recalling his words, the curse on spirit — to be burdened by matter. And however much anyone might wish, having achieved desired material prosperity, to dissociate themselves from all coarse physicality and ascend to the realm of the ideal and unearthly — property relations and especially those regarding means of production will continue to remain key, whoever says otherwise and however they try to camouflage this fact. Therefore, staying on real ground and being part of the object-matter level of reality forming and filling you up, you will inevitably have to keep fighting for your flesh and for your place under the sun. You will also inevitably have to think about your offspring, and in connection with this — about inheritance for your descendants, i.e., about that very mundane property which, god forbid, may end up in alien hands. Your own flesh will not let you soar above the struggle and beyond this entire world like an elf or angel; but once you shed it (if such should ever occur), you will simultaneously lose yourself (your "I") together with all your "fantasies" and "desires" — well, then what will your desires concern? The direct path for you, gentlemen, lies toward socialism — there alone will you find deliverance from unbearable fetters of property and endless

thoughts about accumulation and transfer of material inheritance into the right hands, and finally begin caring about intellectual and spiritual legacy; there you will acquire more or less genuine freedom from dreary thoughts about how to master financial assets, ride financial flows — and who knows what else you cling to with iron grip. Welcome to socialism, gentlemen! It must be said that denial of classes and class struggle is an inexhaustible element of idle reasoning exercises, periodically reviving and again taking possession of unsophisticated minds after each successive "defeat" and public expulsion. It seems, however, that the hour has come when public attention is drawn again to relations of key constituents of developing reality, as well as to their role and significance during replacement of forms of social progress. The mere absence at present of visible signs of class struggle speaks only of disorganization or insufficient organization of representatives of hired labor and lack of outstanding and incorruptible leaders of revolutionary movement at this stage of social development.] In relation {proletariat/autocracy (tsarism)} a fairly noticeable shift occurred towards predominance of the fastest-developing contentful component — proletariat with its Marxist party, becoming richer in content due to growth in educational standard of party and proletarian ranks, while the second component — autocracy — mimics, tries to mask itself as bourgeoisie and merge with it. It should be noted that in his analysis Lenin never considers the main interacting components or figures separately, detached from each other — always only together, in interaction, in relation. Proletariat is nonessential without bourgeoisie or capitalists, just as the latter are nonessential without proletariat. Capital is nonessential without hired labor, and vice versa. Only the interaction of these two constituents of reality, their collision, their conflict, internecine struggle contributes to emergence of new developmental forms and movement toward a new socio-economic formation, i.e., to new forms of acquiring social orders. [Periodically arising public conflicts have as their foundation accumulating unsolvable-for-the-time-being content-form contradictions: between outdated management system, bureaucratized power, "bourgeoisified" ruling class with its institutions and forms of governance on the one hand, and, on the other hand, competence and preparedness of society with its substantive public opinions and representations surpassing this "ossifying" system. Evidence of this includes numerous examples of modern states with their internal conflicts: frames, forms, methods of primitive management do not meet the needs, demands, requirements of a complexifying, perfecting, developing and rapidly increasing-in-contentfulness socium.] A crisis in the revolutionary movement (according to Lenin) is a harbinger of another stage of construction and formation of a new relation of directly interacting constituents-components and at the same time a precursor of a new search for a way "on the road" and purification from the old form (its attributes) restraining the movement of the contentful constituent; it is a

sign of outgrowing the existing form by its developed content and an indicator of necessity to master a new promising form sufficiently developed already in terms of content. At the same time, the correlation of constituents-components in the newly emerging and changing relation is analyzed, where the numerator is enriched by decisions and conclusions of conferences and congresses of the Bolshevik Party, while the denominator is fed by resolutions of the Constituent Assembly of the State Duma (i.e., circulars of the "mimicking" form of state government). [Production, management, economic, ideological-political, etc., crises are indicators of a disrupted balance or equilibrium between interacting components in their various form-relations. The leading contentful constituent moving forward (or preparing to move forward and lead) "requires" search for and discovery of its corresponding formal component that balances (for some period of time) the combined relation of these constituents. The stagnant phase in which this new disequilibrium relation finds itself (in our case: {educated proletariat/mimicking autocracy}) is overcome by searching for and finding a more active form of movement of the contentful component, which, when another way out "on the road" is found, is ensured through accelerated increase in contentfulness of the numerator of the mentioned new relation; i.e., through (in Lenin's words): indication of the immediate goal of struggle; assessment of correctness of social-democratic tactics; clarification of causes of the party crisis and pointing to the role of the proletarian element of the party in combating it; resolution of the issue regarding the ratio of illegal and legal organization; recognition of the need to use the Duma tribune and development of precise guiding instructions for the social-democratic faction.] And, as Lenin summarizes, this stage must be outgrown and the crisis overcome by discovering and applying new forms of struggle [8, p. 540]. In other words, a new exit "on the road" is sought through increasing the contentfulness of forms of the Social-Democratic movement. § 2. Works of the immediate pre-revolutionary period The second period, which we have named as immediate pre-revolutionary (on the eve of the revolution), is represented by a number of works by Vladimir I. Lenin, dictated by rapidly changing events (especially on the eve of the October coup). 1. In the book "On the Right of Nations to Self-Determination" [8, pp. 569-621], written in May 1914, Lenin analyzes in detail the specifics of applying the Marxist position on the secession of nations depending on different historically established conditions for the formation of one or another nation. Due to its closest connection with the present, we will dwell on this work in more detail.

Preliminarily, it should be said that the "right of nations to selfdetermination", regarding the legitimacy and expediency of whose proclamation and protection heated debates do not cease even in our days, is not a figment of V.I. Lenin's imagination or an invention of the Bolsheviks, but the result of discussions and decisions of the International congresses, and in particular the "London International Congress (of socialist workers' parties and trade unions) of 1896". The defense of this right and its inclusion in the program of the Social-Democratic Party of Russia was justified by Lenin by referring to the corresponding stage of the national liberation movement experienced by Russia. By analogy with the support by Marxists under similar historical conditions of the said right in Western Europe, Lenin, focusing on similar liberation processes in Eastern Europe and Asia (including Russia), but occurring with some delay (at least by half a century), concludes on the necessity of advancing the same demand at the stage of revolutionary transformations in Russia as well. To convey the logic of Lenin's thought, let us provide a chain of characteristic quotes from this work. "An unconditional requirement of Marxist theory in the analysis of any social question whatsoever is to place it within definite historical limits, and then, if the question concerns one country (for example, a national program for a given country), to take into account the concrete specifics distinguishing this country from others within the limits of one and the same historical epoch" [8, p. 574]. "The comparison of the political and economic development of different countries, as well as their Marxist programs, is of enormous significance from the standpoint of Marxism, for both the general capitalist nature of modern states and the general law of their development are indisputable. But such a comparison must be made skillfully. The ABC condition here is to clarify the question of whether the historical epochs of development of the compared countries are comparable" [8, p. 578]. "In Western, continental Europe, the era of bourgeois-democratic revolutions covers a rather definite time span, approximately from 1789 to 1871. It was precisely this era that was the era of national movements and the creation of national states. Upon the completion of this era, Western Europe turned into an established system of bourgeois states, as a general rule, at the same time nationally unified states. Therefore, to now seek the right to selfdetermination in the programs of West European socialists means not to understand the ABC of Marxism. In Eastern Europe and Asia, the era of bourgeois-democratic revolutions only began in 1905. ...And in this chain of events only a blind man can fail to see the awakening of a whole series of bourgeois-democratic national movements, aspirations for the creation of nationally independent and

nationally unified states. It is precisely because and only because Russia, together with neighboring countries, is experiencing this era that we need a point on the right of nations to self-determination in our program" [8, pp. 578-579]. "Thus, it is the historical concrete specifics of the national question in Russia that give us a special urgency to the recognition of the right of nations to self-determination in the epoch we are experiencing" [8, p. 581]. "The bourgeoisie, which naturally acts as the hegemon (leader) of any national movement at its beginning, calls the support of all national aspirations a practical matter. But the policy of the proletariat in the national question (as in other questions) only supports the bourgeoisie in a certain direction, but never coincides with its policy. ...Every bourgeoisie wants in national matters either privileges for its nation or exclusive benefits for it; this is called 'practical'. The proletariat is against any privileges, against any exclusivity" [8, p. 582]. "The bourgeoisie always places its national demands in the foreground. It puts them unconditionally. For the proletariat, they are subordinated to the interests of the class struggle. Theoretically, one cannot guarantee in advance whether the secession of a given nation or its equal status with another nation will complete the bourgeois-democratic revolution; for the proletariat, it is important in both cases to ensure the development of its class; for the bourgeoisie, it is important to hinder this development, postponing its tasks before the tasks of 'their' nation. Therefore, the proletariat limits itself to a negative, so to speak, demand for the recognition of the right to self-determination, guaranteeing nothing to any nation, undertaking to give nothing regarding another nation. Let this be not 'practical', but in fact this most reliably guarantees the most democratic of possible solutions; the proletariat needs only these guarantees, and the bourgeoisie of each nation needs guarantees of its benefits without relation to the position (to possible minuses) of other nations. The bourgeoisie is most interested in the 'feasibility' of a given demand — hence the eternal policy of deals with the bourgeoisie of other nations to the detriment of the proletariat. Let this be not 'practical' for opportunists, but this is the only guarantee in fact, a guarantee of maximum national equality and peace in spite of both the feudal lords and the nationalist bourgeoisie. The entire task of the proletarians in the national question is 'impractical' from the standpoint of the nationalist bourgeoisie of each nation, for the proletarians demand abstract equality, the principled absence of the slightest privileges, being hostile to any nationalism.

...The proletariat is against such practicality: recognizing equality and equal right to a national state, it values and places above all the union of proletarians of all nations, evaluating any national demand, any national secession from the angle of the class struggle of workers. The slogan of practicality is in fact merely the slogan of uncritical adoption of bourgeois aspirations. They tell us: by supporting the right to secession, you support bourgeois nationalism of oppressed nations. ...We answer: no, it is precisely the bourgeoisie that is interested in a 'practical' solution here, while for the workers it is important to fundamentally distinguish two tendencies. Since the bourgeoisie of the oppressed nation is fighting the oppressing one, we are always and in any case and more resolutely than all, for we are the boldest and most consistent enemies of oppression. Since the bourgeoisie of the oppressed nation stands for its bourgeois nationalism, we are against. The struggle against the privileges and violence of the oppressing nation and no connivance at the striving for privileges on the part of the oppressed nation. If we do not advance and conduct in our agitation the slogan of the right to secession, we will play into the hands not only of the bourgeoisie, but also of the feudal lords and the absolutism of the oppressing nation. ...In every bourgeois nationalism of an oppressed nation there is a general-democratic content against oppression, and it is this content that we unconditionally support, strictly separating the striving for one's own national exclusiveness, fighting against the striving of the Polish bourgeois to oppress the Jew, etc., etc. [That is, here, undoubtedly, one must see the boundary markers beyond which the striving for national identity degenerates into inveterate nationalism and then into bestial Nazism, and suppress such a content-form transformation]. This is "impractical" from the point of view of the bourgeois and the philistine. This is the only practical and principled and truly helping democracy, freedom, and the proletarian union policy in the national question: recognition of the right of nations to secession for all; evaluation of each specific question of secession from the standpoint eliminating any inequality, any privileges, any exclusivity. Let us take the position of the oppressing nation. Can a people that oppresses other peoples be free? No. The interests of freedom of the Great Russian population require a struggle against such oppression. A long history, a centuries-old history of suppressing the movements of oppressed nations, systematic propaganda of such suppression by the "higher" classes have created enormous obstacles to the cause of freedom of the Great Russian people itself in its prejudices, etc.

...The Great Russian proletariat cannot achieve its goals, cannot clear its path to freedom, without systematically fighting these prejudices. [And the matter here, perhaps, is not only in prejudices or the moralhumanistic side of the issue, but also in the trivial discomfort of the everyday life of the oppressing nation, being in constant tension, suspicion, apprehensions and expectation of blows in the back from the oppressed, suppressed, humiliated nation. The Great Russian, privileged in relation to a non-Russian, is just as unfree, uneasy, unbalanced, unself-sufficient as a non-Russian — due to the social tension created by the counter-resistance and opposition from the oppressed nation. This contentfulness ought to be known to today's "resolvers" of the national question]. ...We, the proletarians, declare ourselves in advance to be opponents of Great Russian privileges and in this direction conduct all our propaganda and agitation... against all state-national privileges... such a task is our main (current) task in the national question, for only in this way do we defend the interests of democracy and the equal union of all proletarians of all nations. Let this propaganda be impractical from the point of view of the Great Russian oppressors and from the point of view of the bourgeoisie of oppressed nations. ...In fact, it is this propaganda, and only it, that ensures a truly democratic and truly socialist education of the masses. Only such propaganda guarantees both the greatest chances of national peace in Russia if it remains a motley national state, and the most peaceful (and harmless for the proletarian class struggle) division into different national states, if the question of such a division arises" [8, pp. 582-586]. "To accuse the supporters of the freedom of self-determination, i.e. the freedom of secession, of encouraging separatism is the same stupidity and the same hypocrisy as to accuse the supporters of the freedom of divorce of encouraging the destruction of family ties. Just as in bourgeois society the defenders of privileges and venality, on which the bourgeois marriage is built, oppose the freedom of divorce, so in the capitalist state the denial of the freedom of self-determination, i.e. the secession of nations, means only the defense of the privileges of the dominant nation and police methods of administration to the detriment of democratic ones" [8, p. 593]. "The real class significance of the liberal hostility to the principle of political self-determination of nations is one and only one: national-liberalism, the defense of state privileges of the Great Russian bourgeoisie. [It is not difficult to see that the position of some leaders of the "liberal" parties of modern Russia is an absolute carbon copy of these Lenin's words].

...The interests of the working class and its struggle against capitalism require full solidarity and the closest unity of workers of all nations, require resistance to the nationalist policy of the bourgeoisie of whatever nationality. Therefore, a deviation from the tasks of proletarian policy and the subordination of workers to bourgeois policy would be both if the SocialDemocrats were to deny the right to self-determination, i.e. the right to secession of oppressed nations, and if the Social-Democrats were to support all national demands of the bourgeoisie of oppressed nations. To a hired worker it is all the same whether his predominant exploiter will be the Great Russian bourgeoisie preferably over a non-Russian one, or the Polish preferably over a Jewish one, etc. A hired worker who has realized the interests of his class is indifferent both to the state privileges of the Great Russian capitalists and to the promises of the Polish or Ukrainian capitalists that paradise on earth will be established when they possess state privileges. ...In any case, the hired worker will remain an object of exploitation, and a successful struggle against it requires the independence of the proletariat from nationalism, the complete, so to speak, neutrality of the proletarians in the struggle of the bourgeoisie of different nations for supremacy. The slightest support by the proletariat of any nation for the privileges of "its" national bourgeoisie will inevitably cause distrust of the proletariat of another nation, will weaken the international class solidarity of workers, will divide them to the delight of the bourgeoisie. And the denial of the right to selfdetermination, or to secession, inevitably means in practice support for the privileges of the dominant nation" [8, pp. 594-595]. "In the question of the self-determination of nations, as in any other question, we are interested first of all in the self-determination of the proletariat within nations" [8, p. 597]. [Nowadays, such an approach remains topical, counting on the unification of all wage workers (regardless of nations and nationality) in the struggle and opposition to the associated oligarchy. In the application to the present day, everywhere in the text of Lenin's work, by the proletariat one should understand the wage workers and representatives of wage labor, and by the bourgeoisie of the oppressing and oppressed nation — the foreign and local oligarchy. After making such a replacement, everything falls into place; as can be seen, the relations of wage workers and the oligarchy, as well as the consequences of these relations, are written by the author of the book with the utmost clarity long before the October Revolution. We are obviously being punished today for Unlearned lessons of dialectics, as negligent students, although we have already passed all this once. To please their personal interests (i.e., in pursuit of profit), the bourgeoisie (oligarchy) of any nation easily steps over national and patriotic principles,

which fully justifies the label "comprador" (venal) bourgeoisie hung on it. This, first of all, must be known and kept in mind by every people trying to find the roots of national troubles. Only a consciously organized opposition to the market (i.e., spontaneously) "molded" oligarchy can change the position (and status) of the working people for the better. Today's bourgeoisie, like the bourgeoisie of the past, are also not interested in social development (i.e., in improving methods of management and planning, economic and political reforms, raising the standard of living of the population), since all this contributes to the growth of consciousness, education and, accordingly, organization of wage workers. Therefore, no one is in a hurry, and will not be, to transfer or invest their foreign savings and capital (allegedly earned "by their own sweat and blood") into the country's economy, to abandon offshore assets. What allows us to confidently use in this connection the terms "oligarchs", "oligarchy"? It is enough to look more closely at the structure of property, income and acquisitions of any major official or deputy (not losing sight of relatives and trusted persons with assets rewritten to them) to see there both the owners of the means of production, and shareholders of the most profitable industrial enterprises and banks, and owners of large property, owners of bank accounts and financial assets, and, moreover, undoubtedly, those residing in power. And then how do such managers differ from oligarchs or, at worst, from "near-oligarchs"? By formal features and definition — not at all, and among themselves they differ only in the size of appropriations and the degree of influence on the political and economic decisions made regarding the fate of the country or region; in the final analysis, all the so-called oligarchs differ from each other only in the size of the damage inflicted on the country. In general, all capitalist states are built on an oligarchic type (scheme): first money is made (and capital is accumulated), then power is desired in the name of new opportunities and new money, and the combination of both again multiplies power and money. For a person with an unprepared, immature, undeveloped consciousness — this is a dead end, a closed vicious circle, a trap. A corrupt capitalist country is always an oligarchic state. On the other hand, the management configuration that has developed in the country today is quite accurately determined by the term "plutocracy". On the third hand, this is, of course, also an "autocracy", represented by the "face" of the current "ruler". Before us is such a volatile mixture of plutocratic, oligarchic and autocratic elements of statehood. In this infrastructure, where there is everything and a lot of it, there is only one small link missing — the rule of the people; the working people simply do not "fit" with the modern government and therefore are not represented in the government]. There are assurances that "the recognition of the right to secession increases the danger of the state's disintegration. ... From the point of view of democracy in general, it is quite the opposite: the recognition of the right to secession reduces the danger of the "disintegration of the state"" [8, p. 592].

Further, the question is examined using the example of the Ukrainian nationality: "Is it not clear that the more freedom the Ukrainian nationality has in one or another country, the stronger the connection of this nationality with the given country will be? It seems that one cannot argue against this ABC truth if one does not decisively break with all the premises of democratism. And can there be greater freedom of a nationality as such than the freedom of secession, the freedom of forming an independent national state?" [8, p. 592]. [At first glance, it may seem incorrect that the dialectical position, according to which the recognition of the right of nations to self-determination does not increase the danger of the state's disintegration, but on the contrary, cements the country and strengthens the ties between nationalities — since in relation to, say, the above-mentioned Ukrainian nationality, this dialectical position was not fulfilled and is not being fulfilled. However, in this particular case, the following circumstances should be taken into account. Unlike the conditions and events corresponding to the temporary stage analyzed in the book by Lenin we are considering, for some time now (already in our times), under the patronage of the local "comprador" oligarchy, on the territory once called the Ukrainian SSR, a shoot of rabid nationalism and fierce hatred of everything Russian, cultivated in the deep past and pulled out from the dilapidated sewer outlets, has bloomed in full bloom. From this perspective, the revived phenomenon already falls under the definition not of a traditional national liberation movement (acquiring freedom and the right of a nation to self-determination), but of aggressive Nazism based on blood feud and innate hatred (the roots of which must be sought in the origin of the once artificially bred and carefully selected "marginal" nationalism), the formation of which has little in common with a national liberation movement. Extremely interesting in this regard is the fact that from a whole range of levels-forms within the composition of their multi-level totality (uniting in itself diverse methods of interethnic interaction), the lowest, most primitive level receives an impetus for priority development (under the "management" of the same universal principle of Self-development) in the image of some fanged horned creature with a tail and fur that has not yet fallen off, crawled out of a dark deep cave to teach the whole civilized world its understanding of life and interethnic communication. Like a weed stifling cultivated plants at the root, this primitive "misunderstanding" is capable for some time of diverting to itself and "grinding" the contentful volumes worked out later, but already tested, or, simply put, absorbing (destroying) the civilized forms of higher levels. Such are the curiosities and "inscrutable" paths of the dialectical development of diverse forms-levels of reality]. As for the attitude to the national question, for example, of Marx: "Marx makes no absolute of national movements, knowing that the complete liberation of all nationalities can only be given by the victory of the working class. To take into account in advance all possible correlations between the bourgeois liberation movements of oppressed nations and the proletarian liberation movement among the oppressing nation (this is precisely the problem that makes the national question so difficult in modern Russia) is an impossible thing" [8, p. 608].

[In other words, one must support the oppressed nation in its struggle and direct this struggle towards facilitating the proletarian movement, since the analysis of all variants of correlations (line-up of forces) between the forces of national liberation and proletarian movements (in their relation) is impossible even with the involvement of the intellectual power of 70 Marxs (to use Lenin's words, who always meant the constant presence of some objective uncertainty in the development of certain forms-relations and the impossibility of knowing or anticipating the development of these forms with one hundred percent certainty). As we can see, the ability to think dialectically allowed the classics of Marxism, and in particular V.I. Lenin, to focus attention by no means on obtaining exhaustive knowledge about the outcome of the interaction of the studied components, but only on understanding the tendency of variability of their relations, in order to already on this basis forecast the further development of their forms]. "The protocols of the congress of 1903 [II Congress of the RSDLP], which adopted the program [of the party] of Russian Marxists, have become the greatest rarity, and the vast majority of modern figures of the workers' movement are not familiar with the motives of individual points of the program. ...Let us note first of all that, however scanty the Russian Social-Democratic literature concerning the "right of nations to self-determination" may be, it is nevertheless absolutely clear from it that this right was always understood in the sense of the right to secession. ...The reader sees that at the II Congress of the party, which adopted the program, there were not two opinions on the question that self-determination means "only" the right to secession" [8, pp. 610-611, 613]. "...The right to secession presupposes the solution of the question precisely not by the central parliament, but only by the parliament (sejm, referendum, etc.) of the seceding region" [8, p. 617]. "Great Russian nationalism, like any nationalism, goes through various phases [i.e., this question, like any other, Lenin considers through the prism of historical development: from having a hard autocratic reactionary color to appearing in the color of bourgeois liberalism], depending on the predominance [i.e., on the bias and shift towards a more substantial, more contentful component in their relation — in this case in the relation {bourgeois national-liberalism/autocratic monarchic national-reactionism}] of one or another class in a bourgeois country. Before 1905, we knew almost only national-reactionaries. After the revolution of 1905, national-liberals were born among us. And further, the birth of Great Russian national-democrats is inevitable" [8, p. 619]. "From the point of view of the theory of Marxism in general, the question of the right to self-determination presents no difficulties. There can seriously be no talk

either of challenging the London decision of 1896, or of the fact that by selfdetermination is meant only the right to secession, or of the fact that the formation of independent national states is a tendency of all bourgeoisdemocratic upheavals. The difficulty is created to a certain extent by the fact that in Russia the proletariat of oppressed nations and the proletariat of the oppressing nation are fighting and must fight side by side. To defend the unity of the class struggle of the proletariat for socialism, to repel all bourgeois and Black-Hundred influences of nationalism — this is the task. Among the oppressed nations, the separation of the proletariat into an independent party sometimes leads to such a fierce struggle with the nationalism of a given nation that the perspective is distorted and the nationalism of the oppressing nation is forgotten" [8, pp. 617-618]. "The slower the democratic transformation of Russia goes, the more persistent, rough, and fierce will be the national persecution and squabbling of the bourgeoisie of different nations. ...Full equality of nations; the right of nations to self-determination; the merging of workers of all nations — this national program teaches workers Marxism, teaches the experience of the whole world and the experience of Russia" [8, p. 620]. [Analyzing the above, one can be convinced that the slogan in defense of the right of nations to self-determination is always relevant — since it is an indicator of the recognition of the equality of all small and large nations in a multinational state (which, undoubtedly, should serve as the main condition for the cohesion of nations in union states); in case of legalizing this right, it is easier for the oppressed classes to defeat any oppressor, uniting on the basis of equality of all nations. True equality of nations is conditioned and ensured only by the recognition of the right of nations to self-determination (political self-determination, i.e. possible and permissible secession). This principle of self-determination is relevant for Russia (as it was relevant for the USSR) also because its peoples, like the peoples of the USSR, united and entered unions, as a rule, with their territories — i.e., not being newcomers or immigrants, which cannot be said, for example, about the USA and a number of other states. But all this was accepted with delight and looked exactly like that until the right of nations to self-determination enshrined in the constitution suddenly served as a reason for merciless accusations of destroying a great country. It goes without saying that the main claims were made against the founder of the first socialist state in the world, Vladimir Ilyich Lenin, who allegedly "laid" a delayed-action "mine" under the Soviet Union.

Advancing such accusations, no one even remotely thinks about the objective possibility of an unfavorable (from the point of view of human plans and

hopes) course and outcome of content-form transformations, which always take place in the process of reality development. No one thinks about the inevitability of content-form transformations, conversions, transfigurations to which any developing form is subjected in the process of "making itself out of itself". In this case, it was only required to be a very well-informed and competent leader to always remember the objectivity of the tendency of natural transfiguration of forms into their opposite, i.e. about the immanent dialectical property (character) of developing reality, described by the formulation of the universal principle of self-development (see above). Nevertheless, the real state of affairs is such that by virtue of this very dialectical nature of the surrounding world, everything (without exception) sooner or later has a habit of returning "to its own circle", but not in its former form, but in other — "modified" — forms. In case of misunderstanding such a layout or in case of inept and imprudent handling of the mentioned modified forms — the impacts, and even blows of these weighted returning forms may fall not where the lower back is (it would be fine if it were so, which is still tolerable), but where the neck is higher. Having detected such a trend manifesting in connection with the development of one or another form of reality, one can and should learn (if necessary) to influence this trend, preventing or anticipating the emergence of undesirable (from the point of view of human consciousness) consequences, which, naturally, no one in those times fulfilled not only was not, but was not even supposed to be fulfilled — after all, the last outstanding dialectician in the chain of responsible persons turned out to be Lenin, and all his other followers and continuators, from the point of view of dialectical training, are neither here nor there (ne prishey kobyle khvost). However, to blame, in all troubles and failures, the one who understands such issues best — this is in our days (as, indeed, always) a downright sign of good manners. Having an idea of the inevitable transformation or transfiguration of changing forms into their opposites (i.e., about returning "to one's own circle"), in order to keep these forms within the framework of their former status, it is necessary in various ways to increase their contentfulness (in this case — literacy, education, awareness, etc.). Perhaps, one cannot even think of a more vivid example in confirmation of the urgent need to master the art of dialectical thinking, dialectical research approach and practical activity! In the USSR, at the stage of swaying and the so-called "perestroika", the party nomenclature (the forerunner of the emerging bourgeoisie) of precisely "aliens", i.e. national formations (republics), finally went out of the control of the central authority, which determined the collapse of the great Union. Starting from the Khrushchev times, and especially during the Gorbachev perestroika time, obvious and covert decomposition, de-ideologization and transfiguration of both central (union) and local (republican), in modern slang, "elites" (or rather, pseudo-elites — party nomenclature, top and middle managers, heads of large enterprises, etc.) was in full swing, which

were swiftly becoming "philistinized" and "bourgeoisified", turning into their own ideologically contentless (but filled with bourgeois-mercantilist content) opposite in full accordance with the dialectical character of reality development. As is proper in such "non-transparent" cases (i.e., in times of turmoil), the contentful cause of what is happening is pushed to the background and its place is "draped" with the Formal component of the real process. In the established relation {nomenclature power in the localities / formal arrangement of local power based on the right of nations to political secession}, due to the sharp spontaneous-uncontrolled expansion of the powers and capabilities of the nomenclature local power (i.e., the "swelling" of the contentful component or the "numerator" of the above-mentioned relation), the "denominator" of the relation also begins to change — towards isolation or separation from the center and the dominance of the nomenclature local pseudo-elite (power structures). The correctness and validity of the idea of the right of nations to selfdetermination even at that moment is beyond any doubt provided that strict control is ensured over other particulars and details. The motives and actions of the nomenclature of "aliens" (using the terminology of Lenin's articles), which, having found itself without "supervision and care", played its mercantilist card to the victorious end, which could have been decisively suppressed on quite legal grounds, should have been put under control. Illegal actions of the decaying nomenclature at the level of republican authorities should have been stopped, relying on the "Constitution (Basic Law) of the USSR" and on the then still functional central and republican structures of law enforcement agencies: KGB, MVD, MO (Chekists, police, army), continuing to keep ideology and personnel policy under control. And, as we now know for sure, due to the lack of such control and countermeasures, the internal decomposition of the pseudo-elite ends, in the end, with its transformation into an oligarchy of central and local significance (scale); and, of course, not without external foreign influence and complicity. Unprincipled and unscrupulous nouveau riche nomenclature figures in conjunction with inveterate bandits and criminals became the new masters of life. Yes, the decisions (and actions) of the local pseudo-elites formally fell under the patronage of the constitutional right of nations to self-determination, but these decisions were not legal from the point of view of confirmation by the officially identified will of the majority of citizens of the national republics. The legality of actions was replaced by "under-the-table" activity and the pushiness of local nomenclature structures and individuals. Thus, not the notorious right of nations to self-determination became the cause of the destruction of the USSR, but the dominance that had developed by that time of the satiated, transfigured de-ideologized "comprador" (venal) top and the lack of control over its behavior.

Thus (it is emphasized specifically for the current "rulers" and their ilk), Ilyich did not "lay" any "mines" in the arrangement of the multinational Soviet state — all decisions of the Bolsheviks were conditioned by the specific context of the epoch experienced and there is nothing to blame him for the alleged lack of foresight; in our opinion, the current "armchair" opponents and experts should read his works more attentively and thoughtfully and not rush to proclaim their own mental superiority. The very raising of the question of the alleged "laid mine" testifies to an insufficiently high level of intelligence and education of the persons raising this issue, since the question regarding the "exhaustion" or discovered over time "nonoperability" of this decision should not be asked of Lenin, who, we repeat, knew how to skillfully draw conclusions and make decisions in accordance with the circumstances of a certain time stage, but with those persons who, due to the rigidity of their thinking, could not subsequently flexibly react to changing conditions. The role and significance of the human factor (and personnel policy), ladies and gentlemen, no one has yet canceled. Again, without possessing a dialectical mindset, it is oh so difficult to search for and find correct solutions to the most complex questions — which pushes (forces) ordinary incompetent persons (including superficial researchers) to shamelessly attribute all mistakes and miscalculations to extraordinary, but, unfortunately, humble and silent predecessors. It is quite probable that in our days a variant of establishing a union association could be more productive, in which any national formation (republic, state, autonomy) would enter the composition of a new federal arrangement on the rights of a territorial district with the same "right to self-determination", but not on a national, but on a territorial basis and with the obligation to establish a single citizenship for the entire population as an indicator of belonging to the common state structure (well, naturally, and with all other attributes of a single state: a single currency, common goals, a single defense doctrine and law enforcement agencies, etc.). But this is still a question requiring the involvement of dialectical minds for its effective solution, who would be able to track the peripeteia of the changing situation and accordingly adjust the decisions made throughout the entire period of the relevance of this issue. God forbid the current "rulers" should imagine themselves to be qualified "resolvers" of national problems, and on this wave get involved in defusing the "mines" allegedly "laid" by Lenin. Without taking into account the numerous nuances of the national question, these "resolvers", not being skilled dialecticians for a single time, will make a mess and out of ignorance or lack of foresight will finally bury a big country under this firewood. In any case, a simple solution to the national question by means of the so-called "melting pot", from which serious racial problems, still not even close to being resolved, suddenly crawled out (as the American near-election crisis of 2020 shows), is also impossible, which should be taken into account by all primitive figures and flat "resolvers" when they assume to close the sore topic once and for all in this way.

Meanwhile, this issue remains extremely topical at least because the sovereignty (and, consequently, the identity) of a small nation (for example, the people of Belarus in modern conditions) can be ensured and preserved on the principles of the right of a nation to self-determination only if it exists (functions) in symbiosis with a large (predominant in population) nation (within or under the wing of a united Russia or an updated, modernized USSR, for example), which guarantees this sovereignty and undertakes to protect the small nation from attacks and claims from aggressive capitalist neighbors; otherwise, as the practice of today shows, the aspiration (as a temptation) of a small (unself-sufficient) state to pursue its own independent policy (adherence to the so-called political multivector approach) will sooner or later turn this state into an appendage of more powerful capitalist states with inevitable leveling (nullification) of its national identity. In fact, the analysis of Lenin's vision of the development options of this topic leads precisely to such a conclusion.] 2. The manifesto "War and Russian Social-Democracy" [8, pp. 622-628], published in November 1914, was the first official document of the RSDLP, which outlined the position of the Bolshevik party regarding the beginning of the world imperialist war. "The European war, which for decades had been prepared by the governments and bourgeois parties of all countries, broke out. ... Seizure of lands and subjugation of foreign nations, ruin of a competing nation, plunder of its wealth, diversion of the attention of the working masses from the internal political crises of Russia, Germany, England and other countries, disunity and nationalist duping of workers and the extermination of their vanguard for the purpose of weakening the revolutionary movement of the proletariat — such is the only real content, significance and meaning of the modern war" [8, p. 622]. [At the beginning of the article, Lenin analyzes the current situation and the alignment of forces, showing that at the head of one of the warring groups stands the German bourgeoisie, assuring the working class that it is waging a war for the defense of the homeland, freedom and culture, for the liberation of the peoples oppressed by tsarism and the destruction of reactionary tsarism, but in fact being a faithful ally of tsarism and the enemy of the revolutionary movement of workers and peasants in Russia and supporting the tsarist monarchy against the revolution in Russia. At the head of the other group of warring nations stands the English and French bourgeoisie, duping the working class and the working masses, claiming that it is waging a war for the homeland, freedom and culture against militarism and despotism of Germany, but in fact striving to seize German colonies and ruin a competing nation, distinguished by faster economic development. Contentful enrichment (accumulation of the content of one's own form) at the expense of others, at the expense of competitors — this is the meaning of this war (division, redistribution and seizure of markets, destruction of competitors). In other words, the attitudes of lower (i.e., more primitive) levels-forms towards multiplying their contentfulness by taking away and consuming "alien" content overcome or take precedence over the socialist attitudes or goals of more developed levels, professing the accumulation and increase of their contentfulness through joint creative labor,

conducting research of developing reality by common forces, cognition and forecasting by joint efforts of the emergence of new forms of reality at all levels, as well as directions of prospective human activity]. "Both groups of belligerent countries do not yield to each other in robberies, atrocities and endless cruelties of war, but in order to dupe the proletariat and divert its attention from the only truly liberating war, namely the civil war against the bourgeoisie of both "their" country and "foreign" countries, for this high purpose the bourgeoisie of each country with false phrases about patriotism tries to exalt the significance of "its" national war and assure that it seeks to defeat the enemy not for robbery and land seizure, but for the "liberation" of all other peoples, except its own. ...But the more diligently the governments and the bourgeoisie of all countries try to disunite the workers and set them against each other... — the more urgent is the duty of the conscious proletariat to defend its class cohesion, its internationalism, its socialist convictions against the rampage of chauvinism of the "patriotic" bourgeois clique of all countries. To refuse this task would mean on the part of the conscious workers to refuse all their liberating and democratic, not to mention socialist, aspirations. With the deepest bitterness, it has to be stated that the socialist parties of the main European countries did not fulfill this task of theirs, and the behavior of the leaders of these parties — especially the German one — borders on direct betrayal of the cause of socialism. At the moment of the greatest world-historical importance, the majority of the leaders of the current, second (1889–1914) Socialist International are trying to replace socialism with nationalism. Due to their behavior, the workers' parties of these countries did not oppose themselves to the criminal behavior of the governments, but called the working class to merge their position with the position of the imperialist governments. The leaders of the International committed treason in relation to socialism, voting for war credits, repeating the chauvinistic ("patriotic") slogans of the bourgeoisie of "their" countries, justifying and defending the war, entering the bourgeois ministries of the warring countries, etc., etc. The most influential socialist leaders and the most influential organs of the socialist press of modern Europe stand on a chauvinistic-bourgeois and liberal [by the way, nothing has fundamentally changed since then], by no means on a socialist point of view. The responsibility for this disgrace of socialism falls primarily on the German Social-Democrats, who were the strongest and most influential party of the Second International" [8, pp. 623-624]. "The collapse of the Second International is the collapse of opportunism [apostasy and conciliation], which was cultivated on the soil of the features of the past (the so-called "peaceful") historical epoch and gained actual domination in the International in recent years. The opportunists had long been preparing this collapse, denying the socialist revolution and replacing it with bourgeois reformism; denying the class struggle with its necessary transformation at certain moments into a civil war and preaching class cooperation...

...It is impossible to fulfill the tasks of socialism at the present time, it is impossible to realize the real international cohesion of workers without a decisive break with opportunism and explaining to the masses the inevitability of its fiasco. [In other words, the war and the inability of the European social-democrats to resist the bourgeoisie, their conciliation with the slogans of the bourgeoisie testify to the collapse of the Second International, which Lenin proposes not to hush up, but on the contrary, to recognize and emphasize. Without demarcation from such a position of the bourgeoisie, it is impossible to fulfill the tasks of socialism — it is absolutely impossible to realize the international cohesion of workers without a break with the apostates and conciliators]. The task of the social-democrats of each country must be in the first place the struggle against the chauvinism of the given country. ...In the current position, it is impossible to determine, from the point of view of the international proletariat, the defeat of which of the two groups of belligerent nations would be the least evil for socialism. But for us, Russian socialdemocrats, there can be no doubt that from the point of view of the working class and the working masses of all the peoples of Russia, the least evil would be the defeat of the tsarist monarchy, the most reactionary and barbaric government, oppressing the greatest number of nations and the greatest mass of the population of Europe and Asia. The immediate political slogan of the social-democrats of Europe should be the formation of a republican United States of Europe, and in contrast to the bourgeoisie... the social-democrats will explain all the falsity and senselessness of this slogan without the revolutionary overthrow of the German, Austrian and Russian monarchies. In Russia, the tasks of the social-democrats, due to the greatest backwardness of this country, which has not yet completed its bourgeois revolution, should still be the three main conditions of a consistent democratic transformation: a democratic republic (with full equality and self-determination of all nations), confiscation of landlords' lands and an 8-hour working day. But in all advanced countries, the war puts the slogan of socialist revolution on the agenda. ...The use by the bourgeoisie of the laws of wartime for completely silencing the proletariat sets before it the unconditional task of creating illegal forms of agitation and organization. ...The Proletarian International has not perished and will not perish. The working masses will create a new International through all obstacles. The current triumph of opportunism is not long-lasting. The more victims of the war there are, the clearer it will be for the working masses of the betrayal of the workers' cause by the opportunists and the necessity to turn weapons against the governments and the bourgeoisie of each country" [8, pp. 626-627].

Summing up all the "pros" and "cons" in the current international situation, i.e., analyzing the probable direction of change in the ratio of the active (contentful) and passive (formal) components in the relation composed of them, and in this regard basing on the necessity of replacing the relation {people (instigated by the bourgeoisie) of one country / people (instigated by the bourgeoisie) of another country} with a more contentful and therefore more correct relation {proletariat of the belligerent countries / bourgeoisie of the belligerent countries}, composed of objectively internally conflicting components, Lenin comes to the conclusion that: "The transformation of the modern imperialist war into a civil war is the only correct proletarian slogan, indicated by the experience of the Commune... and following from all the conditions of the imperialist war between highly developed bourgeois countries. However great the difficulties of such a transformation may seem at one or another moment, socialists will never abandon systematic, persistent, steady preparatory work in this direction, once the war has become a fact. Only on this path will the proletariat be able to break out of its dependence on the chauvinist bourgeoisie and in one form or another more or less quickly take decisive steps along the path to the real freedom of peoples and along the path to socialism" [8, p. 628]. 3. The small article "On the National Pride of the Great Russians" [8, pp. 629-632] with a serious subtext of ambiguous views was written in December 1914. From the content of the article, first of all, it must be understood that the conscious unification of small peoples and nationalities is possible only on the basis of the legalized right of nations to self-determination or a guaranteed opportunity for their free secession. And this content echoes the ideological content of the work "On the Right of Nations to SelfDetermination". Lenin begins his analysis by describing the state of affairs that had developed at that moment, when many correct words "are said, interpreted, shouted... about nationality, about the fatherland... the greatness of the principle of national independence". Meanwhile, he continues, "... before us is a very wide and very deep ideological current, the roots of which are very firmly connected with the interests of the ruling landlords and capitalists of the great-power nations. Tens and hundreds of millions are spent annually on the propaganda of ideas beneficial to these classes..." [8, p. 629]. And it would be improper for us, Lenin writes, as representatives of a greatpower nation, to forget about the enormous significance of the national question, especially at a moment when the tsarist monarchy has put millions of Great Russians and non-Russians under arms to "solve" a whole range of national questions exclusively in accordance with its own interests (the interests of the united nobility, serf-landlords, capitalists), while the same tsarist executioners (noblemen and capitalists) subject our beautiful homeland to oppression, violence, and mockery. Today, in Lenin's words, we are full of a sense of national pride, for the Great Russian nation, overcoming

its slave past, has also created a revolutionary class, has also proven that it is capable of giving humanity great examples of the struggle for freedom and for socialism, and not only great pogroms, rows of gallows, dungeons and great servility before priests, tsars, landlords and capitalists. And we are proud because after 1905 the Great Russian muzhik began to become a democrat and began to overthrow the priest and the landlord. It is precisely because we want a free and independent, sovereign, democratic, republican, proud Great Russia at all costs, building its relations with neighbors on the human principle of equality, and not on the humiliating great nation feudal principle of privileges, that we say: one cannot defend the fatherland otherwise than by fighting with all revolutionary means against the monarchy, landlords and capitalists of one's fatherland, i.e. the worst enemies of our homeland; Great Russians cannot defend the fatherland otherwise than by wishing defeat in any war to tsarism (as the least evil for the overwhelming majority of the population of Great Russia), for tsarism not only oppresses this majority of the population economically and politically, but also demoralizes, humiliates, devalues, prostitutes it, accustoming it to oppressing foreign peoples, accustoming it to cover up its shame with hypocritical, allegedly patriotic phrases. In this connection, from the point of view of the interests of precisely the Great Russian proletariat, it is necessary to conduct a long-term education of the population in the sense of the most resolute, consistent, bold, revolutionary defense of full equality and the right to self-determination of all nations oppressed by the Great Russians. "The interest (not understood in a slavish way) of the national pride of the Great Russians coincides with the socialist interest of the Great Russian (and all other) proletarians" [8, p. 632]. [As already noted earlier, instead of the terms "tsarism", "bourgeoisie", "capitalists", etc., one can safely use terms close in their current meaning: "autocracy", "oligarchy", "plutocracy", "ruling pseudo-elite", "comprador liberal top", etc. This article by V.I. Lenin is such a dynamic creation as all the others: from the initial, less contentful, through change — to a prospective, more contentful result and product. Like any other work by Lenin, this article can be structurally and compositionally laid out in the following template. Initial point (thesis): the position or view of the ruling class (autocracy, tsarism, the emerging and strengthening Great Russian capitalism) on the essence of Russian patriotism; i.e., by stating the skin-deep interests of this class, supported by the propaganda of hypocritical kvass patriotism, one component of the relation is established. Next point in development (antithesis): the essence and definition of Russian patriotism from the point of view of the revolutionary proletariat and its interests. In this way, the other component of the relation is determined. Then, from the found components, a more contentful component is identified (by detecting and verifying the falsity, insincerity and untruth of one component, and vice versa — the authenticity, truthfulness and greater objectivity of the other) and on this basis the correct relation of the found components (synthesis) is

built, possessing the property and ability of self-development, — with simultaneous forecasting of the development prospect of the whole relation as a whole. As a result of comparing two opposite positions and views on Russian patriotism belonging to two different interacting components (classes) in the relation {proletariat/tsarism (autocracy)}, the Bolsheviks give preference to the position of the proletariat and, developing, increasing the contentfulness of this position (by explanations, agitation and propaganda), achieve its predominance in the integral unified relation, which, in the end, in the future and leads to the curtailment of hostilities and the end of the bloody imperialist war. Knowing in advance that in any conflict, in any interaction there are always at least two sides, two components (namely: the contentful and Formal components of development) of one relation, by the nature and intensity of whose interaction the rate and completeness of the increase in the contentfulness of the entire relation are determined, — it is very important to correctly identify and determine these interacting sidescomponents in order to compose a relation from them; a correctly composed relation of two directly interacting sides (two most essential components) gives the most energetic, effective and optimal increase in contentfulness and development of both the components separately and the relation as a whole. Let us give more examples of correctly composed relations. In the relation {wage labor/capital}, the components are inseparable, insignificant without each other, and therefore this relation is not contrived, not sucked out of the finger, but natural and natural. The same applies to such (derivative of this relation and accompanying this relation) relations as {proletariat/bourgeoisie}, {Soviets/Provisional Bourgeois Government}, etc. Correctly composed relations are easily amenable to research and analysis (for example, for the purpose of forecasting the prospects of their change and development)]. [However, the pre-war situation developing in our time seems somewhat different than on the eve of the First World War: it is beneficial for both international and national (to preserve their status quo) capital that Russia should give up its positions, while today's have-nots (wage workers) are inherent in patriotism — as something that is present in them from birth, and not bought or appropriated. Therefore, the slogan or call "for the Motherland!" is sincere and modern as never before precisely for the common people (in the hope of the coming socialist social transformations). The "comprador" top thinks quite differently, there is no trace of patriotism there — on the contrary, the latter is oppressed and despised by all forces and means. The litmus test of Russian patriotism today is the attitude towards Crimea and Donbass. The entire "patriotism" of oligarchs and plutocrats consists in justifying the circumstance that financial-industrial (oligarchic and state-

monopolistic) structures do not "bring" either business or banks to Donbass and partially to Crimea (ignoring the problems of people living in the specified territories) allegedly so as not to harm the whole country. In fact, here one can see simple fears for their personal "hides", which may be spoiled by the notorious Western sanctions. Furthermore, the supply of all types of fuel (and other energy resources), spare parts and components to the armed forces of a hostile state killing people on the territory of Donbass — is also evidence of the same "patriotism" of the same structures. If one digs deep enough, one can find a lot of amazing, shameful and criminal things in the actions and behavior of today's "domestic" bourgeoisie. Even the "Washington regional committee" does not play such a significant role here. In fact, everything boils down to eradicating sabotage and overcoming resistance within the country (treason — that is what it was called in hoary antiquity).] [The policy of the current "ruler" towards Donbass is an ostrich head in the sand, a policy of betrayal and apostasy towards the Russian people living there. Only a cowardly, well-fed, ruling, venal bourgeois top can endure and bear the dictate and mockery of the overseas "hegemon" (unremittingly supervising all decisions on Donbass). A timid "governor" who lacks the will and ability to cope with his "comprador" entourage — this is the reason for the lack of positive effect and the required outcome in solving the problem of Donbass (and Novorossiya as a whole). The key to solving this problem is in Moscow. But an indecisive and venal "ruler" is unlikely to cope with this task; a leader with stronger convictions and righteousness is needed. How many bloody conflicts could have been "nipped in the bud" if one did not follow the wake of the requests and goals of the financial-industrial oligarchate, to whom their own business projects and profits are much closer and more important than the interests of an entire country with its people. People whom he reassured expect protection and help from the current "ruler", and he time and again cowardly tucks his tail in fear of losing his well-being and the favor (support) of his companions — god forbid the Western masters "cut off the oxygen" for him and his entourage as well. Feel the difference: an incorruptible, resolute and fearless revolutionary Lenin on the one hand, and venal, dependent in everything on the overseas "hegemon" (and therefore — timid and indecisive) current "rulers" on the other. On the eve of a quite probable "hot" war, Russia can only be saved by a "purge" of the ranks (according to Lenin) and the neutralization of the "fifth column" (according to Stalin). Unity, firmness and support of the people in this matter are unshakable. The perfect weapon created thanks to the contentful resource of the people — this should be the main argument of the modern "governor" in response to the unprecedented onslaught and pressure from the "hegemon". Instead of

organizing a well-thought-out series of "jabs" (inflicting damage on the overseas "hegemon" in a volume sufficient to demoralize the public consciousness of the "democratizers") and thus showing who is the master in the house by demonstrating the advantages of their latest military equipment and military skill, our "rulers" are incessantly bowing and scraping and bowing. One wonders, who and why then need expensive military "toys", if there is no skill and understanding of how to use them for the benefit of the people? They are needed, first of all, to stop the dictatorship of the aggressor for years to come by inflicting on him a sufficiently disorganizing property and moral damage. They are playing a dishonest game (a game without any rules) against us, and the "rulers", instead of worthily responding with the excellent means at their disposal, shamefully justify themselves and wipe themselves off every time after mocking spits from the completely impudent "hegemon". The people have vested the current "ruler" with power and high powers so that he, on behalf of the people, would not allow the honor and dignity of the people to be trampled. If there is no strength to parry the attacks of the collective West — one should step aside and not cling to power, as Lenin did not cling to it, although he had much more reasons to be vested with this power. Moreover, one should step aside and take with him his diplomatic team of chronic whiners and moaners who do not know how to confront the insolent riff-raff and therefore constantly justify their worthlessness and inefficiency. What the hell is all this military might needed for in the full disposal of the current "governor" (even if it is situationally transferred, for now), if he does not have the will to implement his own policy, does not know how to use this trump card for the benefit of the country and the people, if he stumbles and cowardly retreats at the first shout and askance look from the Western "master"? The timid and indecisive bungler should give way to a stronger, more resourceful, more purposeful, more courageous leader... The understanding and justification of the admissibility and necessity of a Russian force response in this particular case is facilitated by referring to the well-known views of Clausewitz regarding the means of conducting state policy. It is enough to keep in mind that politics (as an instrument of pressure and imposition by various methods of worldview, position, point of view, way of life, etc., etc. of one group of people on another group of people) implies a very extensive set of means and techniques of influencing a partner, competitor, adversary or enemy, among which there may be methods of an encouraging, stimulating, preventive, economic, intimidating, forceful and any other nature. Every political player is free to choose at his discretion the most suitable means of influence from the entire arsenal of available means contrary to the detrimental opinion on the matter that one can and should play only by the rules established by individual players (or a player) claiming exclusivity and absolute dominance. In the times of

unprecedented trampling of all international rights and rules that have come, it is worth, perhaps, finally starting to play by one's own rules (?) or to reconcile and finally allow oneself to be buried alive? Moreover, any exclusivity is alien (foreign) to our international education in the spirit of "man is a friend, comrade and brother to man", and therefore claims to exclusivity and absolutism in this context should be curbed immediately. Contrary to and in opposition to the hypocritical foreign policy of not simple containment and economic pressure, but real strangulation and destruction of Russia — it has long been time for Russia to move on to retaliatory sobering "jabs" (i.e., to counteract by those means that it has in its arsenal and that are most appropriate and effective in the given circumstances). "Well, what else should we expect? We keep begging. We get it on one cheek, and immediately offer the other. And so on to infinity. This will not end with anything good. In the end, if we survive, it is by accident. at the last moment and at a very high cost. As, indeed, we always do... " [14]. Enough to express toothless and spineless diplomatic concern about the insolent antics of the Anglo-Saxon conglomerate and its minions. It is necessary to wean them from arbitrarily hindering the development of anyone by methods of economic, political and any other blockade (i.e., through the so-called sanctions).
