We are talking about the sacred right of every people, every nation to free economic and political development (in this regard, it is appropriate to recall the classical Marxist position, known for its dialectics and indisputable justice, according to which only the free development of each is the condition for the free development of all). Not everyone understands today, unfortunately, that this right still has to be fought for, fought — and fought with all available means. And the fulfillment of this noble mission on an international scale can quite well be taken upon by Russia (possibly with its allies). Our people will understand the necessity and expediency of such preemptive steps and will support them most resolutely. If you do not want to walk as an "outcast" and eventually end up in some kind of "reservation" — you will have to destroy the established "sanction" system once and for all, using the force you have or at least creating a threat of inevitable use of force. And to restore the lost status quo, one must be ready to forcibly bring the "democratizer" out of the state of material and moral comfort that seems to him eternal and cloudless. This must be taken seriously. No one else will do this hard work for us. But. it seems that the current fattening government is quite satisfied with the established order of things. And again we ask ourselves the same rhetorical question: what are all these expensive military means needed for, if not for ensuring the political and economic freedom, independence and sovereignty of the people, who created these security means for exactly this same purpose? After proclaiming such a doctrine and simultaneously carrying out several series of demonstrative preventive "jabs", for many years these genderless

state formations (resembling today the fancied-up strutting "misunderstandings") will not dare to look askance at Russia or raise their voice; at least until they come to their senses and in an urgent reverse order "re-shoe" themselves into the traditional, due to dialectical nature, two-sex (bipolar) execution. And that rooster cry with which they will be hoarse at the moment of receiving a deserved thrashing will once again demonstrate to other peoples the fallacy and inconsistency of all these "democratic mentors", as well as the sham character of any rashly proclaimed hegemony and exclusivity. Paradoxical as it may sound, this is precisely the most effective way to prevent a global war today.] 4. The article "On the Slogan of the United States of Europe" [8, pp. 633-636] was written in August 1915. It deals with the incorrectness (untimeliness) of this slogan in the economic aspect. If in the political aspect this slogan, put in connection with the revolutionary overthrow of the three most reactionary monarchies of Europe (German, Austrian and Russian), is completely invulnerable, there remains the most important question about the economic content and significance of this slogan. "From the point of view of the economic conditions of imperialism, i.e. the export of capital and the division of the world by "advanced" and "civilized" colonial powers, the United States of Europe under capitalism is either impossible or reactionary", since "it amounts to an agreement on the division of colonies" [8, pp. 634635]. Capital has become international and monopolistic; the world is divided among a handful of "great powers" succeeding in great robbery and oppression of nations. In the era of the highest development of capitalism, a handful of great powers have organized the robbery of about a billion of the Earth's population. [Isn't this relevant today, and even to a greater extent? You read Lenin's article more than a century old, and it seems as if it is written about our time!]. A different organization (i.e. voluntary renunciation of colonies, spheres of influence, export of capital) is impossible under capitalism, as are a different basis and a different principle of division, except for force. Capitalism is private ownership of the means of production and the anarchy of production. To preach a "fair" division of income on such a basis is the dull-wittedness of a philistine. A billionaire cannot share income with anyone else otherwise than in proportion "according to capital". Hence — it cannot be divided otherwise than "by force" [i.e. by force methods]. And force changes with the course of economic development, and to check the actual force of a developing capitalist state, there is and can be no other means except war. Under capitalism [due to the anarchy and spontaneity of market production], uniform growth of economic development of individual farms and individual states is impossible, and therefore under capitalism there are no other means of restoring from time to time the disturbed equilibrium and eliminating disproportions than crises in industry and wars in politics. War is not in contradiction with the foundations of private property, but is a consequence of the direct and inevitable development of

these foundations (i.e. the need for periodic revision and redistribution of the accumulations of private property). Thus, based on what has been said on the modern economic basis, i.e. under capitalism, the United States of Europe would mean the organization of reaction to delay the faster development of America. The times when the cause of democracy and the cause of socialism were connected only with Europe have passed irrevocably. Under such conditions, the slogan "United States of the World" would be more correct and relevant, however, it could give rise to an incorrect interpretation of the question in the impossibility of the victory of socialism in one country [8, p. 636]. The unevenness of economic and political development is an absolute law of capitalism. It follows from this that the victory of socialism is possible initially in a few or even in one single capitalist country. The political form of society in which the proletariat wins, overthrowing the bourgeoisie, will be a democratic republic, increasingly centralizing the forces of the proletariat of a given nation or given nations in the struggle against states that have not yet transitioned to socialism. On the basis of the foregoing, Lenin comes to the conclusion (makes a diagnosis) about the impossibility of destroying classes without the dictatorship of the oppressed class and the impossibility of the free unification of nations in socialism without a more or less long, persistent struggle of socialist republics with backward states. By virtue of these considerations, a conclusion is made about the incorrectness of the slogan of the United States of Europe at this particular historical moment [8, p. 636], since it will be expressed in the postponement of the beginning of the socialist revolution. [Contentful essence of the socialist revolution, as noted in the article, is realized and presented not in the form of a one-time act, but in a whole series (epoch) of forms of political revolutions and counter-revolutions, violent political and economic upheavals, aggravated class struggle, civil wars, etc. With regard to social transformations in Russia, the ultimately accomplished socialist revolution with an aim at building a classless society is just an initial stage or the first stage of the implementation of the socialist movement, which in the future will only expand, deepen and accelerate. At the same time, intermittency, stops, sharp turns, jerks, declines, rises (i.e. sections of visible regression and backward movement: crises, wars, coups, revolutions, counter-revolutions, etc.) are quite natural for this kind of movement and only confirm the spiral-cyclical nature of social development (as well as universal development as a whole). It is the striving to achieve the highest contentfulness by the forms-carriers of social progress that will serve as the main factor causing periodic reference to the theoretical and practical developments generalizing the

results and experience of all previous stages of the mentioned revolutionary movement. Here Lenin informs the reader that Switzerland, for example (and some similar "successful" and "prosperous" dwarf state formations), is the result of an agreement of international capital on the arrangement of completely neutral quiet "coves" and "harbors" for the reliable storage of funds, organization of recreation and tourism of the fat cats. It was also conceived by the mighty of this world as a place where the owners of large capital could catch their breath and rest from "righteous" labors next to their savings and accumulations. Thus, the defiantly colorful and openly transparent seemingly shell of some state structure turns out to be just a screen, shielding from the eyes of outsiders the inconspicuous true essence and purpose of such a structure, once erected by the efforts of a small handful of interested persons. Class solidarity, guarding the interests of the masters of life, will never allow the transition to transparency of relations of production, accumulation and consumption under capitalism. However, everything related to the contentful resource of one or another form and the sources of its accumulations will always be under lock and key and seals]. 5. The article "The Military Program of the Proletarian Revolution" [8, pp. 751-761] was written in German in September 1916, and was printed only a year later. Then it was intended for Swiss, Swedish and Norwegian left Social-Democrats. The publication was preceded by the following foreword: "Nowadays, when Lenin is one of those figures of the Russian revolution who are most often spoken about, the following article of this old iron revolutionary, outlining a significant part of his political program, is of particular interest" [8, p. 834]. In the Russian language in Russia, this work was first published only in 1929. Structurally and compositionally, the article is built according to the same very typical stencil: analysis of the current situation (state of affairs); identification of the interacting sides in their relation, both old and new (transformed and developed); determination of the active, leading, leading component in these relations, especially in the new one; forecasting the direction of variability of both the components-constituents themselves in this new relation and the relation as a whole. The appearance of this article was caused by the circumstance that along with the justified and fair opposition to the false slogan of the social-chauvinists about the "defense of the fatherland" in the ongoing imperialist war, voices began to be heard in favor of replacing the old point of the Social-Democratic program "arming of the people" (formation of a "militia") with the new point "disarmament". "The main argument is that the demand for disarmament is the most consistent expression of the struggle against all militarism and against any war." But in this argument lies the main fallacy of the supporters of disarmament: socialists,

without ceasing to be socialists, cannot be against any war, and in particular a revolutionary war, a national liberation war, a civil war (as well as the so-called "defensive" wars of the victorious proletariat against the bourgeoisie of other countries) [8, pp. 751-752]. And from a scientific point of view, it would be completely incorrect and unrevolutionary to bypass or obscure the most important and most difficult thing: the necessity of suppressing the resistance of the bourgeoisie during the transition to socialism. And in wars of this kind, to recognize "defense of the fatherland" means to consider these wars "just" and corresponding to the interests of the proletariat, since a reverse invasion of the bourgeoisie cannot be excluded in any war and, accordingly, resistance to such an invasion must be organized. [Establishing a new relation with new components in this new relation in place of the relation {Entente (Great Britain, France, Russia) / Triple Alliance (Germany, Austria-Hungary, Italy)} (since new forces that entered into a new confrontation and war were awakened: {proletarian plus national revolution / bourgeois plus national-chauvinistic war}) requires finding a new correlation of these new components in the indicated relation, i.e. finding a new balance of the new forces that have entered the arena. Thus, analyzing the predatory conflict through the prism of the previously incorrectly composed relation {people (instigated by the bourgeoisie) of one country / people (instigated by the bourgeoisie) of another country} and showing the hypocrisy and duplicity of the slogans of such falsely understood patriotism, Lenin moves on to a more correctly composed and substantiated relation from such components {proletariat of the conflicting countries / bourgeoisie of the conflicting countries} and already, proceeding from this new relation, one has to look for the correlation of its components, the direction of variability of this correlation and the direction of change (new forms) of the relation itself]. Theoretically, Lenin says, it would be completely erroneous to forget that any war is only a continuation of politics by other means: the current imperialist war is a continuation of the imperialist policy of two groups of great powers, and this policy is generated and fed by the totality of relations of the imperialist epoch. But the same epoch must necessarily generate and feed the policy of struggle against national oppression, and the struggle of the proletariat against the bourgeoisie, and therefore — the possibility and inevitability of revolutionary national uprisings and wars, on the one hand, and wars and uprisings of the proletariat against the bourgeoisie, on the other hand, as well as the unification of both types of revolutionary wars [8, p. 754] To this, Lenin adds the following consideration: one cannot forget that we live in a class society and there is and can be no other way out of it than through the class struggle. In any class society (based on slavery, serfdom, wage labor), the oppressing class is armed (the military and the militia are taken into account — otherwise, in the absence of means of suppression, social inequality or imbalance could not be maintained for an indefinitely long

time). The arming of the bourgeoisie against the proletariat is one of the most basic facts of capitalist society. And to proclaim the slogan of disarmament for the proletariat against this background is tantamount to abandoning the point of view of the class struggle. On the contrary, the arming of the proletariat is the only possible tactic of the revolutionary class, a tactic dictated by the entire objective development of capitalist militarism, prescribed by this development. And therefore our slogan should be the arming of the proletariat [i.e. increasing the contentfulness of the form (the proletarian class) at a certain — force — sublevel of this form] in order to defeat and disarm the bourgeoisie. Saying that "capitalist society was and always is horror without end", Lenin masterfully notes the trend of piling up contentful accumulations conditioned by military actions, leading to "an end with horror" (a terrible end). And at the same time, contrary to established beliefs, behind this "terrible end" he sees the emergence and emergence of a new form and, accordingly, a new perspective, consisting in the fact that "before everyone's eyes, by the forces of the bourgeoisie itself, the only legitimate and revolutionary war is being prepared, namely: civil war against the imperialist bourgeoisie" [8, p. 755] with a new contentful turn in the course of the upcoming contentful transformations and transformations. "Now militarization penetrates all public life" [8, p. 756]: today the imperialist bourgeoisie militarizes the entire male population, including youth, and in the plans for tomorrow — the militarization of women as well. Regarding this, one can say: so much the better, the sooner and closer to the armed uprising against capitalism. Mastering military science is necessary for the proletariat not in order to shoot at their brothers, workers of other countries, as is done in the current war, but in order to fight against the bourgeoisie of their own country, to put an end to exploitation, poverty and wars not by kind wishes and hopes, by statements of condemnation and concern, but by victory over the bourgeoisie and disarming it, which is achieved only by waging war against war, i.e. by civil confrontation and civil war. The main disadvantage of the demand for disarmament lies precisely in the fact that all concrete questions of the revolution are left unattended (ignored). The "disarmament" of the proletariat (people), Lenin insists, professed by conciliators with the bourgeoisie of all stripes (opportunists, following Lenin's terminology), is precisely an escape from the vile reality, and not a struggle against it [the fundamental transformation of the established reality is possible only by decisive actions with weapons in hand]. The struggle against imperialism, not connected with the struggle against opportunism, is an empty phrase and a deception. The main danger emanating from all varieties of opportunism [i.e. conciliation and apostasy] is that they hush up (or talk out) the question of the connection of the ongoing war with the revolution and thus come out with a new absurd slogan: revolution without weapons [8, pp. 757-758]. Furthermore, the bourgeoisie of all great imperialist powers has become so reactionary [i.e. striving to avoid crisis and restore the lost balance in affairs] and so imbued with the striving for world domination [i.e. to achieve their contentful superiority] that any war on the part of the bourgeoisie of these

countries can only be reactionary. The proletariat should be not only against any such war, but should wish for the defeat of "its" government in such wars and use it for a revolutionary armed uprising, if an uprising with the aim of preventing the war fails [8, pp. 758-759]. With the conciliators (fighters for the disarmament of the people) one cannot fight by means of programs alone, but only by means of relentless observation to ensure that they are actually implemented. The largest fatal mistake of the bankrupt Second International was that the word did not correspond to the deed, that a habit of hypocrisy and shameless revolutionary phrase was cultivated. Disarmament as a social idea is generated, obviously, by the special "peaceful" living conditions of individual small states, which have stood aside from the bloody world road of wars for quite a long time and hope to remain so. The petty-bourgeois desire to stay away from the great battles of world history, to use their relatively monopolistic position for passivity — this is the objective social environment that can provide the idea of disarmament with a certain success and a certain spread in some small states. Of course, this aspiration is reactionary and rests on illusions, for imperialism one way or another drags small states into the whirlpool of the world economy and world politics. [This "wonderful" side or feature of the arrangement of "well-fed" lazy, arrogantly haughty communities and societies must always be taken into account: their impudently-pushy essence can be overcome by active persistent impact on them until the moment of their scattering to their shelters; brave and "pushy" at first, they are not able to withstand a long siege and continuous onslaught, leading to their physiological discomfort or threatening complete physical destruction]. Switzerland, for example, its imperialist situation prescribes two lines of the workers' movement: 1) the opportunists in alliance with the bourgeoisie strive to turn Switzerland into a republican-democratic monopolistic union to profit from the tourists of the imperialist bourgeoisie and so that this "peaceful" monopolistic position (a quiet "harbor") is as profitable as possible [here lie the roots of the success, prosperity and well-being of neutral dwarf states like Switzerland and the like. The quiet "backwaters" of Switzerland and similar small lands, where one can sit out and hide one's savings in days of great crises and upheavals, were prepared by the rich for themselves in advance — that is why these "areas" received such a lush bloom in inviolability]; 2) the real Social-Democrats of Switzerland seek to use the relative freedom and "international" position of Switzerland to help the close alliance of revolutionary elements of the workers' parties of Europe to win. [Both lines are conditioned by the special position and the historically established status quo of a small country]. Proceeding from these two pronounced tendencies, it becomes clear, Lenin sums up, that objectively "disarmament" is a specifically national program of small states, and by no means an international program of revolutionary international Social-Democracy [8, pp. 759-761].

And then the hottest pre-revolutionary times came. One would think that the rhetoric and style of Lenin's articles should have become chaotic and less ordered; however, no — all the same sustained and balanced tone, the same unhurried, patient deep analysis, the unhurried course of the presentation of thought, which lead to the most correct remarkable conclusions, based by no means on unfounded calls and slogans, but on the ability to see the general objective behind individual specific facts, i.e. to guess and expose the regular in the process of building a dialectically verified scheme of analysis. 6. Small in volume, but extremely rich in Content and permeated through and through with the spirit of dialectics, the article "On Dual Power" [9, pp. 16-18] was written in April 1917. The fundamental question of any revolution, Lenin writes at the beginning of the article, is the question of power in the state. But the remarkable peculiarity of the current revolution is that it has created dual power, which no one thought of or could think of before. Therefore, the old "formulas" of Bolshevism must be supplemented and corrected. Dual power consists in the fact that next to the Provisional Government of the bourgeoisie, another government has emerged: the Soviets of Workers' and Soldiers' Deputies. Thus, there is a relation composed or formed of two sidescomponents: {"growing" government of the people in the face of the Soviets of Workers' and Soldiers' (i.e. peasants dressed in soldiers' uniforms) Deputies / government of the bourgeoisie}. The peculiarity of the people's power consists in the fact that it: firstly, represents a revolutionary dictatorship, since its source is not the law, but the direct initiative of the masses from below, i.e. revolutionary "seizure" [the source of power is some internal contentful (energetic) constituent of reality, determining the deep striving of the people (or a group of people); power itself is the external form of acquiring this contentful constituent]; secondly, it carries out the replacement of the police and the army by the armed people; thirdly, it places officials and the bureaucracy under the control of the armed people with the possibility of replacing personnel at the first demand of the people and paying labor no higher than the usual wage of a good worker [this extremely important and remarkable move (technique) of the nascent power was invented by no means by Lenin and the Bolsheviks, but was only successfully and justifiably implemented by the Bolshevik government after the victory of the Great October Socialist Revolution. This was an act approved by the people, in confirmation of the establishment of a more just distribution of the worked-out product. By the way, both the "Soviets" and our proud word "comrade" and many other similar fashionable terms and concepts of that time are also not inventions of Lenin and company, but verbal constructions corresponding to the achievements of the advanced enlightened thought of that time, skillfully used by the Bolsheviks in organizing the public arrangement of a new type]. Thus, a state is formed according to the type of the Paris Commune of 1871. At the same time, this is only an embryonic power, for it still yields positions to the bourgeoisie. The reason is the insufficient consciousness and organization of the proletarians and peasants. The uncertainty of the new power is revealed by the simple question it poses: should the Provisional Government be overthrown

immediately? Lenin answers this question as follows: 1) the government must be overthrown — for it is oligarchic, bourgeois, and not nationwide, it can give neither peace, nor bread, nor freedom; 2) however, it cannot be overthrown now, for it is held together by a formal and actual agreement with the Soviets of Workers' Deputies; 3) it cannot be overthrown in the usual way at all, for it relies on the support of the bourgeoisie by the Council of Workers' Deputies, which is the only possible revolutionary government, directly expressing the consciousness and will of the majority of workers and peasants (and above, better than this type of government, as the Soviets of workers, farm laborers, peasant, soldier deputies, humanity has not developed and we still do not know). Hence, "to become power, conscious workers must win the majority over to their side: as long as there is no violence against the masses, there is no other way to power" [9, p. 18]. The bourgeoisie is for the sole power of the bourgeoisie, while the conscious workers are for the sole power of the Soviets of workers, farm laborers, peasant and soldier deputies, for sole power prepared by the clarification of proletarian consciousness and its liberation from the influence of the bourgeoisie, and not by adventures [i.e. the current relation from the indicated components (see above) must now be enriched with content, saturated with contentfulness by feeding its upper component, the "numerator", with Content, in order to strengthen it as the leading component by clarifying and enriching the proletarian consciousness]. Thus, as we see, again guided by the dialectical method, Lenin precisely and unambiguously determines both the relation of the main componentsconstituents of development at this particular stage of the revolutionary movement, and the actual class correlation of forces-components within the relation composed by him, which determines (we are talking about the correlation) the tasks for the near-term development of the revolutionary situation and the direction of the subsequent movement or change of this relation as a whole. [This article is a classic example of skillful, filigree mastery of dialectics and its effective application]. 7. In the article "Three Crises" [9, pp. 124-127], written by Lenin in July 1917, various forms of the course of the revolution are considered (primarily from class positions). Three objective political crises that determined the nature of the course of the revolutionary process of that period served as the basis for lies and slander [i.e. numerous "fakes", as they would say in our time] about the artificial organization of these crises by the hands of the Bolsheviks. Objectively common to all three crises is the "overflowing" discontent of the people with the bourgeoisie and its government, which allows us to see behind all this the incessant class struggle. Individually in these crises — these are different forms of conducting (manifestation) of the mentioned class struggle. In all three cases — this is a demonstration, but: in the first case — stormy-spontaneous, unorganized, leading to shooting at the demonstrators by the Black Hundreds; in

the second case — appointed by the Bolsheviks, canceled and postponed to a new date, a general more powerful demonstration with the predominance of Bolshevik slogans; in the third case — a spontaneous rise of counter-revolution contrary to the efforts of the Bolsheviks. An instructive conclusion from the events under consideration, as Lenin notes, is "that all three crises show us a certain. new in the history of our revolution, form of a demonstration of a more complex type, with a wave-like movement [from extreme to extreme], a rapid rise and a steep fall, with an aggravation of the revolution and counter-revolution, with the "washing out", for a more or less long time, of the middle elements" [9, pp. 125-126]. Lenin masterfully notes that in such periods of acute political crises, the contradictions between the main "players" of the revolutionary action are aggravated [in this case between the components of the very main relation {proletariat/bourgeoisie}], while some intermediate "middle elements" accompanying every turmoil are pushed aside: "But the point is that this is not an ordinary demonstration, it is much more than a demonstration and less than a revolution. This is an explosion of revolution and counter-revolution together, this is a sharp, sometimes almost sudden "washing out" of the middle elements in connection with the stormy manifestation of proletarian and bourgeois [elements]" [9, p. 126]. The objective clash of two opposing sides, two classes — the proletariat and the bourgeoisie — corresponds to the political struggle of their parties — the Bolsheviks and the Cadets. At the same time, the "middle elements" accuse the Cadets that they facilitate the work of the Bolsheviks, and the Bolsheviks that they facilitate the work of the Cadets. Now, Lenin says, if instead of political names we substitute class ones, then we get the dreams of the petty bourgeoisie about the "disappearance" of the class struggle between the proletariat and the bourgeoisie. And then follows an objective and dialectically verified conclusion that no Bolsheviks in the world could have "caused" not only three, but even one "popular movement", if the deepest economic and political reasons did not set the proletariat in motion, that no Cadets and monarchists together can cause any movement "from the right", if equally deep reasons did not create the counter-revolutionary nature of the bourgeoisie as a class [9, pp. 126-127]. This is an indicator of the petty bourgeoisie's misunderstanding of the mechanism or essence of the class struggle of the proletariat against the bourgeoisie. Since the vast majority of the country's population is pettybourgeois in their life situation and even more so in their ideas, and in the confrontation with large capital (in the face of banks and syndicates in the first place) the urban proletariat is not yet capable of immediately winning over the majority of the semi-proletarians and other strata of the population, such an objectively formed situation [corresponding to the objectively formed at the current moment correlation of force-components in the main class relation

{proletariat/bourgeoisie}] will be expressed in the inevitability of crises similar to the three crises under consideration in their three forms. [Economic and political crises (the confrontation of two deeply non-equilibrium components of the known relation {proletariat/bourgeoisie}) set in motion both the revolutionary proletariat (with its form of government or power — the Soviets of workers', soldiers' and peasants' deputies) on the one hand, and the counter-revolutionary bourgeoisie (with its form of government — the Provisional Government) on the other. At the same time, the "middle elements" (petty bourgeoisie) with their parties (the Socialist-Revolutionaries and the Mensheviks) lash both of them for "rocking" the common boat. Behind the forms of the erupting crises, Lenin clearly sees their essence — the interaction, struggle, conflict of two components of development, two main sides of the relation {proletariat/bourgeoisie}. The middle elements or petty bourgeoisie (including the intelligentsia) are always an oscillating stratum, which, depending on the circumstances, can play the role of both a revolutionary-active and a counterrevolutionary element. And such oscillations from one pole to the other are inevitable, which leads to both a change in the established correlation between the sides and a change in the relation itself as a whole. That is why, in the final analysis, for the implementation of a full-fledged uprising, the proletariat today lacks a "critical mass" and therefore the topical (for now) task is to win over the oscillating semi-proletarian mass to its proletarian side, including the poorest peasantry and the petty-bourgeois stratum of the country's population. In this case, we are talking about the need to increase the contentfulness of the "upper" component (the "numerator" of the given relation, i.e. the consciousness and organization of the proletariat) in the actually formed relation of components {proletariat/bourgeoisie} by all possible means, including explanatory work, agitation, and propaganda.] 8. Lenin's attitude to his probable arrest in the hot July days of 1917 will be shown by the example of the publications of two of his small articles "Where is the Power and Where is the Counter-Revolution" [9, pp. 119-123] and "On the Question of the Appearance in Court of the Bolshevik Leaders" [9, pp. 128-129]. In the first article, the false statement of a certain former member of the State Duma about the readiness of Lenin and the Bolshevik party to conclude a separate peace with Germany is analyzed, despite the fact that Lenin, on the contrary, has always and unconditionally rejected a separate peace with Germany in the most resolute and irrevocable manner [9, p. 121]. This same source reported that the Bolshevik leaders received money from German agents. At the same time, everyone perfectly understood that if this information were true, the General Staff would by no means allow its premature leakage to the yellow press and would not hesitate for a minute to arrest the Bolshevik top. In fact, as it turned out later, taking advantage of dual power (or rather, the powerlessness of that period), this information for the purpose of discrediting the

Bolsheviks was spread by the so-called behind-the-scenes counter-revolution — the Cadets, well-known circles of the General Staff and the Black Hundred press. "The counter-revolution takes advantage of the powerlessness, uniting the Cadets with the well-known commanding tops of the army and with the Black Hundred press" [9, p. 123]. The second article on the question of the appearance of the Bolshevik leaders in court brings on this account, as one would expect, two opinions: to appear and not to appear. At first, Lenin very seriously considered the possibility of his presence in court, since such a step would allow using the court as a tribune to clarify the essence of the Bolsheviks' position and additional agitation, to which Ilyich always approached very scrupulously. Moreover, Lenin was by no means a man of a timid disposition to be frightened by threats from the Provisional Government. However, after a comprehensive collective discussion of this option, the VI Congress of the RSDLP(b) unanimously adopted a resolution in which it spoke out against the appearance of Lenin and a number of other comrades in court, foreseeing a possible massacre of the Bolshevik leadership [or, as they would say today, the neutralization of the entire party asset]. "If we assume that in Russia there is and a correct government is possible, a correct court, the convening of the Constituent Assembly is probable, then one can come to a conclusion in favor of appearance" [9, p. 128]. However, due to the absence of power [the established dual power as an equivalent to powerlessness], there was neither a correct court, nor a correct government, nor a Constituent Assembly in Russia at that time. The court is supposedly fair, the court will figure it out, the court will be public, the people will understand — "a reasoning naive to childishness". It is not a court, but a persecution of internationalists, that is what Kerensky and Co. needed. Therefore, "let the internationalists work illegally as much as they can, but let them not make the stupidity of voluntary appearance!" [9, p. 129]. [How honestly, responsibly and devotedly Lenin treated his cause, involving the implementation of the socialist idea in life, how convinced he was of the rightness of this idea, can be judged by the memoirs of his contemporaries, even from among those who were not Bolsheviks and never shared their views. The Menshevik F. Dan, for example, once remarked with irritation: "...There is no longer such a person who would be busy with the revolution 24 hours a day, who would have no other thoughts except the thought of the revolution, and who even sees only the revolution in his dreams. Just try to cope with such a one" [13, p. 6]. Can Lenin's devotion to the common cause, his lack of greed and purposefulness be compared with the qualities of today's "rulers" or the so-called Duma communists and all other "zealots for the people" — with their camouflaged financial assets, unaffordable real estate and "treacherously" tinted bank accounts (including foreign ones). It's laughable, really. Can the current "rulers" and their ilk compare with Lenin's pleiad of state leaders, who descended into

the grave in the same frock coats in which they came into the revolutionary political movement, and then into power? Every "ruler" has, by and large, only two options for "exit" from power structures when the earth is shaking underfoot and power is slipping from his hands. The first option: to inconspicuously "merge", saving one's "hide" (in modern language), changing into a woman's dress or (which is tantamount to the same thing) timely pulling a retirement pajamas on oneself; the second option: again, saving one's precious hide, to apply force against the rebels. Because his own "priceless" life and excessive ambitions are at stake, the modern "ruler" readily and without hesitation is ready to deploy against his own people artillery, tanks, MLRS and aircraft, perfectly realizing that all this is a weapon of group destruction. And how should one treat such a nonentity after that? And against such a background, someone has the tongue to make claims to Lenin, who was not smeared and not noticed in anything similar (i.e., never clung to power and did not allow anything of the kind and close, which cannot be said about many other characters — once former and now residing in power). In this regard, Lenin was always guided by the principle formulated in one of his reports: "A revolution is worth something only if it knows how to defend itself...". It was about defense and the struggle against counter-revolution, which in every way and by all methods, including terror, sought to prevent the formation of a new social order (new socio-economic formation) and the creative transformation of the country: "The revolution was an awakening to a new life of millions. In February and March, these millions did not know what they were going to continue the slaughter into which they were driven by the tsars and Kerenskys and the purpose of which was exposed only in December by the Bolshevik government. This turning point has come; it changes the strength of the revolution. If these masses have now created a new discipline in the Red Army, not the discipline of the stick and the landlord's, but the discipline of the Soviets of workers' and peasants' deputies; if they are now going to the greatest self-sacrifice; if among them a new cohesion has developed, it is because for the first time in the consciousness and experience of tens of millions a new, socialist discipline is being born and has been born, the Red Army has been born. It was born only when these tens of millions of people saw from their own experience that they themselves had overthrown the landlords and capitalists, that a new life was being built, that they themselves had begun to build it and that they would build this life if a foreign invasion did not interfere" [12, pp. 122-123]. For the current "rulers", for example, special cycles of television programs are organized so that they explain their most "humane" and "sweet-tempered" impulses, talk about their grandiose plans, diligently winding flour products of well-known names onto the ear shells of undiscriminating or confused listeners. And as a result or in the dry residue, there will still be the same two options for "exit" from "power": either quietly "merge" in women's underwear or retirement pajamas, so that no one can "catch" the poor fellow for the miscalculations made

in foreign and domestic policy, or drown everything in human blood for the sake of one's own salvation. And there should be no illusions about this. Wretches of all stripes who have got into power use all possible means to stay afloat or get out dry. Meanwhile, every such "ruler" should see at the end of his term of office not a comfortable armchair with a woolen blanket, but a block with an axe; only then can one hope that he will check every step he takes and make every decision in the name of and for the benefit of the people, who have temporarily delegated their power powers to him]. To our great regret, many modern "rulers" (including Russian ones) still, as in the past, build up for themselves, cherish and nurture oligarchicplutocratic states: a) according to their perverted ideas of the ideal (in the sense of perfect) world order; b) as a natural product and indicator of their lack of education and bad taste. Moreover, to develop or "further educate" such a dwarf ruler due to his clinical limitations, as a rule, is not possible. All, outwardly looking like good intentions of such a worthless ruler, turn out, in the final analysis, to be just a cover for the manifestation of private property instincts, petty deeds and self-serving interests of the oligarchic-plutocratic environment, which serves for the caricature ruler as a kind of "airbag". It is amusing to watch how an ordinary figure, after the insignificant sycophantic entourage "blows" into his ears about his non-ordinariness and exclusiveness, suddenly begins to claim the role of a large-scale theorist and teacher of the nation, practically not understanding the "sublime matters", looking down with disdain and hostility at the past of the country, leveling and calling into question what was created by truly great predecessors (during the years of Soviet power and under the leadership of the Bolsheviks hated by him). It is surprising how a "ruler" with an inadequate assessment of the phenomenal past of his people can throw dust in the eyes of his fellow citizens for so long. In contrast to today's "leaders" and "rulers", the Bolsheviks (Lenin's closest comrades) evoke sympathy not only due to their education, erudition, lively mind, sober views of the world, but also thanks to iron will ("one could make nails from these people — there would be no stronger nails in the world"), romanticism (albeit naive to some extent), unwavering faith in the future, in man, in his creative potential. The Bolsheviks from the pleiad of Lenin's closest comrades selflessly, at the cost of their freedom, health, and in many cases their lives, — managed to break through a gap in such a thickness of millennial human ignorance, deception and violence against ordinary people that it is simply impossible to reassess their contribution to the formation of a new worldview (world outlook and world order) and, therefore, one can admire them endlessly.

As for Lenin (as a politician and statesman), the decisions made by him and the actions performed by him must always and first of all be considered in the context of those tasks and problems that in those times, without exaggeration, simply "took by the throat". To understand the logic of decisions and behavior of great politicians, one must consider this behavior and decisions in accordance with the events and circumstances, conditions and realities that, like a certain spatial environment or atmosphere, fill those or other times. By the way, modern political wretches, as already noted above ("true democrats" — by their own standards), for the sake of preserving their power or at the slightest threat to this power of theirs (as a rule, even in insignificant in historicism circumstances) easily use the power appropriated by them for the isolation or elimination of dissenters and freethinkers. One must possess the true "greatness" and "enormity" of Lenin's nature to leave after himself such scanty autobiographical information from personal life, preferences, hobbies, etc. — unlike our current "rulers" striving to immortalize (with the help of court "chroniclers") and capture on film or "in digits" (in the form of a cinematic epic of insignificant episodes) every step of their feigned path under the pharisaic slogan "all life is service to the people"]. 9. "Undoubtedly, the most important question of any revolution," V.I. Lenin begins his article "One of the Fundamental Questions of the Revolution" [9, pp. 208-214], dated September 1917. — is the question of state power; everything is determined by the class in whose hands the power is: one cannot bypass or postpone the question of power, for this is the fundamental question determining everything in the development of the revolution, in its foreign and internal policy. However, the revolution has lost half a year due to the hesitation of the parties of the Socialist-Revolutionaries and the Mensheviks, who are the avant-garde of the petty bourgeoisie with its economic instability in the struggle between labor and capital. If in these great half-years the petty-bourgeois democracy has learned anything, then it is necessary to immediately create a stable unhesitating power; if not, then the revolution has perished, and only the victorious uprising of the proletariat can save it" [9, p. 208]. Only the power of the Soviets can be stable, deliberately relying on the majority of the people, no matter how the lackeys of the bourgeoisie lie. Only Soviet power could be stable and could ensure the broad development of the revolution and the peaceful struggle of parties within the Soviets. Until such power is created, indecision, hesitation, endless "crises of power", a comedy of ministerial reshuffles are inevitable. But the slogan "All Power to the Soviets" is often understood completely incorrectly in the sense of "a ministry from the parties of the Soviet majority". In the indicated sense — this is just a change in the composition of ministers while preserving the entire old apparatus of government power, incapable of carrying out serious reforms.

In fact, "All Power to the Soviets" means a radical reworking of the entire old state bureaucratic apparatus that hinders everything democratic, the elimination of this apparatus and its replacement by a new, people's apparatus of the Soviets, i.e. the organized and armed majority of the people, workers, soldiers, peasants, providing the initiative and independence to the majority of the people not only in the election of deputies, but also in governing the state, in carrying out reforms and transformations [9, pp. 209-210]. The entire history of bourgeois-parliamentary and bourgeois-constitutional countries, Lenin reminds, shows that a change of ministers means very little, for the real work of management is in the hands of a gigantic army of officials, which is thoroughly imbued with an anti-democratic spirit, is connected by millions of threads with the bourgeoisie and is dependent on it. This army is connected by relations of rank worship, known privileges of "state" service, and the upper ranks of this army through the mediation of shares and banks are completely enslaved by financial capital, representing its agents, conductors of its interests and influence. [Isn't it somehow "peculiarly fresh" sounding today!]. To carry out reforms to limit the right of capital and the right of "sacred private property", such a state apparatus is absolutely incapable [9, p. 210]. It was so and it will be so as long as the bourgeois system holds and the old, bourgeois, bureaucratic, state apparatus remains inviolable. The Soviets of workers', soldiers', peasants' deputies are valuable precisely in that they represent a new, immeasurably higher, incomparably more democratic type of state apparatus [9, p. 211]. Here Lenin calls for not being afraid of the initiative and independence of the masses, who are capable of revolutionary transformations and replacing the old state apparatus. Experience has shown that there is no middle ground: either all power to the Soviets both in the center and on the local level, or the landlords and capitalists hinder everything and restore their power. The situation in Russia is such that the unprecedented hardships and misfortunes of war, the formidable danger of collapse and hunger themselves suggested a way out and put forward as urgent reforms and transformations: the bread monopoly, control over production and distribution, limiting the issue of paper money, the correct exchange of bread for goods, etc. The only class that can show the courage and decisiveness that is lacking today is the proletariat; at the same time, the establishment of a firm course for reforms is impossible without the establishment of the dictatorship of the proletariat. And, finally, the brilliant Lenin's conclusion: "Only the dictatorship of the proletarians and the poorest peasants is able to break the resistance of the capitalists, show truly majestic courage and decisiveness of power, ensure for itself enthusiastic, selfless, truly heroic support of the masses both in the army and in the peasantry" [9, p. 214]. The only thing that could make further development gradual, peaceful and calm is the transfer of power to the Soviets, which would mean the transfer of management of the country

and the economy to the workers and peasants, whom no one would dare to resist and who would quickly learn from experience to correctly distribute land, products, bread. [So, again we return to the initial, revealed in the course of Lenin's analysis of the situation relation of component-classes {proletariat/bourgeoisie}, by the interaction of components in which some of their correlation is realized, demonstrating the supremacy, priority or prevalence of one component in relation to another (which determines the power of one class over another). The relation {proletariat/bourgeoisie}, filled with new content, in the form of the transformed, transformed relation {Councils of workers', peasants' and soldiers' deputies / bourgeois Provisional Government} shows the ability of development (displacement) of this relation towards the "upper" component (the "numerator"), which corresponds to the strengthening of its activity, initiative and readiness for revolutionary transformations, to the perception and mastery of new forms of management, productive forces, social production, etc., etc., and which, in turn, determines the acceleration of the contentful development of the entire relation as a whole. The emergence of a new correlation of class forces to a new level of contentfulness (in the form of a new relation) — this is what the Socialist-Revolutionary and Menshevik leaders are afraid of. Again, the disruption of reforms by the bourgeoisie is the result of inactivity or lack of sufficient control over the situation on the part of the revolutionary component. The full development of the indicated relation as a whole is predicted by the transfer of power to the Soviets due to the readiness of the revolutionary popular masses for this. In the same way, in modern conditions, without tough measures and a revolutionary approach to solving the most urgent state tasks, one cannot do without, no matter how hard one tries and no matter how one "blurs" the eyes of one's fellow citizens. If the current "ruler" hopes that everything will resolve itself, he is deeply mistaken in this regard — women have not yet learned to give birth to such oligarchs, magnates, majors and other "appropriators" who would voluntarily renounce what was earned in the sweat of someone else's face and in favor of their own face appropriated. It is a pity, nevertheless, that the current "governor" is not a resolute revolutionary. The reverse side of any revolution, and then counter-revolution, is (along with class wars and squabbles) the settlement of personal, family, shop, corporate and other scores; at the same time, the following semantic chain is approximately realized: revenge => retribution => compensation => restoration of equilibrium (balance), i.e. justice... (the very etymology of words builds them into a similar semantic chain, corresponding to the direction of the course of processes of dialectical development). This is the external manifestation of the dialectical nature of developing reality in the field of human relations. At the "higher" levels — this is a contest of competing concepts and ideologies; at more "down-to-earth" levels — this is

a physical (force) struggle, conditioned by the desire (thirst) for the restoration of trampled justice, violated property (household) equilibrium. The mentioned chain can be clothed in a more attractive form, i.e. somehow ennoble, put on the clothes of legal laws, but it will never be possible to eradicate or abolish this chain, since in its depth, in essence — this is the same engine of contentful renewal, progress, development.] 10. The letter "The Bolsheviks Must Take Power" [9, pp. 325-327], addressed to the Central, Petrograd and Moscow Committees of the RSDLP(b), was written in September 1917. Here Lenin emphasizes that, having received a majority in both metropolitan Soviets of Workers' and Soldiers' Deputies, the Bolsheviks can and must take state power into their own hands. Having an active majority of the people (sufficient to break the enemy's resistance), proposing a democratic peace, giving the land to the peasants immediately, restoring the democratic freedoms trampled by the Kerensky government, the Bolsheviks will form such a government that no one will overthrow. One must not be deceived by the figures of elections to city dumas: only elections to the Soviets show the true, truthful correlation of forces (i.e. of components in the established relation-form {Soviets of Workers' and Soldiers' Deputies / bourgeois Provisional Government}) at the present moment. The people are tired of the hesitation of the Mensheviks and Socialist-Revolutionaries, and only the victory of the Bolsheviks in both capitals will draw the peasants after the Bolsheviks. Thus, the agenda should include questions about the armed uprising in Petrograd and Moscow, the conquest of power and the overthrow of the Provisional Government. In this regard, Lenin very aptly quotes Engels' words about the uprising: "Insurrection is an art" [17, p. 100]. "Having taken power at once in Moscow and Petrograd... we will win unconditionally and undoubtedly," Lenin concludes [9, p. 327]. [The establishment and "fixity" of a new correlation between the components in the notorious relation-form {Soviets / Provisional Government} (i.e. the statement of the fact of the majority of the people on the side of the Soviets) gives grounds for the conclusion about the relevance and legitimacy of a non-violent transition and transfer of all state power into the hands of the Soviets. A correctly performed assessment of the achieved correlation between the mentioned components in their established relation allows one to confidently forecast the course of the uprising and count on its most peaceful and bloodless outcome]. 11. Another letter of Lenin to the Central Committee of the RSDLP(b) (known as an article under the title "Marxism and Insurrection" [9, pp. 328-332] and written approximately on the same days as the previous letter) was a reaction to the accusations of the Bolsheviks' adherence to the ideas of "Blanquism". These accusations arose on the basis of the Bolsheviks' attitude to the uprising as to an art (absolutizing allegedly the role of the subjective

beginning, which is characteristic of Blanquism, based, as is known, on the theory of conspiracies and terror). Meanwhile, it was Marxism, according to Lenin, that called the uprising an art, explaining that the uprising must be treated as an art: to win the first success and from success to go to success, without ceasing the offensive against the enemy, taking advantage of his confusion and indecision, etc. Fighting the accusations of Blanquism, Lenin determines the three main signs-conditions of success [i.e. of achieving the highest contentfulness by the forms-relations participating in the revolutionary process] of the uprising: firstly, reliance on the advanced class; secondly, reliance on the revolutionary upsurge of the people; thirdly, reliance on such a turning point in the history of the growing revolution when the greatest activity of the people is accompanied by the strongest fluctuations in the ranks of the enemies and indecisive "friends" of the revolution. "It is by these three conditions of posing the question of the uprising that Marxism differs from Blanquism," Lenin concludes [9, p. 328]. To prove the urgent necessity of the uprising from the point of view of the objective agenda, and not subjective conjectures, Vladimir Ilyich traces the change of objective conditions, the situation in the period of time from July to September of the same 1917, trying to identify the trend or direction of such changes. In July, in his firm conviction, the above-mentioned indispensable conditions for the success of the uprising were not yet observed, while by September the general picture had changed radically and now "all objective prerequisites for a successful uprising are evident" [9, p. 330]. Victory in the uprising, in Lenin's opinion, will disrupt Kerensky's game with the surrender of Petrograd and the conclusion of a separate peace with Germany against the revolution and will ensure the conclusion of a more complete, open, fair peace in favor of the revolution. Only the Bolshevik party, having won in the uprising, can save Petrograd from surrender to the Germans and save the whole of Russia from the separate division by the imperialists of both warring coalitions. The Bolsheviks must draw up a short declaration, insisting on the necessity of immediate actions for the sake of saving the revolution, the necessity of a complete break with the bourgeoisie, the complete removal of the current government, the complete break with the Anglo-French imperialists preparing a separate division of Russia, the necessity of an immediate transfer of all power into the hands of the revolutionary democracy led by the revolutionary proletariat. The short and sharp declaration must contain two most important points: 1) the people are tired of the hesitation and indecision of the Socialist-Revolutionaries and Mensheviks and therefore breaks with these parties finally, for they have betrayed the revolution; 2) a break with the allied imperialists and a proposal for peace without annexations lead either to an immediate truce or to the transition of the revolutionary proletariat to defense and waging a truly fair revolutionary war under its leadership. Having published the declaration, the Bolsheviks must put their entire faction forward to the factories and barracks

— that is its place, there is the nerve of life, there is the source of salvation of the revolution. Posing the question in this way, the Bolsheviks will be able to correctly appoint the moment of the beginning of the uprising, having previously organized the headquarters of the rebel detachments, mobilized the armed workers for a desperate last decisive battle, occupied the telegraph, telephone and connected all factories, all regiments, all points of armed struggle with the headquarters, etc. [9, pp. 331-332]. [Thus, the transfer of accents and the shift of the correlation of components in the known relations towards more contentful, active and active components and thereby bringing these relations to the form {Soviets (under the aegis of the Bolsheviks) / Provisional Government (in alliance with the bourgeoisie and foreign financial capital)} changes the entire tactics of the Bolsheviks in the conditions of the growing revolutionary movement. In other words, speaking in everyday language, the first revolution of the proletariat, the peasantry and the bourgeoisie in alliance with the Anglo-French financial capital against tsarism before our eyes is growing into a revolution of the proletariat and the poorest peasantry against the bourgeoisie with its ally the Anglo-French financial capital and the Kerensky government]. As has already been shown earlier, a similar schematically-described above plot construction can be seen in almost all of Lenin's theoretical works. Thus, we have some grounds for building and formulating a methodology (or even a whole methodology) for researching the perspective of the movementdevelopment-change of the forms-relations of interacting components in different areas and at different levels of reality. 12. The article "On Compromises" [9, pp. 158-163] was printed on September 19, 1917 in connection with the opened opportunity for the Bolsheviks to conclude a voluntary compromise with a number of other parties. "A compromise," Lenin writes, "is called in politics the concession of some demands, the renunciation of part of one's demands by virtue of an agreement with another party" [9, p. 158]. The usual idea of the Bolsheviks was that the Bolsheviks do not agree with anyone and never on any compromises. Such an idea is flattering, for even enemies are forced to recognize the party of the revolutionary proletariat's fidelity to the principles of socialism and revolution. However, to tell the truth, such an idea does not correspond to the truth. The task of a truly revolutionary party consists not in not succumbing to compromises, but in being able, through all compromises, to maintain fidelity to its principles, fidelity to its class, its cause of preparing the revolution and the corresponding education of the masses. The Bolshevik party, like any other, strives for political domination for itself in the name of achieving its goal; the immediate goal for the Bolsheviks is the establishment of the dictatorship of the revolutionary proletariat. And

yet, in the current conditions of the original turn of the Russian revolution, the Bolsheviks (as a party) can offer a voluntary compromise, however, not to the bourgeoisie (i.e. the main class enemy), but to the SocialistRevolutionaries and Mensheviks (the dominant petty-bourgeois democratic parties). Now within a very short period of time it is possible to return to the slogan "All Power to the Soviets"; at the same time, the government responsible to the Soviets will consist of the Socialist-Revolutionaries and Mensheviks. Such a combined variant could ensure with a gigantic probability the peaceful movement forward of the entire Russian revolution and extremely high chances of the worldwide movement towards peace and the victory of socialism. "Only in the name of this peaceful development of the revolution — an opportunity, extremely rare in history and extremely valuable, an opportunity, exceptionally rare, only in its name the Bolsheviks, supporters of the world revolution, supporters of revolutionary methods, can and must, in my opinion, go for such a compromise" [9, p. 159]. [And again Lenin, as a true dialectician, discovers a certain new (capable of functioning effectively) relation of components {Soviets / government of Socialist-Revolutionaries and Mensheviks}, the correlation of which at that given particular moment would contribute to giving an additional impetus to the prospective revolutionary movement]. But such an outcome was possible at the moment Lenin was preparing this article. However, literally a couple of days later, the situation began to change rapidly and the events that occurred showed that the proposal for a compromise was already late; the few days during which peaceful development would still have been possible had passed. It became obvious that due to the inaction of the Socialist-Revolutionaries, the favorable "days when the road of peaceful development became possible by chance, have already passed" [9, p. 163]. 13. The article "An Outsider's Tips" [9, pp. 391-393] was written on the eve of the Great October Socialist Revolution. The current situation makes it clear that in the conditions of the greatest sympathy and support for the revolutionary-proletarian (Bolshevik) power on the part of all workers and exploited people in the world in general, and in the warring countries in particular, and among the Russian peasantry in particular — all Bolsheviks should be quite clear: the transfer of power to the Soviets now means in practice an armed uprising. To renounce the armed uprising is tantamount to renouncing the main slogan of Bolshevism — "All Power to the Soviets". And here Vladimir Ilyich again insists on the well-known Marxist thesis: "Armed uprising, like war, is an art" [9, p. 391]. Marxism formulates the following main rules of this art: 1) having started the uprising, one must firmly go to the end; 2) in the decisive place and at the decisive moment, it is necessary to gather a large superiority of forces; 3) defense is the death of an armed uprising — one must necessarily go over to the offensive; 4) one must try to take the enemy by surprise; 5) to achieve small successes daily and move from success to success, maintaining at all costs the "moral superiority".

In the adaptation of these general rules to the specific situation, Lenin highlights the following points: 1) the organization of a sudden and rapid offensive on Petrograd both from within and from outside with the aim of creating a gigantic superiority of forces, paralyzing the response actions of the Junkers, and thereby achieving the bloodlessness ("low-bloodedness") of the uprising; 2) by the three main revolutionary forces (the fleet, workers and military units) the main objects must be taken under control and held at any cost: telephone, telegraph, bridges, railway stations; 3) the formation of small detachments from the most resolute and steadfast comrades, ready to provide operational support in the most important directions. [Here we see that without any reservations or delays, Lenin finally bets on the new correlation of components and the superiority of the "Soviets" component (i.e. the predominance of the "numerator" over the "denominator") that had developed by that time in the relation {Soviets / Provisional Government} and on this basis plans the success and the acquisition of a new quality by the indicated relation in its transformation into a more contentful and prospective form.] According to the circumstances that had developed then, Lenin believed that "the success of the Russian and world revolution depends on two or three days of struggle" [9, p. 393]. § 3. Works of the post-revolutionary period The Great October Socialist Revolution has been accomplished. The Bolsheviks took power, but it did not become calmer or simpler. The agenda includes new topical questions: how to hold power, how to suppress the resistance of the counter-revolution, how to move on to building a peaceful life, how to awaken the creative initiative of the masses, etc. 1. In the article "Speech on the Nationalization of Banks at the Meeting of the All-Russian Central Executive Committee on December 27, 1917. Protocol Record" [9, pp. 462-464], published on December 29, 1917, it is about the opposition of the new power of the Soviets by the old bourgeois bureaucratic apparatus. The old apparatus with all its might tries to present the banking issue as extremely fragile, complex and confusing, in order to maximally slow down the process of transferring affairs into the hands of representatives of the new power and to hinder all socialist undertakings. Regarding all decisions of the Council, they ["apparatchiks"] declare that they agree, but only in principle. The entire system of bourgeois intelligentsia, in Lenin's opinion, is distinguished by "its constant agreement in principle and disagreement in practice", which ruins the whole cause [9, p. 462]. From the very first days, the new power faces opposition, when its decisive steps are approved in theory and nothing but sabotage is met in practice. No one (except utopian socialists) had previously claimed that it is possible to

win without resistance and without establishing the dictatorship of the proletariat; and this fundamental point was not disputed by anyone. But when the word "dictatorship" is translated into Russian and its equivalent "iron hand" is reached, then all the former zeal disappears somewhere and lamentations begin about the fragility and confusion of the issue [all the pressure is suddenly leveled and nullified by referring to the complexity and confusion of issues]. However, here one must see that this iron hand, destroying, also creates. After all, at first there was talk of an agreement with the banks ("who wants to work — you are welcome"), they were issued loans for financing enterprises, but they started sabotage of an unprecedented size, and practice led the Bolsheviks to exercise control by other measures. Now the words "dictatorship of the proletariat" are spoken by the Bolsheviks very seriously, and this dictatorship will be implemented. 2. And then, on the hot heels, “A draft decree ["road map", as they would say today] on the implementation of the nationalization of banks and the necessary measures in connection with this” was created and submitted by Lenin for discussion by the bureau of the Supreme Council of the National Economy [9, pp. 465-467], which determined the direction of movement and transformations of the banking sector for the near future and many years to come. Delay with the approval of the decree was already tantamount to the disruption of the entire economy due to the powerful opposition and sabotage on the part of the banks. In the preamble to the mentioned Draft Decree [9, p. 465], it is said about the critical food situation and the threat of famine generated by the speculation and sabotage of capitalists and officials, which leads to the need to take extraordinary revolutionary measures to combat this evil. So that all citizens of the state and, first of all, the working classes under the leadership of their Soviets of workers', soldiers' and peasants' deputies could immediately, "stopping at nothing and acting in the most revolutionary way, take up such a struggle and the establishment of the correct economic life of the country, the following rules are established", which are further set out in the body of the Draft Decree [below are the rules that, in our opinion, are of the greatest interest to us today from the point of view of their continued effectiveness to this day.] o o

o

All joint-stock enterprises are declared the property of the state. All shareholders "belonging to the rich classes" are obliged to continue managing the affairs of enterprises, providing weekly reports on their activities to the local Soviets. Government loans (external and internal) are annulled, but the interests of small owners of bonds (as well as shares) belonging to the working classes of the population are fully secured.

o

o

o

o

o

o

o

Universal labor conscription is introduced, when all citizens of both sexes, from 16 to 55 years old, are obliged to perform work assigned by the local Soviets. For correct accounting and distribution of food and other essential products, all citizens of the state are obliged to join some consumer society and perform the work that will be imposed on them by the Soviets. Unions of railway workers and employees are obliged to urgently develop and immediately begin to implement emergency measures to provide transport, especially for the delivery of food, fuel and other essential items. The duty of waging a merciless struggle against speculators is also imposed on them. "Persons of the rich classes" are obliged to keep all their money sums in the State Bank, its branches and savings banks, receiving for consumer needs the sums established by the local Soviets; sums for production and trade needs can be obtained only with written certificates from worker control institutions; "those guilty of deceiving the state and the people will be subject to confiscation of all property". "The same punishment, as well as imprisonment or sending to the front and to forced labor, is subject to all disobedient to this law, saboteurs and striking officials, as well as speculators. The local Soviets... are obliged to urgently develop the most revolutionary measures of struggle against these real enemies of the people." Trade unions and other organizations of workers in conjunction with the local Soviets organize flying groups of controllers from the most reliable and recommended by party organizations persons to monitor the implementation of this law, to check the quantity and quality of work and to bring to revolutionary courts those guilty of violating or circumventing the law. "Workers and employees of nationalized enterprises are obliged to strain all forces and take emergency measures to improve the organization of work, strengthen discipline, increase labor productivity. The bodies of worker control must provide weekly reports on what has been achieved in this regard. For shortcomings and omissions, the guilty are answerable before the revolutionary court" [9, p. 467]. [And again the contradictory question requires a solution — finding the correct correlation of components-constituents in the relation {dictatorship of the proletariat / soft government apparatus of indecisive bourgeois intelligentsia}. The "iron hand" or the dictatorship of the proletariat is precisely that rigorous accounting and control over production and distribution, which must be strictly exercised not only in wartime conditions, but throughout the entire period of socialist construction, until the principles of morality, ethics, honest attitude to work enter the blood and flesh of every person — both at the level of conscious perception and at the level of the subconscious. The tricks of bourgeois games described in the article were revealed by the Bolsheviks in the first months of the functioning of Soviet power, which is why decisions were made to act in response quite harshly and immediately, so that the previous sacrifices and efforts would not be in vain.]

3. In the article "How to Organize Competition?" [9, pp. 468-476], written in January 1918, the conditions for awakening genuine competition to life, as well as the initiative of the masses, are considered. Already at the dawn of the emergence of capitalist relations, the competition of small commodity producers could on a somewhat wide scale educate enterprise, energy, the boldness of initiative; in fact, today capitalism has replaced small independent production with the largest factory production, joint-stock enterprises, syndicates and other monopolies. Competition in this layout means, on the contrary, the suppression of the enterprise of the mass of the population, the replacement of competition with financial fraud and servility to the "tops" of the social ladder [9, p. 468]. [How amazingly accurately and fairly this "sounds" in relation to the rampage of the wildest capitalism in modern Russia and in the post-Soviet space!]. To develop abilities, to reveal human talents (which the people have in abundance) on a mass scale is possible only with a properly organized socialist method of production. The most urgent task today, when the socialist government is in power, is to organize competition. [It is striking how from the very first days of Soviet power, in the conditions of an economic chaos, collapse, and famine that have not yet been overcome, Lenin thinks in categories of the most peaceful and calm time, in categories of education and formation of new production relations between workers, awakening the initiative of people on a "mass scale"!]. "A broad, truly mass creation of the opportunity to show enterprise, competition, a bold initiative is only now. ...For the first time after centuries of work for others, forced work for exploiters, the opportunity to work for oneself arises, and moreover work based on all the achievements of the latest technology and culture" [9, p. 469]. It is quite clear that the greatest change in history of mankind from forced labor to labor for oneself cannot occur without conflicts, without overcoming the resistance of exploiters, without displacing the capitalists, which will take time. In addition to the "military" measures of suppressing the resistance of the "rich classes", the proletariat and the poorest peasantry will also need huge organizational efforts. Thus, the organizational task is intertwined with the task of ruthless military suppression of yesterday's slave owners (capitalists and their lackeys — the gentlemen of the bourgeois intelligentsia). Naturally, none of the past "masters of life" wants to voluntarily part with their former command and "bread" privileged posts, considering themselves "Godchosen" patrons and managers over the people. "But the matter of the exploiters and their intellectual lackeys is a hopeless matter." Their resistance, workers and peasants will eventually break decisively and ruthlessly.

Intellectuals accustomed to serving capitalists and the capitalist state believe that they cannot be done without. "Their impudent calculation will not be justified: educated people are already standing out, going over to the side of the people, to the side of the workers, helping to break the resistance of the servants of capital. And there are many organizational talents in the peasantry and the working class, and these talents are just beginning to realize themselves, to wake up, to reach for living, creative, great work, to take up the construction of a socialist society independently" [9, p. 470]. Perhaps the most important task today, Lenin says, is to develop as widely as possible the independent initiative of workers in the matter of creative organizational work. It is necessary to break the vile and absurd prejudice that only the so-called "higher classes" and only the rich can manage the organizational construction of society. Organizational work is "within the power of an ordinary worker and peasant who has literacy, knowledge of people, practical experience. ...There is still an inexhaustible spring and a richest spring of such talents in the working class and in the peasantry." Workers and peasants are still timid, they have not yet got used to the fact that they are now the ruling class. But the strength and vitality of the October Revolution of 1917 lies in the fact that it leads the working people to the path of independent creation of a new life. [For the note of the current "rulers" and their ilk: Lenin in a matter of years found ways from scratch to motivate the illiterate, bast-shod population of the bloodless country, and the current "ruler" with his "valiant" arrogant entourage has been struggling for several terms (both in a row and intermittently) to solve the same task, but in vain. The conservative "comprador" entourage of the current "ruler" will not allow him (the ruler) to make a single decision encroaching on the self-serving interest of this entourage.
