So all this pompous camarilla will sit and wait for mythical Western investments like manna from heaven, shifting the blame for their failure on the notorious Western sanctions. Fortunately, their own fortunes are reliably placed in foreign lands with the hope that such ugliness will continue forever. For the information of today's "rulers": Lenin, in the five or six years granted to him after the revolution, managed (in the conditions of discord and devastation, having tried many options) to find ways to awaken both enthusiasm and the initiative of millions, and to attract foreign specialists, and to train and prepare his own... Human potential (physical strength, intellectual and spiritual energy of the people) — this is the source of all investments, technologies and innovations, and therefore the main component of the country's economic success; however, the inept "comprador" entourage of the supreme "ruler" will skillfully, on the verge of sabotage, extinguish this potential and write off all failures to the lack of overseas investments and the omnipotence of overseas sanctions. By the way, has the main "ruler" tried to pull "overseas investments" out of the purse of his entourage?

Regarding the liberal-minded (in relation to the aggressive collective West) circles in Russia, it is somehow already old-fashioned, unpleasant, and not desirable to speak. Since the words of F.M. Dostoevsky, it has been known that "our Russian liberal is first and foremost a lackey and only looks for how to clean someone's boots" [4, p. 133]. Meanwhile, eternal kneeling before the West is conditioned by the universality and generality of the very principle of self-development. The aspiration of all forms to the highest contentfulness has an unsightly reverse side in the imposition (as a sample or standard) of its allegedly most perfect and exclusive form — its understanding of the world, its ideology (with the accompanying "uprooting" of dissent and everything "wrong"), its worldview, world outlook, world perception, its way of life — and all this is presented as an indisputable absolute truth. The Anglo-Saxon world has since time immemorial professed an aggressive way of interacting with other peoples or representatives (carriers) of a different principle, as, for example, the peace-loving and more balanced Eastern Slavic peoples, who always act as a pacifying counterweight to restrain and neutralize such excessive activity (aggressiveness) of the Anglo-Saxons and their ilk. Anglo-Saxon communities and their accomplices (in aggregate — the collective West) feel themselves at the same time champions and bearers of good, enlightenment, civilization, morality, ethics, etc.; they are always charged with the ideology of the expansion of everything, in their assessment, "good" against everything "bad" or "good" (according to their understanding) against "evil". Our liberals are led by this bait or false bait; persons with liberal views are very one-sided and superficial in their perceptions and beliefs, since they take the brilliant external packaging for the most part as the deep hidden essence of things. In addition, the aggression inherent in the Anglo-Saxon "mentors" and Co. is further enhanced due to a number of "misses" pursuing them, i.e. due to receiving periodic spankings and thrashings from the Eastern Slavs in response to their excessive zeal in carrying out incessant attempts at forcible "enlightenment" and "ennoblement" of the latter; therefore, they always have an unquenchable vulnerability due to their unchanged status of the "eternally beaten" in open clashes. It must be said straight out that these "highly civilized" peoples have never been able to correctly assess their contentful resource, i.e. to measure their capabilities against the potential of the Eastern Slavs, which is why they suffered defeats from them time and again. The collective West led by the US bets (unconsciously, of course) on the perfection of the form (i.e. mainly on the formal principle), while ignoring the development of the contentful side-constituent. This is the secret of their certain technological advantage (quality of household items, equipment, services, etc.), achieved by focusing on the Form (sometimes more or

less contentful only from the perspective of short-term success and contentless from the point of view of strategy and deep, long-term perspective of the development of mankind). It is still necessary to thoroughly "dissect" the dry calculating intellect of the Anglo-Saxon world — this formal constituent, this formal principle of the universal human world — in order to understand the danger of such a one-sided approach on the scale of the development of all mankind. In their prevailing mechanicity and technological inclinations (i.e. a bias towards the technological side, often devoid of moral underpinnings and moral attitudes) lie all the explanations of their modern actions and behavior. None other than they have become today the first managers and manipulators of public consciousness on an unprecedentedly massive, global scale, through the Internet, and in particular through social networks and platforms. In all their policy (foreign, internal, technical, technological, social, etc. — especially against the background of the not so long ago once again proclaimed by Obama their imaginary and illusory exclusivity) there is something school-boyishly wellcalculated rational and at the same time humanly extremely primitive and limited (the analogy with cold-blooded "reptiloids" trying to impose their way of life and behavior on warm-blooded earthlings suggests itself). Here it is also appropriate, in our opinion, to touch upon the question of the imposition on the whole world by the collective West of its allegedly most progressive and most democratic "values" and preferences. First of all, it is necessary in this regard to point out the entrenched confusion in the interpretations of the terms "values" and "interests". This confusion is due to the incompetence of the "interpreters" in terms of the correct correlation of these concepts with two different, non-identical spheres of acquiring reality (in the composition of the multi-level form of man): the sphere (level) of the "sensual" and the sphere (level) of the "ideal". It must be said that such issues have long and deeply been analyzed by famous philosophers of the past, but the solutions to these issues have been quite successfully and thoroughly forgotten by our contemporaries. If we talk about "values" in the meaning and sense invested in this concept by the Western world, then we are talking mainly about primitive interests limited to the sensual sphere of man. In this section, all the efforts and aspirations of the Western philistine are aimed mainly at the implementation of quite "down-to-earth" and simple human needs (in food, housing, source of income, in procreation, rest, as well as in the satisfaction of various physiological desires, pleasures, fantasies, requests for boundless rights and freedoms, etc.). The concept of "values" from the point of view, for example, of a Russian person is by no means limited to the sensual sphere, but goes ("protrudes") beyond its limits into the realm of the ideal; and in this sense it is already identified with spiritual categories (which for their more accurate identification and binding require different, more Contentful terminology): with principles, with convictions, with ideals, with worldview, etc., etc., — i.e. with formsattributes of a higher level of acquiring reality, called human consciousness. In this sense and in this hypostasis — this is no longer an orientation towards low-level "values" of the Western world, but a conscious adherence to

more Contentful views: principles of human morality, ethics, spirituality, humanism, etc. In the main and the big, the modern West (unlike, say, Europe of the Enlightenment) is devoid of aspiration to proclaim and follow (including in everyday life) high spiritual principles and convictions — it simply got stuck on "down-to-earth" needs and low-grade interests, refusing further movement towards ideals developing a person. It is precisely for this reason that all the teachings and instructions of the Western world regarding the unquestioning acceptance and "implementation in life" by other peoples of the line of Western, so to speak, "values" look at least ridiculous and laughable.] At the same time, implementing socialist undertakings, the Soviets of workers', soldiers' and peasants' deputies, consumer societies, supply unions, factory committees and worker control bodies must understand that the main economic task at this stage is accounting and control. The old habit of a forced person is still strong — as if to "grab" at least a piece from the bourgeoisie, without burdening oneself with extra work and obligations. Such an approach of "newcomers" to the factory environment: "Grab a bigger piece and run away", — is transferred to the factory that has passed into the ownership of the people. The advanced conscious workers have already started fighting against such personnel, who will undoubtedly be supported by honest, thinking peasants. Universal, universal, universal accounting and control over the distribution of products and the amount of labor — this is the essence of the socialist transformation; the accounting and control necessary for the transition to socialism can only be mass. Proceeding from this, Lenin turns to the new working owners of land, banks, factories and plants: "Take up the accounting and control of production and distribution yourselves, — this is the only way to the victory of socialism, the pledge of its victory, the pledge of victory over all exploitation, over all need and poverty". In Russia, Lenin says, there is enough bread, iron, forest, wool, cotton and flax for everyone, if a nationwide businesslike, practical control over distribution is established, if the enemies of the people (the rich and their "hangers-on" — bourgeois intellectuals) are defeated not only in politics, but also in the everyday economic life of a society in which "a handful robbed the people and mocked the people" [9, p. 472]. [An extremely topical appeal also applicable to the current situation in Russia!]. "The rich and crooks, these are two sides of the same coin, these are the two main categories of parasites fostered by capitalism, these are the main enemies of socialism, these enemies must be placed under the special supervision of the entire population, they must be dealt with, at the slightest violation by them of the rules and laws of socialist society, mercilessly. Any weakness, any hesitation, any sentimentality in this regard would be the greatest crime against socialism" [9, p. 473]. [If only Lenin with his dialectical mind were here, even for an hour !!!

Our great Motherland has again been turned into a country of lackeys and masters, where a handful of plutocrats does at an unconscious level everything that comes to mind, satisfying primarily their primitive momentary whims and fancies, dictated only by the desire to increase their own savings, thereby determining and setting in advance, for years, the configuration of our (or rather, their) future and without our participation in this. Before, such a thing could not even be dreamed of or seen in a nightmare. Lenin's lessons of dialectics have turned out to be, at best, Unlearned lessons of dialectics.] The way to neutralize these parasites is a nationwide, organized by millions of workers and peasants, voluntary and energetic accounting and control over the amount of labor, production and distribution of products. And in order to organize this accounting and control, which is quite accessible and feasible for every honest, sensible and efficient worker and peasant, it is necessary to call their own organizational talents to life, it is necessary to establish on an all-state scale a competition in terms of organizational successes, it is necessary that workers and peasants clearly understand the difference between the necessary advice of an educated person and the necessary control of a simple worker and peasant over the sloppiness, so common among educated people. Sloppiness, carelessness, untidiness, "the tendency to take on everything in the world and bring nothing to an end is one of the properties of "educated people", stemming not at all from their bad nature, much less from malice, but from all the habits of life, from the setting of their work, from overwork", from the abnormal correlation of the mental and physical components of labor in the cycles of their activity, etc., etc. [And here we see a surprisingly insightful and precise statement of the connection between the actual mistakes, shortcomings and misses made during the revolution and those "sad, but inevitable" at that moment abovementioned "properties of intellectuals", as well as the lack of sufficient control from the workers over the organizational work of intellectuals. Most of all, hesitation and indecision, which are so characteristic of the intelligentsia and which the intelligentsia is guilty of, are unacceptable to Lenin. Any hesitation after the adoption of a collective decision was tantamount to betrayal and defeat for Lenin. Ilyich is always tuned and charged for victory. The same applies to compromises (namely in relation to his ideological opponents), to which the bourgeois intelligentsia is most inclined, being weak in spirit (i.e. not having fundamental ideological convictions). Lenin also includes here the desire to sit on two chairs; nowadays — this is the notorious political "multi-vector". Indecision and hesitation, in the end, often turn into extra unnecessary victims; these are those absurd mistakes that could have been avoided by adhering to a firm position. The enemy is like an uncomfortable hard, but "firm ground", and the wavering and indecisive

"fellow traveler" is akin to a swamp: try to know when it will "absorb" and "digest" you, i.e. simply betray. In modern Russia, the role of the petty-bourgeois wavering conciliators with those in power is played by "pocket" parties and groups like modern centrists, communists, liberals of all names, etc., with a vague position — declassed elements, dangling somewhere "between here and between there"; the place of the actual spokesman for the opinions of the working class of wage workers remains vacant as before. In general, regarding the betrayal and apostasy of the intelligentsia, when the cause of a whole life is threatened, anyone would speak even more harshly and emotionally than Vladimir Ilyich does. As in the times of V.I. Lenin, modern political and economic literature is full of all kinds of absurdities: and some "middle class" was invented, and ownership of the means of production no longer has any significance, and a two-party system is strictly obligatory for progress, etc. If you come to think of it, any person always has an internal content-form (i.e. at least twocomponent, two-sided, dual) contradiction, which serves as the engine of all progress and development even more fiercely than the contest, competition or conflict of two or several parties; in one person, opposing components can be observed and coexist, giving such an impulse of activity that no parties are needed at all. As they say in Russia: "In Russia there is still one party — it is just called differently." However, something similar is observed in other countries. For example, in the USA, liberals and conservatives (Democrats and Republicans) have long been fighting for power only for the sake of protecting and increasing the property and income of the propertied class. In addition, one must always be prepared for the fact that over time the "bourgeoisification" of the most advanced and progressive parties at first occurs due to the degeneration of the top and part of the members of these parties into their opposites — such is the dialectical nature of reality: one just needs to recall this more often and shake up (clean out, as Lenin said) the party ranks. As in the times of Lenin's pre-revolutionary political activity, today, to curb the threat of stratification and radicalization of society, the bourgeoisie sometimes try to integrate the topmost layers of the left and anarchists into their system, recruit them into their ranks (having bought them off with an insignificant increase in the wealth tax or small concessions and benefits in the social sphere, etc.). What kind of opposition is it in parliament if it consists of the power-fed representatives of the same "bourgeoisified" class as the official propertied ruling class? Where does the dividing line pass between the official power and the systemic alleged opposition, among the leading members of which the owners and co-owners (shareholders) of "factories, newspapers, steamships" with assets placed outside the borders of our Motherland suddenly find themselves by chance?]

"Intellectuals give the most excellent advice and guiding instructions all the time, but they turn out to be ridiculously, absurdly, disgracefully "armless", unable to put these advice and instructions into practice, to exercise practical control over the fact that the word turns into action. This is where it is impossible to do without the help and without the guiding role of practical organizers from the "people", from the workers and working peasants" [9, p. 474]. In this regard, competitions of practical organizers from workers and peasants with each other should be organized, to fight against any "stamping" and attempts to establish uniformity from above, to which intellectuals are so prone. And then follows an "absolutely dialectically" built semantic, in some ways final construction on the topic of the detailed conversation conducted in the article, giving an idea of the presence of an objective natural direct connection between the contentful and formal components of this construction, which serves as the brightest confirmation of its dialectics, and, consequently, its truthfulness: "With democratic and socialist centralism, stamping or establishing uniformity from above has nothing in common. Unity in the main, in the root, in the essential is not violated, but is ensured by diversity in details, in local features, in approaches to the matter, in ways of exercising control, in ways of destroying and neutralizing parasites (the rich and crooks, slops and hysterics from the intelligentsia, etc., etc.)". [Ah, if Ilyich were with us throughout the entire period of socialist construction, what unforgivably gross mistakes we could have avoided in order not to betray the most fruitful idea and not to deviate from this path! Dialectics is the skill or art of balancing on the razor's edge, to walk on the sharpest blade without cutting and without falling to either side. At the same time, this is not eclecticism and by no means the ability to sit on two chairs at once (not adherence to the so-called multi-vector).] The Soviets of working people's deputies must follow the path of combining, on the one hand, the initiative, independence, freedom of movement, energy from below and, on the other hand, voluntary, alien to templates, centralism. It is necessary that any and every commune (factory, village, consumer society, supply committee) should come out, competing with another commune, as a practical organizer of accounting and control over labor and distribution of products. The communes themselves must develop and test in practice thousands of forms and methods of practical accounting and control over the rich and crooks. "Diversity here is a guarantee of vitality, a guarantee of success in achieving a common single goal. ... The more diverse, the better, the richer the general experience will be, the more reliable and faster, the easier the practice will develop — for only practice can develop — the best methods and means of struggle" [9, p. 475]. The competition of communes, communities, consumer-production societies and partnerships, Soviets of workers', peasants' and soldiers' deputies should

develop on the issues of eliminating hunger, unemployment, eradicating the rich idlers, scoundrels from the lackeys of the bourgeoisie, saboteurs (calling themselves intellectuals), on the issues of increasing labor productivity, building new good houses for the poor, housing the poor in the houses of the rich, supplying a bottle of milk to every child from poor families. It is in the course of this work that organizational talents, which are abundant in the people, should be practically singled out and promoted to the top, to the bodies of all-state administration. "They need to be helped to unfold. They and they alone, with the support of the masses, will be able to save Russia and save the cause of socialism" [9, p. 476]. [And again, new optimal (best) relations with new components are being sought and found: {emancipated creative labor / socialized socialist enterprise}, {organizational talent of the working people / accounting and control over production and distribution}, {knowledge and experience of an educated employee (intelligentsia) / control and efforts of a simple worker regarding the eradication of sloppiness and irresponsibility of an educated employee (intelligentsia)}, {initiative and initiative of workers on the ground / voluntary democratic socialist centralism}, — the correlations of which at this particular moment (in contrast to the previously established relations with known components {wage labor / capital}, {proletariat/bourgeoisie}, {competing diversity/monopoly}, {enterprise / established bureaucratic order}) can give an additional impetus to the further revolutionary movement. Thus, the task of reaching new correlations of components in such more general relations as {theory/practice}, {mental labor / physical labor}, etc. is implicitly solved.] 4. The article "The Main Task of Our Days" [9, pp. 569-573] was printed in March 1918. As an epigraph, the article was preceded by words from N.A. Nekrasov's poem "Who Lives Well in Russia": "You are both poor, you are both abundant, you are both mighty, you are both powerless — Mother Russia!". The article is written at the moment of the great turn from war to peace; from the war between predators, sending millions of exploited workers to the slaughter in order to establish a new order of dividing the looted booty, — to the war of the oppressed against the oppressors, for liberation from the yoke of capital and for a future society of sufficiency and durable peace. Russia had to experience especially acutely the sharpest turns of history, turning from imperialism to the communist revolution. In a few days, one of the oldest, most powerful, barbaric and brutal monarchies was destroyed; in a few months, a number of stages of conciliation with the bourgeoisie, of outliving petty-bourgeois illusions were passed, for which other countries spent decades; in a few weeks the bourgeoisie was overthrown and its open resistance in the civil conflict was overcome. Bolshevism made a triumphant procession from end to end of the vast country, and the lowest of the layers of the working masses oppressed by tsarism and the bourgeoisie were raised, introduced to independent life and endowed with freedom. The Soviet

republic was established — a new type of state, immeasurably higher and more democratic than the best of the bourgeois-parliamentary republics. The dictatorship of the proletariat, supported by the poorest peasantry, was established, and the implementation of the conceived system of socialist transformations began. Faith in one's own strength was awakened and the fire of enthusiasm was lit in millions and millions of workers of all countries [pride for everything done shines through in every Lenin's word.] "And in a few days the imperialist predator who attacked the unarmed ... threw us to the ground. The predator presses and chokes and tears Russia to pieces with the greater frenzy, the more menacingly the specter of the workers' revolution rises in his own country [we are talking about Germany]. We were forced to sign the "Tilsit" peace" [9, p. 570]. But one must have the courage to look straight into the face of the bitter truth and "measure entirely, to the bottom, the whole abyss of defeat, dismemberment, enslavement and humiliation into which we have now been pushed. The clearer we understand this, the more solid, tempered, steel our will to liberation will become... our unbending determination to achieve at all costs that Russia ceases to be poor and powerless, so that it becomes in the full sense of the word mighty and abundant." And this will certainly happen, for there is still enough space and natural wealth, "there is material both in natural wealth, and in the reserve of human strength, and in the excellent scope that the great revolution gave to folk creativity, — to create a truly mighty and abundant Russia." And Russia will become so if it throws away all despondency and all phraseology and, gritting its teeth, gathers all its strength. All that is needed now is to move forward along the path of the socialist revolution, not losing heart from defeats, to work on strengthening self-discipline, organization and order, on improving the system of universal accounting and control over production and distribution of products: "this is the path to creating military power and socialist power" [9, p. 571]. In the event of a heavy defeat (the consequence of which was the signing of the Treaty of Tilsit), Lenin says, it is unworthy either to swagger or to fall into despair, as well as to agree with accusations of betraying one's ideals and one's friends. The commander who leads the remnants of the defeated army into the depths of the country does not commit treason in relation to those units which he is unable to help, but fulfills his duty, saving what can still be saved, "ceding space to gain time", using any respite to gather strength. The war shook Russia, and it, undoubtedly, is moving "from the Treaty of Tilsit to a national upsurge, to a great patriotic war... to an international socialist revolution", and not to a bourgeois state. "We have been defenders since October 25, 1917. We are for the "defense of the fatherland", but that patriotic war to which we are going is a war for the socialist fatherland, for socialism as a fatherland, for the Soviet Republic..."

Instead of the slogan, Lenin continues, of bourgeois patriotism "Hatred for the German, beat the German", the Bolsheviks will say: "Learn from the German! Remain faithful to the fraternal union with the German workers... History moves in zigzags and roundabout ways. It turned out so that it is the German who now embodies, along with bestial imperialism, the beginning of discipline, organization, harmonious cooperation on the basis of machine industry, the strictest accounting and control. And this is exactly what we lack. This is exactly what we need to learn. This is exactly what our great revolution lacks in order to come from a victorious beginning... to a victorious end. This is exactly what is required of the Russian Soviet Socialist Republic in order to cease to be poor and powerless, in order to irrevocably become mighty and abundant" [9, pp. 572-573]. [The very unexpected turn "about the German", very consonant with some progressive opinions in our days too! Taking this circumstance into account, a new relation of components with a perspective of increasing the contentful component (due to the growth of the economy) and an increase in contentfulness of the entire relation as a whole is emerging on the horizon].

5. "Theses of the Central Committee of the RCP(b) in connection with the situation on the Eastern Front" [10, pp. 145-147] of April 11, 1919 played a huge role in the mobilization of the proletarian masses in the struggle against Kolchak. Kolchak's victories on the Eastern Front force both party organizations and trade unions to implement the following measures in the shortest possible time to involve broader layers of the working class in the active participation in the defense of the country. Comprehensive support for the announced mobilization. The mobilized must see the active participation of trade unions and feel the support of their working class. From each party and trade union organization, the Central Committee demands a weekly, albeit the shortest, report on what has been done to help the mobilization and the mobilized. In the frontline areas, it is necessary to carry out the universal arming of all members of trade unions. "Trade unions must everywhere with their own forces and means carry out a verification registration of their members for sending all those not absolutely necessary at home, to fight for the Volga and the Urals" [10, p. 146]. The most serious attention should be paid to strengthening agitation among the mobilized, mobilized and Red Army men: not to limit oneself to lectures and rallies, but to ensure that every member of the trade union personally participates in agitation events and in personal conversations. "Replace all male employees with women. Conduct a new reregistration for this, both party and trade union. Introduce special cards for all members of trade unions and all employees with a note on personal participation in the matter of helping the Red Army" [10, p. 146].

o

o o

Immediately establish assistance bureaus or support committees with published addresses and notification of them to the population in the widest possible way. Every mobilized person must know that in such a bureau or committee he will find help, advice, support to facilitate communication with military institutions, etc. A special task of such bureaus will be to assist in the matter of supplying the Red Army with weapons, clothing, factory stocks of various property. "For this task, one must take it with all one's might", immediate assistance must be provided to military institutions by the population itself [10, p. 147]. Through trade unions, the wide involvement of peasant youth from non-agricultural provinces in the ranks of the Red Army should be organized. "In relation to the Mensheviks and Socialist-Revolutionaries, the party line in the current situation is as follows: to prison those who help Kolchak consciously or unconsciously. We will not tolerate in our republic working people who do not help us in deed in the struggle against Kolchak" [10, p. 147]. In conclusion, Lenin calls to take up this work in a revolutionary, nontemplate way. Kolchak can be defeated quickly and finally, for the victories of the Bolsheviks in the south and the improving international situation contribute to this. "It is necessary to strain all forces, to unfold revolutionary energy, and Kolchak will be quickly defeated. The Volga, the Urals, Siberia can and must be defended and won back" [10, p. 147]. [And again, we see how, on the basis of an accurate analysis of the alignment of forces and the situation that has arisen (without any panic and cries like "everything is lost" and "every man for himself"), clear instructions are born and a complex of the most urgent measures is formed, aimed at correcting the situation for the better. We see from the Content of the given points how seriously Lenin assesses this threat, how he seeks ways and approaches to activating the creative energy of people capable of eliminating the threat. We also see how much weight the trade union movement had at that time: the trade union acts on an equal footing with party and management organizations.] 6. The article "An Example of the Petrograd Workers" [10, pp. 213-215] appeared in October 1919 in parallel with the resolution of the plenum of the Central Committee of the RCP(b) to carry out the mobilization of communists and send the best representatives of the party and the working class to the Southern Front in connection with the difficult situation that had developed there. The workers of Petrograd, at the call of the party, began an intensified mobilization and sending of the best workers to fight against Denikin, who was actively helped with money and weapons by French and English capital. At the same time, foreign propaganda was working to intimidate the population and the personnel of the revolutionary troops.

Never before has the enemy been so close to Moscow, Lenin writes. "But to repel this danger, in addition to the former forces of the troops, we are moving new detachments of advanced workers capable of creating a turning point in the mood in the retreating units" [10, p. 214]. The disorganization of the army ranks was largely caused by a large number of deserters. In this regard, the support of the army by a new influx of proletarian forces is of paramount importance. "Unstable elements will be strengthened, the mood will be raised, a turning point will be achieved. The proletariat, as has constantly happened in our revolution, will support and direct the wavering strata of the working population" [10, p. 214]. For a long time, the Petrograd proletariat has borne more burdens than the workers of other industrial centers; on its shoulders are the struggle against hunger, and against military danger, and the pulling out of the best workers to Soviet positions throughout Russia. But there is not the slightest despondency or decline of strength among the Petrograd workers — they perfectly fulfill the task of the vanguard detachment, sending help and support where it is most needed. "When such fresh forces go to strengthen the faltering parts of our army, then the working masses, soldiers from the peasants, receive new leaders from among their own people, from among the more developed, more conscious, more spiritually firm workers" [10, p. 214]. This is the source of the superiority of the peasant army over the enemy, which replenishes and supports its ranks only with "landlords' sons". It is known that such "support" ruined Kolchak, it will ruin Denikin too. Lenin calls on other workers, following the example of the Petrograd workers, to intensify their activities in the troops, to show more initiative and boldness in the desire not to yield to the Petrogradites — and then victory will be for the workers, the bourgeois counter-revolution will be finished off. After Petrograd, Moscow has already started moving, after Moscow the rest must start moving. [From the content of the article it is clearly visible how important the moral state (state of spirit) of the fighting soldiers of the Red Army is for Lenin. It is visible how truly significant the high consciousness of the advanced workers and members of the Bolshevik party was in those days! Again, it is visible how strong and unshakable Lenin's faith in victory and a favorable outcome of the armed struggle is, — his inexhaustible optimism in the most difficult moments of a mortal struggle causes deep respect.] 7. The article "From the Destruction of the Centuries-old Order to the Creation of a New One" [10, pp. 282-284], written in April 1920, is devoted to the issues set out in the one-day newspaper "Communist Subbotnik" of April 11, 1920. The main topic of the newspaper is the question of communist labor. This most important issue of the construction of socialism could be raised only after the proletariat had conquered political power, only after the expropriation of landlords and capitalists, only after the decisive victories of the proletariat that had conquered state power over the exploiters who had organized desperate resistance, counter-revolutionary uprisings and civil war. It seemed that such a

time had already come in early 1918, but a new, stronger wave of counterrevolutionary uprisings broke out so quickly that the Soviet power then had no opportunity to deal with the issues of peaceful construction. Now, after overcoming incredible difficulties and "unheard-of victories of the Red Army over the hordes of international capitalist reaction" there are chances that peace will be more lasting and stable. Some experience in the construction of socialism already exists, therefore the question of communist labor can and must be raised point-blank. However, it would be more correct to say: not about communist, but about socialist labor, for we are talking not about the highest, but about the lowest stage of the development of the new social order growing out of capitalism. Communist labor is unpaid, voluntary labor, outside the norm, out of the habit of working for the common good and out of a conscious (turned into a habit) attitude to the necessity of labor as a need of a healthy organism. However, "it is clear to everyone that it is still very, very far to the wide, truly mass application of such labor for us, i.e. for our society, our social order" [10, p. 283]. But the fact that this question has already been raised by the advanced proletariat and the state power is a step forward. To come to the big, one must start with the small. "Subbotniks, labor armies, labor conscription — this is the practical implementation in various forms of socialist and communist labor" [10, p. 283]. Mistakes and errors in such a difficult matter are inevitable. "To build a new labor discipline, to build new forms of social connection between people, to build new forms and techniques of attracting people to labor, this is the work of many years and decades. This is the most grateful and noble work" [10, p. 283]. Now, after the overthrow of the bourgeoisie and the suppression of its resistance, there is already soil on which such work has become possible; the proletariat will take up this work with all its energy and will fulfill it. [Again, the inexhaustible optimism of V.I. Lenin is admirable. The thesis about labor out of habit as a need of a healthy organism is closely connected with the thesis about the contentful filling of the forms of the labor process to the level of the identification of these forms in the final analysis with the very process of cognition itself and with the striving through such forms of the cognitive process to look around the corner, beyond the horizon, into the future as effectively and productively as possible.] Conclusion It should be said that the above-mentioned articles of Lenin were not selected specially or with any particular bias — any of his works of the indicated periods is built according to the stencil we have disassembled, according to the type of thinking that was formed in V.I. Ulyanov from a young age. As can be seen, every article or work of Lenin, regardless of the range of issues considered in it, is a model of the effective application of the dialectical approach,

extremely in demand and necessary when conducting research of objects, processes, phenomena in any area of reality, in all spheres of human activity in order to obtain the most correct and contentful results of such research. In each work, the scheme of productive ("triadic" or three-stage) development of the idea (thesis — antithesis — synthesis) is easily guessed, as well as adherence (in the analysis) to the well-known cliché of observing the unity of the historical and the logical. What first of all attracts attention in the course of getting acquainted with the content of the theoretical works of V.I. Lenin? The same or similar algorithm for presenting material concerning the issues under study, which is due to the very universal nature of development or the very dialectical nature of any object, process, phenomenon under study. And Lenin inexorably follows this algorithm, revealing the dialectical essence of the problems under study ("piling up" events, emerging situations, achieved results, etc.). Let us reproduce this algorithm once again. 1. Composing correct relations of directly interacting side-components in the forms (objects, processes, phenomena) under study. At this initial stage of research, one must be able to identify and show the directly interacting (i.e. the most essential at the moment) opposing, competing, opposing sides-components in the forms-relations under study. 2. Determination and prioritization in the found relations, i.e. identification of the active and passive sides-components in the composed relations. At this stage of research among the directly interacting components in their relations (as a rule, in pairs of such components) one must again be able to identify, guess, understand and highlight, on the one hand, the most contentful (active, leading) and, on the other hand, more formal (passive, conservative) components. Thus, such relations are brought to the generalized universal form {content/form}. 3. Determination of the probable direction of change (transformation), movement, development of the constituent sides-components (i.e. probable change in the correlation or balance of components) within and inside the relations formed from them, and consequently, the direction of variability of the relations themselves as a whole (taking into account the influences and impact of surrounding forms on these relations). A correctly composed relation of two correctly identified directly interacting components allows to determine and designate the bias or direction of their subsequent change, movement, development of their relation; this bias or direction of movement-development of the relation is conditioned by the correlation (approximate) of components within the relation, i.e. the degree of predominance of one (contentful) component over the other (formal) component.

4. Forecasting (anticipation) of the development of the relations under study, i.e. determination of the prospects for the modification of the forms under study (objects, processes, phenomena), as well as the prospects for the emergence of new forms. At this stage, both operational and strategic forecasts of the development of the forms-relations under study are made, which corresponds to forecasting the variability of existing forms and anticipating the emergence of new forms of reality. When moving to the next stage of research or the next form of reality under study, paragraphs 1-4 are repeated in the same volume. As can be seen from the presented scheme (algorithm) of conducting research, the identification of two (or more) directly interacting, opposing components in their relation turns out to be an extremely important point, since it is this Content-form structure (system) that will indicate the direction of its change and obtaining in the future an even more contentful structure or a new relation (system) composed of even more contentful components. It is important that these are not "pulled by the ears" components, not mechanically (or eclectically) combined opposites, but naturally growing one from the other opposing constituent sides, interconnected so much that one can firmly talk about their insignificance without each other. A correctly composed relation of two directly interacting components is something (a system) resembling the behavior of a liquid (or other similar continuous medium) in communicating vessels. Here, for example, are such relations (or systems) as: {time/space}, {motion/rest}, {active principle/passive principle}, {energy/mass}, {field/matter}, {north pole of a magnet/south pole of a magnet}, {subjective /objective}, etc., etc., — they are examples of correctly composed relations of directly interacting sidescomponents of reality. The correct choice of a pair of interacting components and the formation of a correct relation from them is tantamount to an artfully formulated question; then the very correct formulation of the question contains at least half of the answer to this question, which is a prerequisite for the successful solution of the task at hand. Well, and examples of incorrectly composed relations can be given much more — all unfairly conducted research and obtained false results sufficiently testify to the initially incorrect relations identified [inappropriately chosen and designated so-called false grounds lead any research to a dead end]. Therefore, it is extremely important to identify the most essential sides of the problems under study, which are in direct interaction and direct relation to each other. At each stage of the development of reality, at each specific spatio-temporal segment of it, one must be able to correctly identify and identify (i.e. recognize) the directly interacting components that are in contradiction or conflict with each

other, conditioned (we are talking about contradiction or conflict) by the search for a new form for the developing contentful component. From the listed steps of the above scheme (algorithm), a methodology (and even more — a whole methodology) for the effective research of any issue, any problem, both in everyday life and in the field of science (so to speak, a universal research template) is formed. Of course, in that former form Lenin's works (and they were written on topical issues in connection with some specific events and circumstances) today to some extent lose their formal significance, but "pushing" the author into the background for this reason is as reckless and foolish as, for example, giggling at the fact that Alexander the Great fought all his life on horseback, and not in a tank or an airplane. After all, in terms of contentful filling and presentation of views, his works are, without any exaggeration, masterpieces (for those obsessed with the idea of knowing the essence of dialectics). One just needs to look more pedantically into the composition of any of his articles to see the beauty of the logical-dialectical schemes, constructions and forms. The future requires a person to be more and more versed in the issues of dialectics. As can be seen, in our research and conclusions we do not particularly touch upon the temporal context, focusing mainly on Lenin's logic — all the pearls of the research approach shine without a deep attachment to the nuances of that great historical era. The historical background in each particular case gives the development process only peculiar shades and features, leaving its core (i.e. tendencies and patterns) typical and universal. In this perspective, any article by Lenin, any of his works is a reader on dialectics, a manual on mastering the art of dialectical thinking (to some extent invariant with respect to specific historical frameworks), i.e. on mastering what belongs to the future. No one has been able to understand and feel the "soul" of dialectics so deeply for a long time, to the greatest regret. And how much optimism and faith (namely due to the dialectical nature of the character) in a favorable, positive outcome of the matter is laid in each of his articles! After all, at times the entire revolutionary movement hung on such a thin hair that it was time to fall into despair and run anywhere, fearing the reaction. He did not flinch once, and there are no traces of retreat or repentance found to this day by either "impartial" historians or rabid enemies. Only forward — to the victorious heights of scientific thinking. It would seem that Russia has a full set of conditions and resources (both human and natural) to be the most self-sufficient and harmoniously developing state on the planet. And the understanding of the direction, trend, perspective of the movement-development of the people and the country (where and why are we going?) has been lost since the times of the last most integral dialectician, which was V.I. Lenin. The idea (in the most general form — ideology, and not just ideology, but the most advanced ideology, which Lenin was guided by) as the most perfect derivative of human consciousness and is the guiding star, which is

the most content-capacious of all reliably known to us forms of our (human) level of development of reality, — why the most contentful flows of the level of human consciousness strive to possess this form (to clothe themselves in this form). So what, it would seem, is simpler — take and learn dialectics from Lenin (master, so to speak, the dialectical style of thinking and advanced ideas, which are an inevitable product of this kind of thinking) instead of subjecting him to ostracism. But no: to grow up to the perception of his ideas, it turns out, is much more difficult than to revile, showing off one's not so perfect brains and very limited abilities. Marxism is supposedly too orthodox, and Lenin's ideas have led to a dead end and have lost all relevance — these are the arguments of today's academic community (primarily among economists, politicians, historians, political scientists, etc.). Enlightened people, however, say that one should learn to follow not the letter of Marxism, not the letter of Lenin's manuscripts, but their spirit. The spirit (contentful principle) of dialectics is eternal: it only passes from form to form, i.e. it simply changes the form of its acquiring. And to learn to grasp, to perceive this spirit — this is the purpose and main goal of the life and activity of every educated person. The approach described here is intended to direct a person not just to the knowledge of some world that has existed since time immemorial, but predisposes to a meaningful study of everything that "is being done", that "is being reproduced" and "is being recreated" right here and now — in real-time mode both in our near and far environment. Another appeal to Lenin's words that even 70 Marxs will not be able to cover the "sum of all these changes in all their branches" and cope with solving such a task, contributes to the realization of the circumstance that any, albeit the most adequate at the moment, solution models the behavior of a very limited, "trimmed", "frozen" system, while reality is constantly changing and slips out from under any rigid and comprehensive control. That is why one must learn to perceive and grasp not the volumes of all kinds of changes of real forms, but the trend of these changes, which is ultimately the key and condition for the successful cognition of developing reality and the comprehension of objective truth. With an eye to what has been said, it becomes obvious how one should relate to the question of the cognizability of the world. Since the world, the Universe are incessantly and unceasingly "being made", "being recreated by piling up" (according to the formulation of the principle of self-development), and do not represent a kind of suitcase with books, then the cognition of the being made, every moment reproduced world — this is all the more an infinite and infinitely fascinating contentful process. How can one talk about some kind of comprehensive, complete and final knowledge of the world if the world is incessantly "unfolding" with more and more contentful forms? In this section, it is more reasonable to talk about the cognizability and cognition of the world as about the improvement of capabilities for

anticipating such an "unfolding" of forms, capabilities for guessing the emergence of new ones and the trend of changing existing forms, i.e. for forecasting future events and their consequences, speaking in the usual everyday language. Effective forecasting of the development of the forms of reality — this is the perspective of the contentful development of mankind. In times when, due to the infinitely routine everyday cyclicity, a person sees no meaning in life and in his existence, an opportunity opens up to look at all this with completely different eyes. Every person is not just capable, but is obliged to become a researcher. In each of us, a subconscious striving is dormant to manage to look around the corner, beyond the horizon (satisfying one's innate curiosity), to anticipate the onset of events, to forecast and anticipate the emergence and development of new forms. It is only necessary to activate this dormant natural striving and give it a new impulse, i.e. consciously bring it to a new level. There is not a single argument refuting or at least questioning the validity (universality and generality) of the dialectical concept of the development of reality — undoubtedly, it must be mastered and learn to correctly and effectively apply it. In conclusion, I would like to appeal to the youth again: read Lenin, guys, enjoy the beauty of thought, clarity and consistency of the presentation of the material, as well as the magnificent Russian language. This is our largest national thinker, a born dialectician, who will give odds to any modern philosophizing professional. His works contain developments that will be of interest to society and the state for decades to come. This is not the priests' scholastic tricks, sucked out of the finger and postulating the Absolute Form — with whatever spirituality, "spirituality", perfect organization and Absolute Content (in words) this Form is identified with. Through the mouth of Vladimir Ilyich, life itself speaks to us. In his reflections you will find answers for yourself or at least prerequisites for finding answers to the main worldview questions, you will feel out the right path to understanding the patterns of the development of reality (both of nature and of human communities). One should not think that everything has already been passed, laid out and summed up. Everything is just beginning — with each new turn of the development of reality, the struggle for the highest contentfulness acquires new and more diverse forms: "And again the battle continues, and the heart is anxious in the chest, and Lenin is so young, and a NEW October is ahead...".
